# libpetri requirements: natural language next to Lean

Generated by `#write_requirements_report` from the `requirement` blocks and `@[evidence]` attributes under `lean/Libpetri/`. Do not edit; CI regenerates it and fails on a difference.

## How to read this report

Each requirement block shows the spec text (quoted verbatim from `spec/*.md`, or proposed), a plain reading of the formula line by line, and the Lean statement. A requirement is a predicate, never an axiom: it is checked against a firing semantics, not assumed.

**Names in the Lean statements**

| Name | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `N` | the type of place names; a statement holds for every `N` |
| `F` | the firing semantics under test (`F : FireRel N`); `F t m d m'` says that transition `t` fires from marking `m`, deposits `d` and leaves marking `m'` |
| `RefFire` | the reference firing semantics (`Reference.Fires`), the registered witness model |
| `Trans N`, `Reference.Trans N` | transitions over places named by `N` |
| `m`, `m'` | markings: token counts by place (`N → Nat`) |
| `t`, `p` | a transition, a place |
| `fun x ↦ e` | the function that maps `x` to `e` |

**EARS keywords** (Easy Approach to Requirements Syntax; defined in `Libpetri/Spec/Ears.lean`)

| Notation | Reads as |
|---|---|
| `while P, never R` | no `R` step starts in a state where `P` holds |
| `on R, shall Q` | every `R` step from `s` to `s'` satisfies `Q s s'` |
| `when T, shall R` | in a trace, every `T` is followed (at it or later) by an `R` |
| `after T, never B` | in a trace, once a `T` has happened no later event is a `B` |
| `until G, never B` | in a trace, no `B` happens before the first `G` |
| `at-most-once P` | in a trace, at most one event is a `P` |
| `occurs P` | in a trace, some event is a `P` |

**Evidence roles** (each is a theorem; its shape is checked when it is tagged)

| Role | The theorem shows |
|---|---|
| ✓ holds | the requirement holds of the witness model (`RefFire`), at every type `N` |
| ✓ nonvacuous | the requirement's premises can be met: the witness model has a case it applies to |
| ✓ allows | the requirement permits a behaviour |
| ✓ total | some outcome exists in every case the requirement covers |
| ✓ consistent | the named requirements can all hold together |
| ✗ forbids | a concrete firing semantics breaks the requirement (`¬ REQ x`) |
| ✗ violated | the requirement is broken by the model named in the theorem |
| ✗ mutant | a plausible wrong reading `M` is told apart from the requirement: `M x ∧ ¬ REQ x` |
| ✗ conflict | the named requirements cannot all hold together: `¬ ∃ x, R₁ x ∧ … ∧ Rₙ x` |
| ? ambiguity | two readings of the text disagree on one model: `A x ∧ ¬ B x` |
| ? undecided | an open question the spec does not settle |

**Kind**: B behaviour, F pure function, T timing, Q quality (tested, not proven), A assumption, N configuration. **Source**: `verbatim` (the quote is in `spec/*.md` under the ID's heading) or `proposed` (new text, not yet in the spec). **Sign-off**: `pending`, or the date and statement hash a reviewer signed; the build fails when the hash no longer matches the block.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CONC_026"></a>
## CONC-026: Optional output validation skip (option enabled)

`Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026` · Kind **N** · Scope an executor that offers the option, with the option enabled · Source **verbatim** (spec/05-concurrency.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `03cde977bb7d7835`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* the option is enabled, the executor *shall* deposit the tokens an action wrote without validating them against the transition's output spec.<br><br>The skip covers the whole spec, its `Place` and `ForwardInput` leaves included: a transition whose output spec is a single leaf is not checked for a write to that place either.<br><br>While the option is enabled they do not apply: a firing that ends with its action deposits the action's write whether or not it equals an output branch.<br><br>The option skips nothing else.<br><br>A write to a place the transition does not declare as an output still fails ([CORE-063]).<br><br>The build-time checks of [IO-011], [IO-012], [IO-013] and [IO-014] run when the transition is built, before any executor exists, and no executor option reaches them.<br><br>When enabled, output tokens are deposited without validation. | 1. For every transition t that builds, whatever its output spec (an And, an Xor, or a single Place or ForwardInput leaf), every marking m at which t is enabled and every deposit d that writes only output places of t:<br>2. some firing of t from m deposits d.<br>3. Every firing of every transition deposits only in output places of the transition.<br>4. A transition that does not build never fires. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>      Builds t →<br>        ∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>          Enabled t m →<br>            ∀ (d : List N),<br>              (∀ (p : N), Produced d p → OutputArc t p) → ∃ m', F t m d m') ∧<br>    (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N),<br>        on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦<br>          ∀ (p : N), Produced d p → OutputArc t p) ∧<br>      ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>        ¬Builds t → ∀ (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ), ¬F t m d m'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is the firing relation F of one executor that has the option and has it enabled, each firing read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. The action may write any tokens into the transition's output places, so 'deposit the tokens an action wrote without validating them' is read as: every such write is the deposit of some firing (the first two lines). Deposits are compared per place (CORE-036), so several tokens in one place are one more such write.
- 'The skip covers the whole spec, its Place and ForwardInput leaves included' is why the first line ranges over every output spec: a transition whose output spec is Place(P) also fires with a write that misses P (CONC_026_forbids_leafValidated).
- 'It also covers the join-relay check of [NU-054] and the multiplicity warning of [IO-016] AC4, both of which run as part of validation.': a correlation name and a diagnostic are below the token-count granularity of the firing semantics; not encoded.
- The paragraph naming IO-010, IO-011, IO-012, the per-firing check of IO-015 and the last two firing lines of CORE-036 is a scope: those blocks describe an executor with output validation enabled (their scope strings say so), and this block describes the executor with the option enabled. Theorem CONC_026_scoped shows both hold at once, each of its own executor, and that every firing of the validating executor on a transition that builds is a firing of the skipping one.
- 'The option skips nothing else' and 'A write to a place the transition does not declare as an output still fails ([CORE-063])' are the third line: a write outside the output places is not the deposit of any firing.
- 'A firing that ends with the timeout outcome deposits what [IO-013] and [IO-014] say.' is those blocks' subject, over the relation of timed-out firings; it is not restated here. A timeout deposit writes only output places, so the first line covers it.
- 'The build-time checks ... no executor option reaches them' is the fourth line: a transition that does not build (glossary: Builds) is not a transition any executor holds, so it never fires, the option enabled or not.
- The first sentence's MAY (whether an executor offers the option at all), 'for known-correct nets' and the performance sentence are not encoded: the block describes an executor that offers the option, with it enabled. The implementation notes are not normative.
- AC1 (the option is opt-in, validation is on by default) is about the builder's default, not about the firing relation; not encoded.
- AC2 (when enabled, output tokens are deposited without validation) is the first two lines.
- AC3 (when disabled, validation behaves identically to the bitmap executor) is the plain firing relation's subject (IO-010 to IO-015); not encoded here.
- AC4 (Place(P) writing nothing, And(A, B) writing only A, both deposited) is the first two lines; CONC_026_nonvacuous fires both under the witness.
- AC5 (no NU-054 diagnostic, no IO-016 AC4 warning) is below the token-count granularity; not encoded.
- AC6 (a write to an undeclared place still fails, and building a transition that IO-012 rejects still fails) is the third and fourth lines.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_skip` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✓ CONC-026 holds of the skip-validation firings (`refFireSkipValidation`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CONC_026 refFireSkipValidation</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_forbids_leafValidated` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✗ An executor that still validates a single leaf violates CONC-026: the `Place(0)` transition never completes with the empty write, which the skip deposits ("its `Place` and `ForwardInput` leaves included", AC4).
  <pre>¬CONC_026 clashSkipAndXorFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_forbids_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✗ The reference semantics, which validates, is not an executor with the option enabled: the `And(Place(0), Place(1))` transition never deposits the partial write `[0]`.
  <pre>¬CONC_026 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_forbids_unbuilt` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✗ An executor that fires a transition that does not build violates CONC-026 (AC6): the `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` transition, which [IO-012] rejects, fires.
  <pre>¬CONC_026 clashUnbuiltFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_forbids_undeclared` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✗ An executor that deposits a write to an undeclared place violates CONC-026 (AC6): the `And(Place(0), Place(1))` transition deposits `[2]`.
  <pre>¬CONC_026 clashUndeclaredFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_mutant_dropBuild_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): The executor that fires unbuilt transitions kills the third mutant.
  <pre>CONC_026_mutant_dropBuild clashUnbuiltFire ∧ ¬CONC_026 clashUnbuiltFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_mutant_dropDeposits_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): The reference firings of transitions that build kill the first mutant: they deposit only output places and never fire a transition that does not build, but they validate.
  <pre>(CONC_026_mutant_dropDeposits fun t m d m' ↦ Builds t ∧ RefFire t m d m') ∧<br>  ¬CONC_026 fun t m d m' ↦ Builds t ∧ RefFire t m d m'</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_mutant_dropUndeclared_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): The undeclared-write executor kills the second mutant.
  <pre>CONC_026_mutant_dropUndeclared clashUndeclaredFire ∧ ¬CONC_026 clashUndeclaredFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): CONC-026 is exercised (AC4): under the skip-validation firings the `And(Place(0), Place(1))` transition fires from the empty marking with the partial write `[0]`, and the `Place(0)` transition with the empty write; neither write is an output branch.
  <pre>(∃ m', refFireSkipValidation (clashT clashAnd01) clashEmpty [0] m') ∧<br>  ¬OutputBranch (clashT clashAnd01) [0] ∧<br>    (∃ m', refFireSkipValidation (clashT (Out.place 0)) clashEmpty [] m') ∧<br>      ¬OutputBranch (clashT (Out.place 0)) []</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.CONC_026_scoped` (Libpetri.Spec.Clashes): ✓ The validating blocks and the skip hold together, each of its own executor: IO-010, IO-011 and IO-012 hold of the reference firings (output validation enabled), CONC-026 of the skip-validation firings, and every reference firing of a transition that builds is a skip-validation firing, so enabling the option only adds firings.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  IO_010 RefFire ∧<br>    IO_011 refBuild RefFire ∧<br>      IO_012 IO_012_check refBuild RefFire ∧<br>        CONC_026 refFireSkipValidation ∧<br>          ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            Builds t → RefFire t m d m' → refFireSkipValidation t m d m'</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_022"></a>
## CORE-022: Transition enablement

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `85f3b6c51be85f72`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| A transition is **enabled** in a marking when every input place holds at least its required count (see [IO-001]–[IO-004]), every read place is marked and no inhibitor place is marked.<br><br>*While* an input place of a transition holds fewer tokens than its required count, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a read place of a transition is unmarked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* an inhibitor place of a transition is marked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a transition is enabled, the transition *shall* be able to fire.<br><br>This requirement concerns transitions that build. A definition that building the transition rejects ([CORE-030], [IO-002], [IO-004], [EXEC-012], [IO-011]–[IO-014]) is not a transition of any net and is never enabled anywhere; in particular an output spec whose alternatives no write can tell apart ([IO-012]) never reaches a marking. | 1. For every transition t that builds (Builds t: at most one input arc per place, every input arc requiring at least one token, no place with both an input arc and a read arc, no place twice in one output branch, at most one Timeout node, no Xor node inside the Timeout child, no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child, every forward's from an input place, no two output branches claiming the same set of places):<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which the place of some input arc of t holds fewer tokens than that arc's required count;<br>3. t does not fire from a marking in which some read place of t is unmarked;<br>4. t does not fire from a marking in which some inhibitor place of t is marked;<br>5. from every marking m in which t is enabled, t fires to some marking m'. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>    Builds t →<br>      (while fun m ↦ ∃ s ∈ t.core.inputs, m s.place &lt; requiredCount s.card, never<br>          Fire F t) ∧<br>        (while fun m ↦ ∃ p, ReadArc t p ∧ ¬Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>          (while fun m ↦ ∃ p, InhibitorArc t p ∧ Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>            ∀ (m : CountMarking N), Enabled t m → ∃ m', Fire F t m m'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Enabled' is the glossary term (`Enabled`, readable form `enabled_iff`); 'can fire' / 'able to fire' is read as 'some firing exists'. The last line says a firing exists, not that an executor performs it now: when an enabled transition actually fires is the subject of the timing and scheduling requirements (TIME-*, EXEC-*).
- Enablement is a function of the current marking alone: the three 'shall not' lines forbid a firing from every marking in which a condition fails, whatever happened before. This is how 'when any condition ceases to hold, the transition becomes disabled' of the previous wording is read.
- The input condition ranges over every input arc of t, not only the first one per place. Under CORE-030 a transition has at most one input arc per place, so the two readings coincide on every transition that can be built.
- The 'able to fire' line needs every transition to have at least one way to end (an output branch, or the empty deposit when it has no output spec); the reference semantics has one for every output spec (`Reference.exists_deposit`).
- Transitions with a ν match specification (NU-020) add a correlation condition on top of these three; this block reads CORE-022 for transitions without one, as the reference semantics has none.
- The inhibitor line restates the first clause of CORE-031 because CORE-022 lists it as one of its conditions; it is kept so that this block stands on its own.
- 'Transitions that build' is read as the build-time checks themselves (`Builds`): the input-arc check of CORE-030, the input-count checks of IO-002 and IO-004 (no Exactly(0), no AtLeast(0)), the input-and-read check of EXEC-012 (ruling D15) and the output-spec checks of IO-011 (a place twice in one branch), IO-013 (more than one Timeout node), IO-014 (a ForwardInput outside a Timeout child; a forward from a non-input place) and IO-012 (two output branches claiming the same set of places). A transition that fails one constrains nothing here: no executor ever holds it, so it neither fires nor is required to. Branch sets are compared as sets (`SameSet`), so Xor(And(A, B, C), And(A, B)) and Xor(And(P, A), And(P, B)) build and are covered, while Xor(P, P) and Xor(And(A, B), And(B, A)) are not.
- The reference `Out` is binary; n-ary And / Xor are read as right-nested, which keeps the IO-016 enumeration order, so the IO-012 branch indices and the build verdict are those of the n-ary spec.
- AC1 (satisfied inputs, reads present, inhibitors empty, enabled) is the last line.
- AC2 (missing input tokens, disabled) is the second line.
- AC3 (missing read tokens, disabled) is the third line.
- AC4 (inhibitor place has tokens, disabled) is the fourth line.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_builtOnly` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✓ CORE-022 holds of the reference restricted to transitions that build (`CORE_022_refFireBuilt`): the "shall not" lines are the reference's, and a transition that builds keeps every reference firing. This is the relation that also satisfies IO-012, whose `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` transition (`core022_dup`) does not build.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_022 CORE_022_refFireBuilt</pre>
- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-022: a reference firing starts with `Seam.enabledNA`, whose readable form (`enabled_iff`) is the three conditions, so a failed condition rules out every firing; and an enabled transition always has a deposit to end with (`Reference.exists_deposit`), so it fires. The reference fires every transition, those that do not build included, so the scoping to transitions that build is not needed here.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_022 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_forbids_ignoresInhibitors` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✗ Ignoring inhibitor arcs violates CORE-022 (fourth line): `core022_t` fires from `core022_inhibited`, whose inhibitor place is marked.
  <pre>¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresInhibitors</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_forbids_ignoresInputs` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✗ Ignoring input arcs violates CORE-022 (second line): `core022_t` fires from `core022_noInput`, whose input place is empty.
  <pre>¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresInputs</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_forbids_ignoresReads` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✗ Ignoring read arcs violates CORE-022 (third line): `core022_t` fires from `core022_noRead`, whose read place is empty.
  <pre>¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresReads</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_forbids_neverFire` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✗ A firing relation that never fires violates CORE-022 (last line): `core022_t` is enabled at `core022_enabled` but does not fire.
  <pre>¬CORE_022 core022_neverFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_forbids_strictBuild` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): ✗ Rejecting a spec the build accepts violates CORE-022 (last line, scoping): `core022_overlap` builds and is enabled at the empty marking, but an executor that refuses overlapping alternatives never fires it.
  <pre>¬CORE_022 core022_refFireStrict</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_mutant_dropFires_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): The firing relation that never fires kills the fourth mutant: it keeps every "shall not" line, since it forbids every firing, but an enabled transition does not fire.
  <pre>CORE_022_mutant_dropFires core022_neverFire ∧ ¬CORE_022 core022_neverFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_mutant_dropInhibitors_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): Ignoring inhibitor arcs kills the third mutant: it keeps the input, read and "able to fire" lines, but not the inhibitor line.
  <pre>CORE_022_mutant_dropInhibitors core022_ignoresInhibitors ∧<br>  ¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresInhibitors</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_mutant_dropInputs_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): Ignoring input arcs kills the first mutant: it keeps the read, inhibitor and "able to fire" lines, but not the input line.
  <pre>CORE_022_mutant_dropInputs core022_ignoresInputs ∧ ¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresInputs</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_mutant_dropReads_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): Ignoring read arcs kills the second mutant: it keeps the input, inhibitor and "able to fire" lines, but not the read line.
  <pre>CORE_022_mutant_dropReads core022_ignoresReads ∧ ¬CORE_022 core022_ignoresReads</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_mutant_strictBuild_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): An executor that also refuses overlapping alternatives kills the scoping mutant: it satisfies every line for the transitions that pass the stricter check, but `core022_overlap` builds and never fires.
  <pre>CORE_022_mutant_strictBuild core022_refFireStrict ∧ ¬CORE_022 core022_refFireStrict</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): Every line of CORE-022 is exercised by the reference semantics, toggling one condition at a time as the test derivation says: `core022_t` builds, fires from `core022_enabled` (last line), and does not fire with its input place emptied (second line), its read place emptied (third line) or its inhibitor place marked (fourth line); each of those markings fails exactly its own condition.
  <pre>Builds core022_t ∧<br>  Enabled core022_t core022_enabled ∧<br>    (∃ m', Fire RefFire core022_t core022_enabled m') ∧<br>      (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire RefFire core022_t core022_noInput m') ∧<br>        (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire RefFire core022_t core022_noRead m') ∧<br>          (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire RefFire core022_t core022_inhibited m') ∧<br>            (∃ s ∈ core022_t.core.inputs,<br>                core022_noInput s.place &lt; requiredCount s.card) ∧<br>              ¬Marked core022_noRead 1 ∧ Marked core022_inhibited 2</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_022_nonvacuous_scope` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_022): The scoping paragraph separates its two sides on the reference: `Xor(Place(0), Place(0))` (`core022_dup`) is enabled at the empty marking and has a reference firing, yet does not build, so CORE-022 does not reach it and the built-only executor never fires it; the overlapping `Xor(And(0, 1, 2), And(0, 1))` (`core022_overlap`) builds, is enabled at the empty marking and fires in both.
  <pre>¬Builds core022_dup ∧<br>  Enabled core022_dup core022_empty ∧<br>    (∃ m', Fire RefFire core022_dup core022_empty m') ∧<br>      (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire CORE_022_refFireBuilt core022_dup core022_empty m') ∧<br>        Builds core022_overlap ∧<br>          Enabled core022_overlap core022_empty ∧<br>            ∃ m', Fire CORE_022_refFireBuilt core022_overlap core022_empty m'</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_030"></a>
## CORE-030: Input arc

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `291c542b00432099`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *When* a transition fires, it *shall* consume from each of its input places that is not also one of its reset places ([CORE-034]) as many tokens as the input arc's consumption count ([IO-001]–[IO-004], [IO-007]), oldest first.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, each of its input places that is neither one of its reset places nor one of its output places *shall* end the firing holding exactly the consumption count of its input arc fewer tokens than it held before the firing. | 1. For every transition t that declares at most one input arc per place, every input place p of t whose input arc has cardinality c, p not a reset place of t, and every deposit d, every firing of t from m to m' depositing d, from a marking in which p holds at least the arc's required count:<br>2. leaves m'(p) + consumptionCount(c, m(p)) = m(p) + (the tokens d deposits in p): the firing removes exactly the consumption count from p, besides what it deposits there;<br>3. and, if p is not an output place of t either, leaves m'(p) + consumptionCount(c, m(p)) = m(p): p ends holding exactly the consumption count fewer tokens, whatever deposit the firing reports. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N) (c : Card),<br>    InputPlacesDistinct t →<br>      InputArc t p c →<br>        ¬ResetArc t p →<br>          ∀ (d : List N),<br>            on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>              requiredCount c ≤ TokenCount m p →<br>                TokenCount m' p + consumptionCount c (TokenCount m p) =<br>                    TokenCount m p + Deposits d p ∧<br>                  (¬OutputArc t p →<br>                    TokenCount m' p + consumptionCount c (TokenCount m p) =<br>                      TokenCount m p)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking with its deposit (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. Markings are token counts (glossary: Marking).
- AC1 (a transition with an input arc consumes tokens from the place on firing) is the second line, with the amount the consumption count of the arc's cardinality; the third line is the second quoted sentence.
- AC2 (consumed tokens follow FIFO order) and 'oldest first' are not encoded: which tokens leave a place is below the token-count granularity of the firing relation.
- AC3 and the sentence 'If a transition declares two input arcs on the same place, building the transition shall fail with a descriptive error that names the transition and the place and suggests a single arc with `exactly(n)` / `atLeast(n)` instead.' are not encoded: the firing model has no transition builder and no error values. They are read as a hypothesis instead: the requirement constrains only transitions whose input places are distinct (InputPlacesDistinct), the only ones AC3 lets any executor, state-class graph or verifier see. Firings of a transition with duplicate input arcs are left unconstrained here, as the spec says their consumption has no coherent semantics.
- The build-time output-spec checks (IO-011 to IO-014) are not a hypothesis here: neither line depends on the output spec beyond which places it names, so both hold of every transition with distinct input places, whether or not its output spec builds.
- A place that is both an input place and a reset place of t is excluded by both quoted sentences: CORE-034 says such a place ends the firing holding exactly what was deposited there, so it is CORE-034's to constrain (the third hypothesis, ¬ ResetArc t p).
- Interpretation: the consumption is read additively, m'(p) + consumed = m(p) + deposited, so a firing that also deposits into its own input place is counted correctly.
- The deposit a firing reports is not trusted for the count on a place the output spec does not name (glossary: Output arc): no firing of t can deposit there ([CORE-035]), so the consumption shows in the marking alone, as the third line says. Without it a firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit equal to the consumption count in its input place would pass the second line. This is the guard IO-001 to IO-004 also carry, so each of those blocks stands alone.
- Interpretation: both lines are required only of firings from a marking in which p holds at least the arc's required count. A firing from a marking where p holds fewer is not enabled (CORE-022, IO-005); whether such a firing may happen at all is those requirements' business, and CORE-030, which speaks only of how much a firing consumes, says nothing about it.
- 'The same place' is equality of place names (TypeScript, Rust, [CORE-002]); Java's name-and-type equality is not modelled.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-030: `Seam.fireNC` leaves a non-reset place with `m p - nconsumed + deposited`, `Seam.nconsumed` is the consumption count of the arc `nspecAt` finds, and enablement (`Seam.enabledNA`) guarantees the place holds at least the required count, so the subtraction does not truncate. A reference firing deposits only in places its output spec names, so outside them the deposit is empty and the place loses exactly the consumption count.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_030 RefFire</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_allows_unenabled` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): CORE-030 allows a firing of a transition that is not enabled, as long as every enabled firing consumes the consumption count: the unguarded firing relation satisfies CORE-030, and `core030_t .one` firing from an empty place `0` tells it apart from the unguarded mutant, which would forbid that firing although CORE-030 says nothing about it.
  <pre>CORE_030 core030_unguardedFire ∧ ¬CORE_030_mutant_unguarded core030_unguardedFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_forbids_ghost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): ✗ A firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit in its input place violates CORE-030 (third line): `core030_t .one` has no output place, fires from one token in place `0`, reports a deposit of one token there, and leaves that token in place `0`.
  <pre>¬CORE_030 core030_ghostFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_forbids_idle` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): ✗ A firing that consumes nothing through its input arcs violates CORE-030 (second line): `core030_t .one` fires from one token in place `0` and leaves that token there.
  <pre>¬CORE_030 core030_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_mutant_noConsume_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): The idle firing kills the no-consumption mutant: it satisfies it (nothing changes, nothing is deposited) but not CORE-030.
  <pre>CORE_030_mutant_noConsume core030_idleFire ∧ ¬CORE_030 core030_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_mutant_required_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): The required-count firing kills the second mutant: it satisfies it, but `core030_t .all` fires from two tokens in place `0` and leaves one, where CORE-030 says `All` consumes both.
  <pre>CORE_030_mutant_required core030_requiredFire ∧ ¬CORE_030 core030_requiredFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_mutant_trustDeposit_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): The ghost-deposit firing kills the trust-deposit mutant: the deposit it reports in each input place makes up for the tokens it does not take, so it satisfies the mutant; but `core030_t .one` has no output place, and its input place keeps its token.
  <pre>CORE_030_mutant_trustDeposit core030_ghostFire ∧ ¬CORE_030 core030_ghostFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_030_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_030): Both lines of CORE-030 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core030_t .all` has a single input arc, of cardinality `All`, on place `0`, which is neither a reset place nor an output place, and it fires from two tokens in place `0` to none, consuming both.
  <pre>InputPlacesDistinct (core030_t Card.all) ∧<br>  InputArc (core030_t Card.all) 0 Card.all ∧<br>    ¬ResetArc (core030_t Card.all) 0 ∧<br>      ¬OutputArc (core030_t Card.all) 0 ∧<br>        RefFire (core030_t Card.all) (core030_at0 2) [] (core030_at0 0)</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

## CORE-031: Inhibitor arc

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `4f884470bf67c07e`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* an inhibitor place of a transition is marked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* an inhibitor place of a transition is unmarked, the inhibitor arc *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire. | 1. For every transition t and every inhibitor place p of t:<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p is marked;<br>3. from a marking m in which p is unmarked, t fires to m' exactly when t without its inhibitor arcs on p fires from m to m'. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    InhibitorArc t p →<br>      (while fun m ↦ Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        ∀ (m : CountMarking N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>          ¬Marked m p → (Fire F t m m' ↔ Fire F (withoutInhibitor t p) m m')</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' and 'enabled' are both read as 'some firing exists'.
- AC1 (inhibitor place empty, enablement unaffected) is the second line: with p unmarked, removing the inhibitor arcs on p changes neither whether t fires nor where it leads.
- AC2 (inhibitor place marked, transition disabled) is the first line.
- AC3, first half (a firing consumes nothing through its inhibitor arcs): by the second line a firing of t from a marking with p unmarked is a firing of t without its arcs on p, so those arcs add nothing to what it consumes or produces; by the first line t does not fire when p is marked. AC3, second half: what a firing of another transition consumes through an input arc on p is CORE-030's subject, not this block's.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-031: `Seam.enabledNA` requires every inhibitor place to be empty, so `t` does not fire while `p` is marked; and the firing effect (`Seam.nconsumed`, `Seam.fireNC`) and the deposits read inputs, resets and the output spec only, so with `p` unmarked removing the inhibitor arcs on `p` changes nothing.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_031 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_forbids_ignoresInhibitor` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): ✗ Ignoring the inhibitor arc violates CORE-031 (first clause): `core031_t` fires while place `0` is marked.
  <pre>¬CORE_031 core031_ignoresInhibitorFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_forbids_inhibitorAlwaysBlocks` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): ✗ An inhibitor arc that blocks its transition while its place is empty violates CORE-031 (second clause): `core031_t` cannot fire from the empty marking, but without its inhibitor arc it can.
  <pre>¬CORE_031 core031_inhibitorAlwaysBlocksFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_mutant_dropMarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): The idle firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (whether a transition fires does not depend on its arcs at all) but not CORE-031 (`core031_t` fires while its inhibitor place is marked).
  <pre>CORE_031_mutant_dropMarked core031_idleFire ∧ ¬CORE_031 core031_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_mutant_dropUnmarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): The always-blocking firing kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant (a transition with an inhibitor arc never fires) but not CORE-031 (`core031_t` does not fire from the empty marking although it fires there without its inhibitor arc).
  <pre>CORE_031_mutant_dropUnmarked core031_inhibitorAlwaysBlocksFire ∧<br>  ¬CORE_031 core031_inhibitorAlwaysBlocksFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_031_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Core): Both clauses of CORE-031 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core031_t` has an inhibitor arc; it fires from the empty marking, as it does without its inhibitor arc (second clause, with the place unmarked); and it does not fire from the marking in which its inhibitor place is marked, although without its inhibitor arc it does (first clause).
  <pre>InhibitorArc core031_t 0 ∧<br>  Fire RefFire core031_t core031_empty core031_empty ∧<br>    Fire RefFire (withoutInhibitor core031_t 0) core031_empty core031_empty ∧<br>      ¬Fire RefFire core031_t core031_marked core031_marked ∧<br>        Fire RefFire (withoutInhibitor core031_t 0) core031_marked core031_marked</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_032"></a>
## CORE-032: Read Arc

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `114ece4e82239077`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a read place of a transition is unmarked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a read place of a transition is marked, the read arc *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire or the marking its firing leaves.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, each of its read places on which it has neither an input arc nor a reset arc *shall* end the firing with its token count before the firing plus the tokens the firing deposits there. | 1. For every transition t and every read place p of t:<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p is unmarked;<br>3. from a marking m in which p is marked, t fires to m' exactly when t without its read arcs on p fires from m to m';<br>4. if t has no input arc and no reset arc on p, every firing of t from m that deposits d and leaves m' has m'(p) = m(p) + (tokens d puts in p). | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    ReadArc t p →<br>      (while fun m ↦ ¬Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (∀ (m : CountMarking N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            Marked m p → (Fire F t m m' ↔ Fire F (withoutRead t p) m m')) ∧<br>          ((∀ (c : Card), ¬InputArc t p c) →<br>            ¬ResetArc t p →<br>              ∀ (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>                F t m d m' → m' p = m p + Deposits d p)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' and 'enabled' are both read as 'some firing exists'.
- AC1 (read place must have tokens for enablement) is the second line.
- AC2 (token remains in place after firing) is the fourth line, for a read place the transition neither consumes from nor resets. A place that is also an input place or a reset place of the same transition loses tokens through those arcs ([CORE-030], [CORE-034], [EXEC-013] AC4); the third line says the read arc adds nothing to that loss.
- AC3 (action receives the read token value) is not encoded: the firing relation works on token counts and has no action or token values. Which token is read is [EXEC-012]'s subject, the context access [CORE-061]'s.
- 'Marked' is read as 'holds at least one token' (glossary: Marked), the spec's 'at least one token for enablement'.
- AC2's 'token remains' is encoded at count level only. A firing that takes the read token out of p and puts it, or another token, back in the same step leaves the same counts and satisfies this statement, but moves the token to the FIFO tail and changes its timestamp. Token identity, FIFO position and timestamp are below the count granularity of the firing relation, as for CORE-030 AC2; they belong to [EXEC-012] ('both A and B remain after firing', the oldest is peeked), and no count-level block can catch the bug.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-032: `Seam.enabledNA` requires every read place to hold a token, so `t` does not fire while `p` is unmarked; the consumption (`Seam.nconsumed`), the deposits and the effect (`Seam.fireNC`) never read the read arcs, so with `p` marked removing them changes nothing; and with no input arc and no reset arc on `p`, `Seam.fireNC` leaves `m p - 0 + Deposits d p` there.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_032 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_forbids_ignoresRead` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): ✗ Ignoring the read arc violates CORE-032 (first clause): `core032_t` fires from the empty marking.
  <pre>¬CORE_032 core032_ignoresReadFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_forbids_readAlwaysBlocks` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): ✗ A read arc that blocks its transition while its place is marked violates CORE-032 (second clause): `core032_t` cannot fire from the marked marking, but without its read arc it can.
  <pre>¬CORE_032 core032_readAlwaysBlocksFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_forbids_readConsumes` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): ✗ Consuming the read token violates CORE-032 (third clause): `core032_t` fires from the marking with one token in every place and leaves place `0` empty, depositing nothing.
  <pre>¬CORE_032 core032_readConsumesFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_forbids_skipReadReset` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): ✗ Skipping the reset of a read place violates CORE-032 (second clause): with one token in place `0`, `core032_tRes` without its read arc fires and drains place `0`, but no firing of `core032_tRes` leaves place `0` empty.
  <pre>¬CORE_032 core032_skipReadResetFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_mutant_dropConserve_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): The clearing firing kills the third mutant: it satisfies the mutant (it fires exactly when the reference does, and the reference satisfies the first two clauses) but not CORE-032 (`core032_t` fires from the marked marking and leaves place `0` empty, depositing nothing).
  <pre>CORE_032_mutant_dropConserve core032_clearsAllFire ∧ ¬CORE_032 core032_clearsAllFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_mutant_dropMarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): The always-blocking firing kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant (a transition with a read arc never fires) but not CORE-032 (`core032_t` does not fire from the marked marking although it fires there without its read arc).
  <pre>CORE_032_mutant_dropMarked core032_readAlwaysBlocksFire ∧<br>  ¬CORE_032 core032_readAlwaysBlocksFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_mutant_dropUnmarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): The idle firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (whether a transition fires does not depend on its arcs, and no firing changes a token count) but not CORE-032 (`core032_t` fires while its read place is unmarked).
  <pre>CORE_032_mutant_dropUnmarked core032_idleFire ∧ ¬CORE_032 core032_idleFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_032_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_032): All three clauses of CORE-032 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core032_t` has a read arc on place `0` and no input or reset arc there; it does not fire from the empty marking, although without its read arc it does (first clause); it fires from the marked marking, as it does without its read arc (second clause); and that firing deposits nothing and leaves place `0` holding its one token (third clause).
  <pre>ReadArc core032_t 0 ∧<br>  (∀ (c : Card), ¬InputArc core032_t 0 c) ∧<br>    ¬ResetArc core032_t 0 ∧<br>      ¬Fire RefFire core032_t core032_empty core032_empty ∧<br>        Fire RefFire (withoutRead core032_t 0) core032_empty core032_empty ∧<br>          RefFire core032_t core032_marked [] core032_marked ∧<br>            Fire RefFire (withoutRead core032_t 0) core032_marked core032_marked ∧<br>              core032_marked 0 = core032_marked 0 + Deposits [] 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_033"></a>
## CORE-033: Read arc multi-reader

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `a864c7bea1b325a9`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a place is marked, a read arc to that place *shall not* stop its transition from firing, so all transitions with read arcs to the place can be enabled simultaneously.<br><br>*When* a transition with a read arc to a marked place fires, and the transition has no input arc and no reset arc to that place, the read arc of any transition to that place *shall not* stop that transition from firing afterwards (no conflict), since read arcs do not consume tokens. | 1. For every transition t and every read place p of t, at every marking m in which p is marked:<br>2. if t without its read arcs on p can fire from m, then t can fire from m;<br>3. for every two transitions t₁, t₂ with read arcs on p, where t₁ has no input arc and no reset arc on p:<br>4. after every firing of t₁ from a marking m in which p is marked to m', if t₂ without its read arcs on p can fire from m', then t₂ can fire from m'. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>      ReadArc t p →<br>        ∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>          Marked m p →<br>            (∃ m', Fire F (core033_withoutRead t p) m m') → ∃ m', Fire F t m m') ∧<br>    ∀ (t₁ t₂ : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>      ReadArc t₁ p →<br>        ReadArc t₂ p →<br>          (∀ (c : Card), ¬InputArc t₁ p c) →<br>            ¬ResetArc t₁ p →<br>              on Fire F t₁, shall fun m m' ↦<br>                Marked m p →<br>                  (∃ m'', Fire F (core033_withoutRead t₂ p) m' m'') →<br>                    ∃ m'', Fire F t₂ m' m''</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' and 'enabled' are both read as 'some firing exists', to any target marking.
- AC1 (two readers both enabled when the place has tokens) is the first two lines: whether a firing relation lets t fire from m depends on t and m alone, so 'both enabled' is each enabled, and each reader's read arc on a marked p adds no condition. The text does not claim a reader fires with its other arcs unsatisfied, hence the comparison with the reader without its read arcs on p.
- AC2 (both can fire without conflict) is read in the untimed interleaving: firing one reader does not take away, through p, the other reader's ability to fire (last two lines). Conflicts through other places (t₁ consuming an input of t₂, or depositing into an inhibitor place of t₂) are not this block's subject.
- The restriction to a t₁ with no input arc and no reset arc on p is an interpretation choice: such a t₁ removes tokens from p (CORE-030, CORE-034; EXEC-012 allows a read arc and a reset arc on one place), so it can disable the other reader; the text's reason 'read arcs do not consume tokens' covers the read arcs only.
- t₁ and t₂ may be the same transition: a reader firing twice in a row is covered too. In the untimed step model this is no stronger than the case of two transitions with the same arcs.
- The read token's value reaching the action (CORE-032 AC3, EXEC-012) is below the count granularity and not this block's subject.
- Both clauses assume p is marked: a reader firing from an empty read place is CORE-032 AC1 (its first clause), not this block's subject. A firing relation that ignores read arcs satisfies this block (CORE_033_allows_ignoresRead).
- Clause 2 asks only that p stay marked for the other reader, which is what 'no conflict' needs. That a read leaves the count of p unchanged (apart from deposits) is CORE-032 AC2 (its third clause); the 'since read arcs do not consume tokens' of this text is that requirement's reason, not a second acceptance criterion here. A read that takes one token whenever two or more are present satisfies this block (CORE_033_allows_readTakesSurplus).
- Each firing is one atomic step, so a read token held or reserved while the reader's action runs (taken at the start and put back at completion, or a per-place lock on read places), which would keep two readers from being in flight together, cannot be expressed here. In-flight concurrency of readers needs a start/complete step model (CONC, EXEC); a take-and-put read that reorders the FIFO queue is below count granularity (EXEC-012).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-033: a read arc on a marked place adds no condition (`core033_ref_fire_of_withoutRead`), and a firing of a reader with no input arc and no reset arc on `p` consumes nothing from `p` and does not reset it (`refFire_apply`), so `p` stays marked for the other reader.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_033 RefFire</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_allows_ignoresRead` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): ✓ CORE-033 allows a firing relation that ignores read arcs, under which `core033_t₁` fires from the empty marking: both clauses assume the read place is marked, so a reader firing from an empty read place is outside this block. CORE-032's first clause rejects it (CORE-032's `CORE_032_forbids_ignoresRead`, for the same relation).
  <pre>CORE_033 core033_ignoresReadFire ∧<br>  Fire core033_ignoresReadFire core033_t₁ core033_empty core033_empty</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_allows_readTakesSurplus` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): ✓ CORE-033 allows a read that takes surplus tokens: the read place never becomes empty, so no reader blocks another ('no conflict' holds), yet `core033_t₁` fires from `core033_two`, deposits nothing and leaves one token in place `0`. CORE-032's third clause (the count after a firing is the count before plus the deposits) rejects it.
  <pre>CORE_033 core033_readTakesSurplusFire ∧<br>  core033_readTakesSurplusFire core033_t₁ core033_two [] fun n ↦ if n = 0 then 1 else 2</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_forbids_readAlwaysBlocks` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): ✗ A read arc that blocks its transition while its place is marked violates CORE-033 (first clause): `core033_t₁` cannot fire from `core033_one`, but without its read arc it can.
  <pre>¬CORE_033 core033_readAlwaysBlocksFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_forbids_readConsumes` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): ✗ A read that consumes violates CORE-033 (second clause): `core033_t₁` fires from `core033_one` and drains place `0`, after which `core033_t₂` cannot fire although it can without its read arc.

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_mutant_dropEnabled_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): The always-blocking firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (a transition with a read arc never fires, so no reader's firing takes anything away) but not CORE-033 (`core033_t₁` does not fire from `core033_one`).

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_mutant_dropNoConflict_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): The consuming read kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant (a `One` input on a marked place adds no condition) but not CORE-033 (`core033_t₁` drains place `0` and blocks `core033_t₂`).

- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_033_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_033): Both clauses of CORE-033 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core033_t₁` and `core033_t₂` both read place `0`, which `core033_one` marks; `core033_t₁` without its read arc fires from `core033_one`, and so does `core033_t₁` (first clause); `core033_t₁` fires from `core033_one` back to `core033_one`, from which `core033_t₂` without its read arc and `core033_t₂` both fire (second clause).

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_034"></a>
## CORE-034: Reset arc

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `93e6155b06c813c7`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *When* a transition fires, each of its reset places *shall* lose every token it held before the firing and end the firing holding exactly the tokens the firing deposited there.<br><br>A reset arc *shall not* affect whether its transition can fire, whether the reset place is marked or not.<br><br>A reset arc *shall not* change what its transition can deposit: from every marking, the transition and the same transition without its reset arcs *shall* be able to make exactly the same deposits, so a reset arc neither adds a deposit nor removes one.<br><br>A reset arc *shall not* change the token count of any place other than its own. | 1. For every transition t and every reset place p of t, writing t∖p for t without its reset arcs on p:<br>2. every firing of t that deposits d leaves p with exactly as many tokens as d puts in p, whatever p held before;<br>3. every firing of t∖p from a marking m that deposits d is matched by a firing of t from m whose deposit puts the same number of tokens in every place (a reset arc removes no deposit, and t fires wherever t∖p does);<br>4. every firing of t from m that deposits d is matched by a firing of t∖p from m whose deposit puts the same number of tokens in every place and that leaves every place other than p with the same token count (a reset arc adds no deposit, t∖p fires wherever t does, and the arc changes no other place). | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    ResetArc t p →<br>      (∀ (d : List N),<br>          on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ TokenCount m' p = Deposits d p) ∧<br>        (∀ (d : List N),<br>            on fun m m'' ↦ F (withoutReset t p) m d m'', shall fun m x ↦<br>              ∃ d' m', F t m d' m' ∧ SamePerPlace d d') ∧<br>          ∀ (d : List N),<br>            on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>              ∃ d' m'',<br>                F (withoutReset t p) m d' m'' ∧<br>                  SamePerPlace d d' ∧<br>                    ∀ (q : N), q ≠ p → TokenCount m' q = TokenCount m'' q</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' is read as 'some firing exists', to any target marking: removing a reset arc changes where a firing leads, so the second sentence compares the existence of a firing, not its target. It is the existence half of the second and third lines taken together (`CORE_034_canFire`), so it has no line of its own.
- Deposits are compared per place (CORE-036): two deposits that put the same number of tokens in every place are the same deposit, whatever their order (`SamePerPlace`).
- AC1 (fires even if the reset place is empty) is the second line at a marking with p empty: the reset arc on p adds no condition, so t fires there whenever t∖p does. The text does not claim t fires with its other arcs unsatisfied.
- AC2 (all tokens removed on firing) is the first line. A reset place that is also an output place keeps what the firing deposited there (glossary: Deposit); a reset place that is also an input place ends with the deposit too, whatever was consumed from it.
- AC3 (no token requirement, no contribution to enablement) is the second and third lines at every marking, empty or marked.
- AC4 (same deposits with and without the reset arc, compared per place; a reset arc on an output place neither adds nor removes an outcome) is the second line (no outcome removed) and the third line (no outcome added).
- The third line is also the frame of a reset arc: it rules out a reset that also empties a place the transition has no arc to, and a reset whose removed tokens feed a Forward or timeout deposit (they are not consumed; IO-013/IO-014 forward only consumed tokens). It compares against the same transition without the arc rather than spelling out the whole marking change; the effect of the other arcs is CORE-030, CORE-031, CORE-032 and IO-013's subject.
- The comparison is over firing relations, not over which output specs build: a transition and the same transition without its reset arcs have the same output spec, so the build-time checks of IO-011 to IO-014 accept both or neither.
- Token counts only: which tokens are removed, and EXEC-003 AC5 (a reset later in the same firing pass leaves same-pass deposits in place) are below this granularity; that refinement is EXEC-003's subject, not this block's.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-034: `Seam.fireNC` sets a reset place to the deposit count (`refFire_apply`), neither `Seam.enabledNA` nor the deposit (`Seam.nconsumed`) reads the reset arcs, so a firing of `t` and of `t` without its reset arcs on `p` exist at the same markings with the same deposits, and `Seam.fireNC` reads the reset arcs only at the reset place itself.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_034 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_firstBranch` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset arc that removes a deposit violates CORE-034 (second clause): without its reset arc `core034_tXor12` deposits in place `2`, with it only in place `1`.
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_firstBranchFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_keepsTokens` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset arc that removes nothing violates CORE-034 (first clause): under the idle firing `core034_t` fires from `core034_full`, deposits nothing, and place `0` keeps its three tokens.
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_resetAfterDeposit` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset that also removes the firing's own deposit violates CORE-034 (first clause): `core034_tOut` deposits one token in its reset place `0` and must end holding it, not zero.
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_resetAfterDepositFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_resetForwards` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset whose removed tokens feed a forward violates CORE-034 (third clause): on timeout `core034_tFwd` forwards the three tokens its reset removed from place `0` into place `1`, a deposit no firing of `core034_tFwd` without its reset arc makes, in any order (it consumed nothing from `0`).
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_resetForwardsFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_resetNeedsToken` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset arc with a token requirement violates CORE-034 (second clause): without its reset arc `core034_t` fires from the empty marking, with it no firing matches.
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_resetNeedsTokenFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_forbids_resetsNeighbour` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): ✗ A reset that also empties a place the transition has no arc to violates CORE-034 (third clause): without its reset arc `core034_t` leaves place `1` at three tokens, with it place `1` ends at zero.
  <pre>¬CORE_034 core034_resetsNeighbourFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_mutant_dropEmpties_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): The idle firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (every transition fires from every marking, whatever its arcs, deposits nothing and changes nothing) but not CORE-034 (place `0` keeps its tokens).
  <pre>CORE_034_mutant_dropEmpties core034_idleFire ∧ ¬CORE_034 core034_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_mutant_dropFrame_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): The neighbour-emptying firing kills the third mutant: the reset place itself ends with the deposit and every firing without the arc is matched by one with it (the mutant holds), but place `1` is emptied by a reset on place `0` (CORE-034 fails).
  <pre>CORE_034_mutant_dropFrame core034_resetsNeighbourFire ∧<br>  ¬CORE_034 core034_resetsNeighbourFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_mutant_dropRemoves_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): The first-branch firing kills the second mutant: its firings are reference firings, so they empty every reset place and are matched by the firing without the arc (the mutant holds; a deposit of the first branch stays one when a reset arc is dropped, because the deposit does not read the reset arcs), but `core034_tXor12` cannot deposit in place `2` although it can without its reset arc (CORE-034 fails).
  <pre>CORE_034_mutant_dropRemoves core034_firstBranchFire ∧ ¬CORE_034 core034_firstBranchFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_034_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_034): All three clauses of CORE-034 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core034_t` has a reset arc on place `0`; from `core034_full` it fires and empties place `0` (first clause, three tokens removed, none deposited), where without its reset arc place `0` would keep them, while every other place ends the same with and without the arc (third clause); it fires from the empty marking, as it does without its reset arc (second clause, reset place unmarked); `core034_tOut`, whose reset place is also its output place, ends with place `0` holding the one token it deposited there (first clause with a nonzero deposit); and `core034_tXor` (AC4), with the reset arc on one of its two output places, deposits in place `0` or in place `1` exactly as it does without the arc, and after depositing in place `0` holds that token alone there.
  <pre>ResetArc core034_t 0 ∧<br>  RefFire core034_t core034_full [] core034_after ∧<br>    core034_after 0 = 0 ∧<br>      RefFire (withoutReset core034_t 0) core034_full [] core034_full ∧<br>        (∀ (q : ℕ), q ≠ 0 → core034_after q = core034_full q) ∧<br>          Fire RefFire core034_t core034_empty core034_empty ∧<br>            Fire RefFire (withoutReset core034_t 0) core034_empty core034_empty ∧<br>              RefFire core034_tOut core034_full [0] core034_afterOut ∧<br>                core034_afterOut 0 = 1 ∧<br>                  ResetArc core034_tXor 0 ∧<br>                    RefFire core034_tXor core034_full [0] core034_afterOut ∧<br>                      RefFire core034_tXor core034_full [1] core034_afterXor1 ∧<br>                        RefFire (withoutReset core034_tXor 0) core034_full [0]<br>                            core034_afterNoReset0 ∧<br>                          RefFire (withoutReset core034_tXor 0) core034_full [1] fun n ↦<br>                            if n = 1 then 4 else 3</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
