<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_035"></a>
## CORE-035: Output arc

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `7ce7e918319b593c`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *When* a transition fires, it *shall* deposit tokens only in its output places, according to its output spec (see [IO-010]–[IO-015]).<br><br>*When* a transition fires, the marking the firing leaves *shall* hold every token the firing deposited.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, every place that is neither an input place nor a reset place of the transition *shall* change by exactly the tokens the firing deposited there. | 1. For every transition t and every deposit d:<br>2. every firing of t that deposits d puts tokens only in places that t's output spec names;<br>3. every firing of t that deposits d leaves a marking in which every place holds at least as many tokens as d put there;<br>4. every firing of t that deposits d, from m to m', leaves every place p with no input arc and no reset arc of t holding m p plus what d put in p, no more and no less. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N),<br>    (on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦<br>        ∀ (p : N), Produced d p → OutputArc t p) ∧<br>      (on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ ∀ (p : N), Deposits d p ≤ m' p) ∧<br>        on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>          ∀ (p : N),<br>            (∀ (c : Card), ¬InputArc t p c) → ¬ResetArc t p → m' p = m p + Deposits d p</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the tokens of one outcome (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. 'When the transition's action completes' is read as 'when the firing deposits', for every outcome: the timeout outcome ([IO-013]) also deposits only in places the output spec names (a Timeout child, a ForwardInput's `to`).
- An output place is a place the transition's output spec names (glossary: Output arc), every Xor branch, Timeout child and ForwardInput `to` included. A transition with no output spec has no output place, so its firings deposit nothing.
- Which output places one firing deposits in, and how many tokens, is the subject of [IO-010]–[IO-016] ('according to its output spec'), not of this block: a firing relation that never deposits satisfies CORE-035 and is ruled out by [IO-016].
- The second line says only that the deposited tokens are in the resulting marking; the exact count of an input place is [CORE-030]'s and of a reset place [CORE-034]'s. A reset place that is also an output place ends holding exactly what was deposited, which satisfies the line.
- The third line is the marking side of 'only in its output places': on every place [CORE-030] and [CORE-034] do not cover (a pure output place, a read-only or inhibitor-only place, a place with no arc), the firing adds exactly its deposit. A place the firing does not deposit in is therefore unchanged, and an output place keeps its earlier tokens (no overwrite, no double or deduplicated deposit). On read places this coincides with [CORE-032]'s third clause.
- AC1 (after the transition fires, produced tokens appear in the output place) is the second line, with the first saying the place is an output place.
- AC2 (output tokens carry the current timestamp): not encoded. The firing relation works on token counts and carries no token timestamps; timestamps are [CORE-011] and the timed semantics' subject.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): The reference semantics satisfies CORE-035: `Reference.deposit` takes its tokens from an output branch (`Out.branches`) or from the first `Timeout` child (`Out.timeoutDeposits`), both of which name only places of the output spec; and `Seam.fireNC` adds the deposit's counts to every place (after consumption, or in place of the old count on a reset place), so a place with no input or reset arc gains exactly the deposit.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], CORE_035 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_dedup` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ A deduplicating store violates CORE-035 (third clause): `core035_t` fires from `core035_one`, and place `0` keeps one token instead of two.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_dedupFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_double` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ A double deposit violates CORE-035 (third clause): `core035_t` fires from the empty marking and place `0` ends with two tokens for a deposit of one.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_doubleFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_lost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ Losing the deposited tokens violates CORE-035 (second clause): `core035_t` deposits a token in place `0`, but under `core035_lostFire` the marking it leaves is still empty.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_lostFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_overwrite` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ Overwriting an output place violates CORE-035 (third clause): `core035_t` fires from `core035_one`, and place `0` ends with one token instead of two.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_overwriteFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_phantom` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ A phantom write violates CORE-035 (third clause): `core035_t` deposits only in place `0`, yet place `7` gains a token.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_phantomFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_resetAfter` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ Resetting after depositing violates CORE-035 (second clause): `core035_r` deposits a token in its reset place `0`, which `core035_resetAfterFire` then empties.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_resetAfterFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_forbids_stray` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): ✗ Depositing outside the output spec violates CORE-035 (first clause): `core035_silent` has no output spec, yet under `core035_strayFire` it deposits a token in place `0`.
  <pre>¬CORE_035 core035_strayFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_mutant_dropAppear_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): The reset-after firing kills the second mutant: off the reset places it is the reference firing, so it satisfies the mutant, but it empties a reset place the deposit filled.
  <pre>CORE_035_mutant_dropAppear core035_resetAfterFire ∧ ¬CORE_035 core035_resetAfterFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_mutant_dropFrame_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): The double firing kills the third mutant: its deposits are reference deposits and the marking holds them, but it adds each deposit twice.
  <pre>CORE_035_mutant_dropFrame core035_doubleFire ∧ ¬CORE_035 core035_doubleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_mutant_dropOutputs_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): The stray firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (it adds its deposit to the marking) but not CORE-035 (it deposits outside the output spec).
  <pre>CORE_035_mutant_dropOutputs core035_strayFire ∧ ¬CORE_035 core035_strayFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.CORE_035_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.CORE_035): All three clauses of CORE-035 are exercised by the reference semantics: `core035_t` fires from the empty marking depositing a token in place `0`, which is an output place of `core035_t` (first clause) and holds that token afterwards (second clause); fired again from `core035_one`, place `0`, which has no input or reset arc, ends with its old token plus the new one (third clause).
  <pre>RefFire core035_t core035_empty [0] core035_one ∧<br>  Produced [0] 0 ∧<br>    OutputArc core035_t 0 ∧<br>      Deposits [0] 0 = 1 ∧<br>        core035_one 0 = 1 ∧<br>          RefFire core035_t core035_one [0] core035_two ∧<br>            core035_two 0 = core035_one 0 + 1</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-CORE_036"></a>
## CORE-036: Arc semantics summary

`Libpetri.Spec.CORE_036` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors; the outcome line with output validation enabled (CONC-026, the default) · Source **verbatim** (spec/01-core-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `78d3fa5e658ee902`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* an input place of the transition holds fewer tokens than the required count of its input arc, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a read place of the transition is unmarked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* an inhibitor place of the transition is marked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* the transition is enabled, it *shall* be able to fire, whatever its output places and reset places hold.<br><br>*When* the transition fires, each input place that is not a reset place *shall* lose the consumption count of its input arc, and gain what the firing deposits there.<br><br>*When* the transition fires, each place that is neither an input place nor a reset place *shall* lose no tokens, and gain what the firing deposits there.<br><br>*When* the transition fires, each reset place *shall* end holding exactly what the firing deposited there.<br><br>*When* the transition fires and the firing ends with its action, on an executor with output validation enabled ([CONC-026], the default), the places in which it deposits *shall* be exactly the places of one output branch of its output spec, each receiving one or more tokens.<br><br>*When* the transition fires and the firing ends with the timeout outcome, it *shall* deposit in every place as many tokens as the timeout outcome puts there ([IO-013], [IO-014]).<br><br>Deposits are compared per place: the order in which a firing deposits its tokens is not normative, and two deposits that put the same number of tokens in every place are the same deposit. | 1. For every transition t:<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which an input place p holds fewer tokens than the required count c of its input arc;<br>3. t does not fire from a marking in which a read place p is unmarked;<br>4. t does not fire from a marking in which an inhibitor place p is marked;<br>5. from every marking at which t is enabled, some firing of t exists (to some marking);<br>6. every firing of t that deposits d leaves an input place p that is not a reset place with what it held, minus the consumption count of its arc, plus what d puts in p;<br>7. every firing of t that deposits d leaves a place p that is neither an input place nor a reset place of t with what it held plus what d puts in p;<br>8. every firing of t that deposits d leaves a reset place p with exactly what d puts in p;<br>9. every firing of t from m that deposits d either puts one or more tokens in each place of some output branch b of t and none in any other place, or puts in every place as many tokens as some timeout outcome d₀ of t for what the firing consumed from m. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>    (∀ (p : N) (c : Card),<br>        InputArc t p c → while fun m ↦ TokenCount m p &lt; requiredCount c, never Fire F t) ∧<br>      (∀ (p : N), ReadArc t p → while fun m ↦ ¬Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (∀ (p : N), InhibitorArc t p → while fun m ↦ Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>          (∀ (m : CountMarking N), Enabled t m → ∃ m', Fire F t m m') ∧<br>            (∀ (d : List N) (p : N) (c : Card),<br>                InputArc t p c →<br>                  ¬ResetArc t p →<br>                    on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>                      TokenCount m' p =<br>                        TokenCount m p - consumptionCount c (TokenCount m p) +<br>                          Deposits d p) ∧<br>              (∀ (d : List N) (p : N),<br>                  (∀ (c : Card), ¬InputArc t p c) →<br>                    ¬ResetArc t p →<br>                      on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>                        TokenCount m' p = TokenCount m p + Deposits d p) ∧<br>                (∀ (d : List N) (p : N),<br>                    ResetArc t p →<br>                      on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦<br>                        TokenCount m' p = Deposits d p) ∧<br>                  ∀ (d : List N),<br>                    on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m x ↦<br>                      (∃ b, OutputBranch t b ∧ OnPlacesOf d b) ∨<br>                        ∃ d₀, TimeoutOutcome t (Consumes t m) d₀ ∧ SamePerPlace d d₀</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part, so 'produced on complete' (Output) and 'consumed / removed on fire' (Input, Reset) both land in the one step. 'Able to fire' is read as 'some firing exists', to any target marking.
- Requires token? column. 'Yes' for an input arc is read as 'holds at least the required count' (glossary: Enabled, Required count), which is one token for One and All; 'Yes' for a read arc as 'marked'; 'No (blocks if present)' for an inhibitor arc as 'marked blocks'. The 'No' entries (output, reset, and the inhibitor arc while its place is unmarked) are read together as the fourth line: the table lists every token requirement, so a transition whose input, read and inhibitor arcs are all satisfied can fire whatever its output and reset places hold.
- Consumes? column. 'Yes' for an input arc is read as the consumption count of IO-007 (glossary: Consumption count). 'No' for output, inhibitor and read arcs is the sixth line: a place that only those arcs touch loses nothing. 'Yes (all)' for a reset arc is the seventh line (glossary: Deposit): a reset place that is also an output place keeps what the firing deposited there, and a reset place that is also an input place ends with the deposit too, so the fifth line excludes reset places.
- The fifth line uses truncated subtraction, as Seam.fireNC does; under the first line the subtraction never truncates, because a firing only starts when every input place holds at least its required count.
- Effect column, Output ('Token produced on complete') is the outcome line, the last line of the reading, which reads the eighth, ninth and tenth sentences quoted together. A firing relation does not record how a firing ended. As IO-012 says, a firing ends with its action when it does not end with the timeout outcome, and a firing whose deposit puts in every place as many tokens as some timeout-outcome deposit of the transition from the same marking may have ended with the timeout outcome. So the eighth and ninth sentences are read as one disjunction: the deposit marks exactly the places of an output branch (eighth: the branch fixes which places receive tokens, one or more each, not how many; glossary: Output branch, Produced set), or it equals a timeout outcome per place (ninth: as many tokens in every place). A transition with no output spec has the one empty branch, so it deposits nothing. Read ('Token remains') is the sixth line. Inhibitor ('Disables transition') is the third line.
- Tenth sentence (deposits are compared per place). Every line of the reading reads the deposit d only through Deposits d (the produced set of d is the set of places p with Deposits d p ≠ 0), so two deposits that put the same number of tokens in every place satisfy it alike and the order of d plays no part: core036_c8_perPlace shows it for the outcome line, and core036_perPlace_closed shows that closing any firing relation under per-place equal deposits keeps CORE-036. Lines 5 to 7 already compare per place.
- The outcome line is scoped by CONC-026: F is read as the firing relation of an executor with output validation enabled, the default. With the CONC-026 skip enabled a firing that ends with its action deposits what the action wrote, as CONC-026 says, and the outcome line is not claimed of it; lines 1 to 7 hold of every executor.
- TimeoutOutcome reads the first Timeout node in pre-order. A transition that builds holds at most one Timeout node and no ForwardInput outside a Timeout child (IO-013, IO-014), so that node's outcome is the timeout outcome. Transitions that building rejects (IO-011 to IO-014) are not transitions of any net (CORE-022); the statement still ranges over every Trans, and the reference semantics satisfies it for all of them, so it needs no well-formedness premise. That at most one output branch matches a deposit (IO-012) is not part of CORE-036: the outcome line asks for some branch.
- Read, 'provides value': the value of the read token is not visible at token-count granularity; that the action receives it is CORE-032 AC3, not encoded here.
- Input token order (FIFO, CORE-030 AC2) and output token timestamps (CORE-035 AC2, IO-014 AC5) are below token-count granularity and not encoded here.
- AC1 (each arc type behaves exactly as described in the table) is the whole statement: one line per cell of the 'Requires token?' column (lines 1 to 4) and of the 'Consumes?' and 'Effect' columns (lines 5 to 7 and the outcome line), as laid out in the assumptions above.
- AC2 (an action that writes two tokens into one place of the branch it satisfies fires, and the marking receives all of them) is CORE_036_allows_multi: CORE-036 holds of a firing relation in which t: a → and(b, c) fires with the write b, b, c, and that firing leaves 2 tokens in b and 1 in c. That such a write is deposited at all is IO-015 and IO-016 AC4; CORE-036 only has to allow it.
- AC3 (two firings that deposit the same number of tokens in every place, in different orders, deposit the same outcome) is core036_c8_perPlace and core036_perPlace_closed, and concretely CORE_036_allows_multi: the write c, b, b of the same transition is allowed alike and leaves the same marking.
- InputArc names the first input arc on a place (glossary: Input arc); CORE-030 rejects a transition with two input arcs on one place at build, so for every well-formed transition it is the only one.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-EXEC_002"></a>
## EXEC-002: Priority-Based Firing Order

`Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002` · Kind **F** · Scope every executor backend; on the all-immediate fast path only what both outcomes of ruling D14 share · Source **verbatim** (spec/04-execution-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `d48569d73495c708`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| When multiple transitions are ready to fire (armed, past their earliest bound and not expired; see the glossary), they are sorted by:<br><br>Priority, descending: a higher priority fires first.<br><br>Stamp, ascending (FIFO): among equal priority, the transition armed first fires first ([TIME-010]).<br><br>Declaration order, ascending: among equal priority and equal stamp, the transition declared first fires first.<br><br>The ready order of a firing pass *shall* hold every ready transition exactly once and no other transition.<br><br>*Where* the net does not take the all-immediate fast path, the ready order *shall* offer a ready transition of higher priority before a ready transition of lower priority.<br><br>*Where* the net does not take the all-immediate fast path, the ready order *shall* offer, among ready transitions of equal priority, the one with the earlier stamp first.<br><br>The ready order *shall* offer, among ready transitions of equal priority and equal stamp, the earlier-declared one first.<br><br>The sort above describes the general path.<br><br>Equal priority and equal stamp (transitions newly armed by the enablement update of the same orchestrator cycle share one stamp, [TIME-010]): the tie breaks by ascending declaration order, so the order is fully deterministic. | 1. For every input of a pass, fast path included:<br>2. the order holds every ready transition exactly once and nothing else;<br>3. two transitions of equal priority and equal stamp come in ascending declaration order;<br>4. and off the all-immediate fast path, the order is strictly sorted by priority descending, then stamp ascending, then declaration order ascending. | <pre>fun O ↦<br>  ∀ (I : ReadyIn),<br>    (O I).Perm I.readyList ∧<br>      List.Pairwise I.tieLt (O I) ∧ (¬I.FastPath → List.Pairwise I.key.lt (O I))</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is an ordering function O from the inputs of one pass (ReadyIn: the net's transitions numbered in declaration order, their priorities, their timings, their stamps and which are ready) to the list of ready transitions in the order the pass offers them for firing. Ready is the glossary term (armed, past the earliest bound, not expired); the stamp is the firing-clock reading taken by the enablement update that most recently newly armed the transition (glossary: Armed, [TIME-010]); the field ReadyIn.clock holds it. In the block these are free inputs; ReadyIn.ofTSt reads them off a timed state of the reference step (the state's stamp, glossary Ready as the ready bit), and ClashesTime shows a bridged input reached by the timed reference step. Which reading becomes a stamp is TIME-010's, not this block's. Which of the offered transitions fires is EXEC-003, not this block.
- The four 'shall' sentences are the three lines of the formula. The first is line 1. The fourth, on every input, is line 2. The second and third, off the fast path, are line 3 together with lines 1 and 2: since the order holds every ready transition exactly once, being strictly sorted by the key (priority descending, stamp ascending, declaration order ascending) is the same as offering, of every two ready transitions, the one of higher priority first, of equal priority the one with the earlier stamp first, and of equal priority and stamp the earlier-declared one first (EXEC_002_iff_sentences). The three items of the sort list are the same key, item by item.
- 'All-immediate fast path': ruling D14 is open. The section names the condition: 'When every transition is `Immediate` and all priorities are equal, backends fire straight from the enablement bitmap in ascending declaration order and do not read the enablement timestamp at all — including when two ready transitions carry different timestamps because one was held across cycles while in flight.' On the fast path the block asks only what both outcomes of D14 share: the first two lines. Whether the fast-path order is declaration order or the general sort when timestamps differ is not encoded; both satisfy the block (EXEC_002_D14_neutral). 'This is deliberate, and every backend takes the path under the same condition.' is part of that paragraph and is not encoded either.
- AC1 (priority 10 before priority 5) and AC2 (equal priority on the general path, the one armed first, with the earlier stamp, fires first) are the third line; AC3 (equal priority and equal stamp, ascending declaration order) is the second line, on every input. That transitions newly armed by the enablement update of one cycle share one stamp is TIME-010's stamp rule at the grain of a cycle, deferred to Phase 2 (RefExec); in the block the stamps are free inputs, so AC3 is checked on every pair with equal stamps. EXEC_002_nonvacuous checks the test derivation (priorities 5, 10, 5, all Immediate, armed together), AC2 and AC3 on the reference order, and the fast path.
- AC4 (every backend produces the identical ready order) follows on the general path: two ordering functions that satisfy the block agree on every input that does not take the fast path (EXEC_002_unique), because the key is total on distinct transitions. 'Stamp order is the general-path order' is line 3. That the shipped backends compute it is Q evidence, cited from Sched.lean (collect_ready_general_refines) and Refinement.lean (precompiled_refines_bitmap_immediate, the immediate fragment). AC4's 'Backends MUST NOT differ in which order they use, nor in the condition that selects it.' is covered only through the one fixed condition ReadyIn.FastPath, which the block uses for every backend; a backend whose own fast-path condition is narrower or wider is visible only where its order then breaks the first two lines or, off ReadyIn.FastPath, the third (a narrower condition that checks only the ready transitions is killed, EXEC_002_forbids_readyFast). Pinning the fast-path order, which would make every condition disagreement visible, waits for D14.
- 'Fires first' is read as 'is offered first in the pass's order'; with tokens to compete for, the earlier transition takes them (EXEC-003). That a pass starts its firings in this order is a Phase 2 behaviour line over the executor, not this block.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): ✓ The reference ready order satisfies EXEC-002.
  <pre>EXEC_002 refReadyOrder</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_forbids_allFast` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): ✗ An order that offers a transition that is not ready on the fast path violates EXEC-002: on `exec002_Ifp` it offers transition `1`.
  <pre>¬EXEC_002 exec002_allFast</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_forbids_lifo` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): ✗ The LIFO order violates EXEC-002 (AC2): on `exec002_Ia` it offers `0`, armed last, before `1`, armed first.
  <pre>¬EXEC_002 exec002_lifoOrder</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_forbids_readyFast` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): ✗ The narrower condition violates EXEC-002 (AC4, the condition that selects the order): on a net with a `Delayed` transition that is not ready, the two ready `Immediate` transitions of one priority come in declaration order, though `1` was armed first and the net is on the general path.
  <pre>¬EXEC_002 exec002_readyFast</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_forbids_scan` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): ✗ The declaration-order scan violates EXEC-002 (AC1): in the test derivation the priority-5 transition `0` comes before the priority-10 transition `1`.
  <pre>¬EXEC_002 exec002_scanOrder</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_mutant_generalOnly_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): The reversed fast path kills the mutant: it satisfies the general-path-only reading, but on the fast-path input `exec002_Ifp` it offers `2` before `0`, though both outcomes of D14 break that tie of priority and stamp by ascending declaration order (AC3).
  <pre>EXEC_002_mutant_generalOnly exec002_revFast ∧ ¬EXEC_002 exec002_revFast</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_mutant_tid_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): The pre-fix drain kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-002. On `exec002_Ia` it offers `0` before `1`, though `1` was armed first.
  <pre>EXEC_002_mutant_tid exec002_tidOrder ∧ ¬EXEC_002 exec002_tidOrder</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_mutant_tieDesc_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): The descending tie-break kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-002. In the test derivation the two priority-5 transitions, armed at the same reading, come out as `2` before `0`.
  <pre>EXEC_002_mutant_tieDesc exec002_descOrder ∧ ¬EXEC_002 exec002_descOrder</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_002_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_002): Every line of EXEC-002 is exercised by the reference order. AC2 and the AC4 test derivation: two equal-priority transitions armed in the order opposite to their declaration come out as `[1, 0]` (third line). The test derivation of AC1 and AC3: priorities 5, 10, 5, all `Immediate`, armed together, come out as P10, then the two P5 in declaration order, `[1, 0, 2]` (second and third lines). A transition that is not ready is left out (first line), off the fast path and on it; on the fast path the two ready transitions, tied, come out in declaration order (first and second lines).
  <pre>¬exec002_Ia.FastPath ∧<br>  refReadyOrder exec002_Ia = [1, 0] ∧<br>    ¬exec002_I3.FastPath ∧<br>      refReadyOrder exec002_I3 = [1, 0, 2] ∧<br>        ¬(have __src := exec002_I3;<br>              { n := __src.n, prio := __src.prio, timing := __src.timing,<br>                clock := __src.clock, ready := fun t ↦ t != 1 }).FastPath ∧<br>          refReadyOrder<br>                (have __src := exec002_I3;<br>                { n := __src.n, prio := __src.prio, timing := __src.timing,<br>                  clock := __src.clock, ready := fun t ↦ t != 1 }) =<br>              [0, 2] ∧<br>            exec002_Ifp.FastPath ∧<br>              exec002_Ifp.ready 1 = false ∧ refReadyOrder exec002_Ifp = [0, 2]</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. <a id="Libpetri-Spec-EXEC_010"></a>
## EXEC-010: FIFO Token Consumption

`Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/04-execution-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `c628c652e6b36318`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| When a transition fires, it consumes tokens from the oldest-first (FIFO) end of each input place's queue.<br><br>The number of tokens consumed is determined by the input cardinality's `consumptionCount(available)`.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, it *shall* consume from each of its input places that is not a correlated input of its match specification ([NU-020]) the oldest tokens the place holds, as many as the consumption count of the input arc.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, its action *shall* receive the tokens consumed from each input place that is not a correlated input of its match specification in queue order, the oldest first.<br><br>A correlated input of a ν-net match specification is the one exception to oldest-first consumption: it takes the matched tokens of the chosen name, oldest first among the tokens of that name, so it can pass over an older token of another name ([NU-020]).<br><br>Its non-correlated inputs consume as above. | 1. For every queue firing relation Q:<br>2. for every firing of a transition t and every input place p of t that is not a reset place of t: the queue p holds after the firing begins with the tokens of p's queue before the firing after its oldest Consumes tokens, in their queue order, and it holds exactly those tokens and the tokens the action emits to p, in some order;<br>3. for every firing of a transition t and every input place p of t: the tokens delivered to the action from p are the oldest Consumes tokens of p's queue before the firing, in queue order. | <pre>fun {N T} [DecidableEq N] Q ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>      ∀ p ∈ inPlaces t,<br>        ¬ResetArc t p →<br>          on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M M' ↦<br>            List.drop (Consumes t (Counts M) p) (M p) &lt;+: M' p ∧<br>              (M' p).Perm (List.drop (Consumes t (Counts M) p) (M p) ++ EmittedTo e p)) ∧<br>    ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>      ∀ p ∈ inPlaces t,<br>        on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M x ↦<br>          δ.inputs p = OldestFirst M p (Consumes t (Counts M) p)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing relates a queue marking before it to the queue marking after it, with what it delivered to the action and the emissions of the action (glossary: Queue order, QueueMarking, Delivered, QFireRel). Consumes t (Counts M) p is the consumption count of t's input arc on p at the token counts of M (glossary: Consumption count, Consume), so 'as many as the consumption count of the input arc' is the length of the prefix both lines name, and the paragraph 'The number of tokens consumed is determined by the input cardinality's consumptionCount(available)' is the same count.
- Correlated inputs (ruling D16): the transitions of the model (Reference.Trans) carry no ν-net match specification, so every input place is an input that is not a correlated input of a match specification, and the lines quantify over every input place. The exception paragraph is therefore not a line of the formula: how a correlated input consumes (the matched tokens of the chosen name, oldest first among them) is NU-020's, not encoded here; this block makes no claim about a correlated input, and a mutant that consumed a correlated input oldest first would not be killed by it.
- Line 1 is the first 'shall' sentence. It is stated at every input place that is not a reset place, including a place the action also emits to (a self-loop): the place keeps its tokens after the oldest Consumes ones, in queue order and ahead of every token emitted to it, and gains exactly the tokens emitted to it, so the tokens that left it are the oldest. Keeping the kept tokens ahead (a prefix, not only a subsequence) is what makes the consumed tokens the oldest when token values repeat: a place holding 10, 11, 10 whose action emits 10 back would otherwise pass with 10, 11, 10, the newest 10 consumed, which no count or value check can tell from the oldest 10 consumed and the emission put at the head (killed by EXEC_010_mutant_sublist_killed). That the emitted tokens follow the kept ones agrees with EXEC-020's tail append; the order among the emitted tokens is EXEC-020's, so line 1 fixes them only up to order. A reset place is left to line 2: it ends the firing holding exactly what was deposited there (CORE-034). With nothing emitted to the place, line 1 says the queue before the firing is its oldest Consumes tokens followed by the queue after it (exec010_line1_noEmission). The line is exercised by EXEC_010_nonvacuous; a relation that removes the newest tokens from a self-loop place is killed by EXEC_010_forbids_selfLoopNewest.
- Line 2 is the second 'shall' sentence: the action receives, from each input place, the consumed tokens in queue order, the oldest first (OldestFirst, the FIFO prefix). On an input place that is also a reset place line 2 is the only line: the consumed prefix is still the oldest, the rest is drained. Line 2 says nothing about what is delivered from a place that is not an input place; the spec is silent there, and this block makes no claim about it.
- The firing is the atomic firing of the reference: the counts the consumption count reads are the counts at the start of the firing. Same-pass deposits (EXEC-003 AC5: a draining arc ships the pass-start count, not the live one) need the cycle relation of Phase 2 (RefExec) and are deferred to it; consume_faithful (Conservation.lean) states the same FIFO prefix for one firing on the compiled consume program, under the module's assumption that no same-pass deposit is outstanding.
- A transition with two input arcs on one place is rejected when it is built (CORE-030); the model reads the first arc (nspecAt), as Consumes does.
- AC1 (tokens A, B, C added in that order; One consumes A), AC2 (Exactly(2) consumes A, B) and AC3 (All consumes A, B, C) are EXEC_010_nonvacuous, with tokens 10, 11, 12 for A, B, C: the reference delivers [10], [10, 11] and [10, 11, 12] and leaves [11, 12], [12] and [].
- The test derivation (identifiable token values, fire, check the consumed tokens are in FIFO order) is the same check as AC1 to AC3.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): The reference queue firing satisfies EXEC-010: from each place it delivers the FIFO prefix of length `Consumes` (`refQFire_inputs`, line 2), and a place that is not a reset place keeps the rest of its queue followed by the tokens emitted to it (`refQFire_apply`, line 1).
  <pre>∀ {N T : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], EXEC_010 RefQFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_forbids_dropNewest` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): ✗ Removing the newest tokens violates EXEC-010 (line 1), even though the action receives the oldest ones: `One` from `[10, 11, 12]` leaves `[10, 11]`.
  <pre>¬EXEC_010 (exec010_by exec010_dropNewestSel)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_forbids_reversed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): ✗ Delivering the consumed tokens newest first violates EXEC-010 (line 2, "in queue order, the oldest first"): `Exactly(2)` from `[10, 11, 12]` delivers `[11, 10]`.
  <pre>¬EXEC_010 (exec010_by exec010_revSel)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_forbids_selfLoopNewest` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): ✗ Removing the newest tokens from a self-loop place violates EXEC-010 (line 1), even though the action receives the oldest ones: `exec010_selfT` from `[10, 11, 12]`, the action emitting `30` back to `0`, delivers `[10]` and leaves `[10, 11, 30]` instead of `[11, 12, 30]`. The transition has no read arc (`exec010_selfT_noInputRead`), so ruling D15 does not exclude it.
  <pre>¬EXEC_010 exec010_selfLoopNewestQ</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_mutant_lifo_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): The LIFO firing kills the LIFO mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-010, since `One` from `[10, 11, 12]` delivers `[12]` instead of `[10]`.
  <pre>EXEC_010_mutant_lifo (exec010_by exec010_lifoSel) ∧ ¬EXEC_010 (exec010_by exec010_lifoSel)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_mutant_sublist_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): The subsequence reading cannot tell the newest `10` consumed from the oldest `10` consumed with the emission put at the head: `exec010_dupNewestQ` satisfies it. EXEC-010 kills it, since the queue `10, 11, 10` does not begin with `11, 10`, so the next `One` firing would receive `10` where the spec requires `11`.
  <pre>EXEC_010_mutant_sublist exec010_dupNewestQ ∧ ¬EXEC_010 exec010_dupNewestQ</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_mutant_subset_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): The skip-the-head firing kills the subset mutant: it satisfies the subset reading and keeps queue order on both sides, but not EXEC-010, since `One` from `[10, 11, 12]` delivers `[11]` and leaves `[10, 12]`.
  <pre>EXEC_010_mutant_subset (exec010_by exec010_skipHeadSel) ∧<br>  ¬EXEC_010 (exec010_by exec010_skipHeadSel)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_010_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_010): Every line of EXEC-010 is exercised by the reference. The examples fire `qexT c` (input `c` on place `0`, a read and a reset on `1`, output place `2`) from `qexM` (tokens `10, 11, 12` on `0`, added in that order), the action emitting `30` to `2`: place `0` is an input place that is not a reset place and receives no emission, so both lines apply. AC1: `One` delivers `[10]` and leaves `[11, 12]`; AC2: `Exactly(2)` delivers `[10, 11]` and leaves `[12]`; AC3: `All` delivers `[10, 11, 12]` and leaves `[]`.
  <pre>(0 ∈ inPlaces (qexT Card.one) ∧<br>    ¬ResetArc (qexT Card.one) 0 ∧ EmittedTo exec010_e 0 = []) ∧<br>  (∃ δ M',<br>      RefQFire (qexT Card.one) qexM δ exec010_e M' ∧<br>        δ.inputs 0 = [10] ∧ M' 0 = [11, 12]) ∧<br>    (∃ δ M',<br>        RefQFire (qexT (Card.exactly 2)) qexM δ exec010_e M' ∧<br>          δ.inputs 0 = [10, 11] ∧ M' 0 = [12]) ∧<br>      ∃ δ M',<br>        RefQFire (qexT Card.all) qexM δ exec010_e M' ∧<br>          δ.inputs 0 = [10, 11, 12] ∧ M' 0 = []</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_queue` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyQueue): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002, the TIME blocks and the queue blocks EXEC-010, EXEC-012, EXEC-013 and EXEC-020 hold together on one model: the model of `spec_consistent_coreIO_time`, with one builder for the IO build lines and EXEC-012's, and a queue firing relation whose firings, counted, are the firings of the same firing relation `F`, and which lifts every firing of `F`. The witnesses are the reference builder, the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order, the timed reference step and the reference queue firing.
  <pre>∀ {N Tk : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R QF,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br> R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧<br> <a id="Libpetri-Spec-EXEC_012"></a>
## EXEC-012: Read Arc Peek

`Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_012` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors and transition builders · Source **verbatim** (spec/04-execution-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `731597b373125339`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| When a transition fires, read arc values are provided to the action without consuming the tokens.<br><br>The oldest token in the read place is peeked.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, its action *shall* receive, for each of its read places, the oldest token the place holds.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, each of its read places that is not one of its reset places *shall* end the firing holding the tokens it held before the firing, in the same queue order, followed by the tokens the firing deposits there.<br><br>*If* a transition declares an input arc and a read arc on the same place, *then* building the transition *shall* fail with<br><br>The message is the same, byte for byte, in every implementation.<br><br>"The same place" is the implementation's own `Place` equality, as for [CORE-030] ([CORE-002]; name-and-type in Java, name-only in TypeScript and Rust).<br><br>It runs after every input-arc check ([CORE-030], [IO-002], [IO-004]) and before the timing check ([TIME-001]) and the output-spec checks ([IO-011]–[IO-014]).<br><br>It walks the read arcs in declaration order and names the place of the first read arc whose place is an input place of the transition. | 1. Let Build be the builder (Build t = none: t builds; Build t = some msg: building t fails with msg) and Q the queue firings.<br>2. For every transition t with no input arc and read arc on one place, every read place p of t, every delivery δ and every emission e, every firing of t from the queue marking M that delivers δ and emits e delivers at p the head of p's queue in M: the first token delivered at p is the head of M at p, every token delivered at p is that head, and when t has one read arc on p at most one token is delivered there;<br>3. for every such t, every read place p of t that is not a reset place of t, every δ and every e, every firing of t from M to M' that delivers δ and emits e leaves at p the queue M held at p, identity and order, followed by the tokens e emits to p in emission order (AppendedAtTail);<br>4. every transition with an input arc and a read arc on one place fails to build;<br>5. every transition whose input places are distinct and whose input arcs each require at least one token, and whose first read arc on an input place, in declaration order, is on p, fails to build with the message msgInputRead t p;<br>6. no transition whose input places are not distinct, or one of whose input arcs requires fewer than one token, fails to build with this message, for any place;<br>7. no transition with no input arc and read arc on one place fails to build with this message, for any place. | <pre>fun {N T} [DecidableEq N] [ToString N] Build Q ↦<br>  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N) (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>      NoInputRead t →<br>        ReadArc t p →<br>          on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M x ↦<br>            (δ.reads p).head? = Head M p ∧<br>              (∀ y ∈ δ.reads p, Head M p = some y) ∧<br>                (List.count p t.core.reads = 1 → (δ.reads p).length ≤ 1)) ∧<br>    (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N) (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>        NoInputRead t →<br>          ReadArc t p →<br>            ¬ResetArc t p →<br>              on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M M' ↦ AppendedAtTail M M' e p) ∧<br>      (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N), ¬NoInputRead t → Build t ≠ none) ∧<br>        (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>            InputsDistinct t →<br>              InputCountsPositive t →<br>                firstInputRead t = some p → Build t = some (msgInputRead t p)) ∧<br>          (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>              ¬InputsDistinct t ∨ ¬InputCountsPositive t →<br>                Build t ≠ some (msgInputRead t p)) ∧<br>            ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>              NoInputRead t → Build t ≠ some (msgInputRead t p)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The model is the pair (Build, Q). Build is the transition builder, read as the message building a transition fails with, none when it builds; the witness is the reference builder refBuild (glossary: Build-time checks), which runs the [IO-002]/[IO-004] count checks (stand-in message msgCount) and the D15 check after its CORE-030 check and before its output-spec checks; it is the builder of the CORE and IO blocks too. Q is a queue firing relation (glossary: Queue order; QFireRel in GlossaryQueue.lean): a firing of t from the queue marking M, the head of each queue the oldest token, that delivers δ (the consumed input tokens and the read tokens, per place) to the action, whose action emits e (places and tokens, in emission order), and that leaves M'. The witness is the reference queue firing RefQFire. Timing and priority play no part.
- Ruling D17: a Q step is a firing whose completion the executor admits and whose produced tokens pass output validation ([EXEC-020], [EXEC-021]), so e is what the firing deposits. A firing whose completion is not admitted (its timeout outcome [EXEC-022], a stop on a terminal place [EXEC-042], an immediate termination [ENV-015]) or whose output validation fails ([EXEC-021], TransitionFailed) is not a Q step, and line 2 says nothing about it; what such a firing leaves in its read places (the queue, plus the tokens [IO-015] already published) is [EXEC-020]'s and [EXEC-021]'s. The witness RefQFire has only admitted, validated firings (deposit ... = some (e.map fst)).
- Firings are atomic: line 2 is the firing's own effect on p, with no other firing consuming from p or depositing to p between the firing's start and its completion. In the runtimes an action can be in flight while other firings consume from a shared read place or deposit to it first ([EXEC-003]; ruling D7, live intermediate marking), so the second shall sentence read of the whole interval does not hold of them; which of the two the sentence means is not ruled and is not encoded.
- Ruling D15: the third shall sentence is lines 3 to 6. The firing lines (1 and 2) speak only of transitions with no input arc and read arc on one place (NoInputRead), because line 3 rejects every other transition when it is built, so no executor holds one. On those transitions the queue of a read place after input consumption is its queue before the firing (exec012_head_postInput), so the read sees the head 'after input consumption' ([EXEC-013] AC4) and 'the oldest token the place holds' at once; and draining a reset place comes after the peek, so a read and a reset on one place deliver the pre-drain head (line 1 reads M, before the drain; [EXEC-013]).
- Line 1 reads 'receive, for each of its read places, the oldest token the place holds' as: every token the firing delivers at the read place is the head of its queue, the first is the head, and a single read arc on p delivers at most one token there, so a marked p with one read arc delivers exactly its head (a firing that hands the head over twice is killed as EXEC_012_mutant_noCount). How many copies a transition that declares two read arcs on one place gets is not fixed by the sentence, so for it the line constrains only which token they are. That a firing needs its read places marked is [CORE-032]'s and [CORE-022]'s; on an unmarked read place line 1 forbids a delivered token (its head is none).
- Line 2 is the second shall sentence and 'without consuming the tokens': the read place keeps its queue, identity and order, and the firing's deposits there follow at the tail ([EXEC-020]). A read place that is also a reset place is drained ([EXEC-013], [CORE-034]) and is excluded, as the sentence says. Keeping only the count (a firing that reorders the read place) is killed as EXEC_012_mutant_countOnly, and a read that consumes the head as EXEC_012_mutant_readConsumes.
- Lines 4 to 6 are the check order and the named place. The message is msgInputRead, the fenced message with <t> the transition's name and <p> the place; exec012_msg pins the template byte for byte, and how an implementation renders <t> and <p> follows its sibling build messages (ruling D15: Java may differ only there). Line 4 names the first read arc, in declaration order, whose place is an input place (firstInputRead), when the input-arc checks that run before it pass: distinct input places ([CORE-030], InputsDistinct) and input arcs that each require at least one token ([IO-002] AC1, [IO-004] AC1; InputCountsPositive). Line 5 is 'after every input-arc check': a transition that fails one of them fails with that check's message, not this one (two input arcs on one place: the [CORE-030] stand-in, EXEC_012_forbids_early; an exactly(0) input arc: the count stand-in, EXEC_012_forbids_beforeCounts). Line 6 says the message names only an input-and-read pair: a builder that also rejects a read and a reset on one place with it is forbidden as EXEC_012_forbids_readReset.
- 'Before the output-spec checks' is encoded by line 4, which asks for this message whatever the output spec, so a transition that also breaks an output-spec check fails with this message (EXEC_012_before_output). 'Before the timing check ([TIME-001])' is quoted but not encoded: the transition of this model carries no timing (timing is TimedNet's, and TIME-001's builder is a separate contract over timings), so this builder never runs that check, and nothing here tells the order against it apart.
- 'The same place' is equality on N, read as the implementation's own Place identity ([CORE-002]): N is abstract with decidable equality (the place name in TypeScript and Rust, name and type in Java), and ToString N renders the place's name in the message. 'On every construction path' (channel merge [MOD-021], instantiation [MOD-020], fusion [MOD-061]) is not encoded: Build reads one transition definition, and the construction paths that reach it are the composition model's.
- AC1 (read place has tokens A, B; the action receives A; both A and B remain after firing) is EXEC_012_nonvacuous: a reference firing with one input arc on place 0 and one read arc on place 1, which holds 20 then 21, delivers 20 at 1 and leaves 20, 21 there, followed by the token 30 the action emits to 1.
- AC2 (a one() input arc and a read arc on one place is rejected with the message, and so are all(), exactly(2) and atLeast(1)) is EXEC_012_ac2, under the reference builder.
- AC3 (inputs on a and b with reads declared b then a names b; two input arcs on p and a read on p give the CORE-030 message) is EXEC_012_ac3; the CORE-030 message is the reference builder's stand-in, which line 5 keeps apart from this message.
- AC4 (an input on a and a read on b builds; a read and a reset on one place builds) is EXEC_012_ac4 for the witness. The statement asks of AC4's transitions only that they do not fail with this message (line 6); that they build at all is the build-time checks' (glossary: Builds), not this requirement's.
- Without ruling D15 a read on an input place could be read as a peek before consumption. That reading (a builder with no D15 check and a firing that reads the pre-firing head on every transition) is EXEC_012_mutant_preFixReads. It is killed by the build line alone: on transitions with no input arc and read arc on one place its pre-consumption peek and RefQFire agree, as D15 intends. The reference builder without the check (exec012_buildNoCheck, refBuild as it was before ruling D15) is forbidden as EXEC_012_forbids_noCheck.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_012_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_012): ✓ The reference pair satisfies EXEC-012: `RefQFire` delivers at a read place the head of the queue after input consumption (`qReads`), which on a transition with no input arc and read arc on one place is the head before the firing (`exec012_head_postInput`), one copy per read arc (line 1); it drains only reset places and appends the emitted tokens at the tail, so a read place that is not a reset place keeps its queue (line 2, `refQFire_read_kept`); and `refBuild` rejects every transition with an input arc and a read arc on one place, with the D15 message after the [CORE-030] and count checks and before the output-spec checks, and never with that message otherwise (lines 3 to 6).
  <pre>∀ {N T : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N], EXEC_012 refBuild RefQFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_012_forbids_beforeCounts` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_012): ✗ A builder that runs the D15 check before the count checks violates line 5: an `exactly(0)` input arc and a read arc on place `0` fail with this message instead of the count check's.

- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_012_forbids_early` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_012): ✗ A builder that runs the D15 check before the [CORE-030] check violates line 5: on AC3's second transition it fails with this message instead of [CORE-030]'s.

- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_012_forbids_readReset` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_012): ✗ A builder that rejects a read and a reset on one place with this message violates line 6: AC4's second transition has no input arc and read arc on one place. Lines 3 to 5 alone allow it.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-EXEC_013"></a>
## EXEC-013: Reset Arc Execution

`Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/04-execution-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `5aef35dcb440cf25`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| When a transition fires, all tokens are removed from each reset place.<br><br>This happens during the firing step, before the action executes.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, it *shall* drain each of its reset places ([CORE-034]) before its action starts.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, it *shall* consume from its input places first, then peek its read places, then drain its reset places.<br><br>*When* a transition with a read arc and a reset arc on the same place fires, its action *shall* receive as the read token the oldest token the place held before the drain.<br><br>*If* a reset place of a firing transition is unmarked, *then* the drain *shall* remove nothing and the firing *shall* proceed. | 1. For every transition t with no input arc and read arc on one place (ruling D15):<br>2. every firing of t that emits e leaves each reset place of t holding exactly the tokens e sends there, in emission order;<br>3. every firing of t from M that delivers δ hands the action, from each input place of t that is also a reset place, as many tokens as the input arc consumes, taken from the place's queue in M and kept in queue order;<br>4. every firing of t from M that delivers δ hands the action, for each place that is both a read place and a reset place of t, a nonempty read bag whose every token is the head of the place's queue in M;<br>5. from every queue marking M at which t is enabled and one of its reset places is unmarked, every emission e whose places are a deposit t offers is the emission of some firing of t from M. | <pre>fun {N T} [DecidableEq N] Q ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>    NoInputRead t →<br>      (∀ (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>          on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun x M' ↦<br>            ∀ (p : N), ResetArc t p → M' p = EmittedTo e p) ∧<br>        (∀ (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>            on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M x ↦<br>              ∀ p ∈ inPlaces t,<br>                ResetArc t p →<br>                  (δ.inputs p).length = Consumes t (Counts M) p ∧<br>                    (δ.inputs p).Sublist (M p)) ∧<br>          (∀ (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>              on fun M M' ↦ Q t M δ e M', shall fun M x ↦<br>                ∀ (p : N),<br>                  ReadArc t p →<br>                    ResetArc t p →<br>                      δ.reads p ≠ [] ∧ ∀ tok ∈ δ.reads p, Head M p = some tok) ∧<br>            ∀ (M : QueueMarking N T) (e : List (N × T)),<br>              (∃ p, ResetArc t p ∧ M p = []) →<br>                Enabled t (Counts M) →<br>                  (∃ ch,<br>                      deposit (Consumes t (Counts M)) (OutputSpec t) ch =<br>                        some (List.map Prod.fst e)) →<br>                    ∃ δ M', Q t M δ e M'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is one step Q t M δ e M' (glossary: QFireRel): t fires from the queue marking M, delivers δ to its action (the consumed tokens per input place and the read tokens per read place), the action emits e (places and tokens, in emission order) and the firing leaves M'. Queue order, Head, Emitted to and Appended at the tail are the glossary terms of GlossaryQueue.lean. Timing, priority and events play no part. Correlated ν inputs are not modelled (NU-020); ruling D16 excludes them from EXEC-010's oldest-first consumption, and line 2 does not say which tokens an input consumes, only that they are taken from the queue before the drain, so it makes no claim that D16 would contradict.
- e is the emission of a completion the executor admitted and output validation accepted ([EXEC-020], [EXEC-021], ruling D17); a discarded, timed-out, stopped or failed completion is not a step of Q. Tokens an action published before it completed ([IO-015]) are not modelled: they land after the drain and survive it, also when the completion then fails validation (ruling D17). Both are Phase 2 (RefExec).
- Every line is stated for transitions satisfying NoInputRead (ruling D15): a transition with an input arc and a read arc on one place is rejected when it is built (EXEC-012) and never fires. Under D15 no place is both an input place and a read place, so the 'input consumption, then read peeks' half of the fourth quoted sentence is not observable in a single firing: a read place is untouched by input consumption, and the read sees its pre-firing head whichever comes first. That half is therefore not a line; the halves 'input consumption, then reset draining' and 'read peeks, then reset draining' are lines 2 and 3.
- Line 1 is the first quoted sentence and the single-firing consequence of the second and third: the drain comes before the action's outputs land, so every token the action emits to a reset place survives it, and the place ends with exactly those tokens, in emission order. A drain after the action's outputs would leave the place empty (exec013_outputsFirst violates it). The other half of 'before its action starts', that the drained marking is the intermediate marking other transitions see while the action runs (the unquoted body sentence 'Like input consumption, the drain lands in the intermediate marking, so it restarts the clocks of transitions it disables there'), is TIME-012's and the Phase 2 executor model's (RefExec), not a line here. On token counts line 1 is CORE-034's first line.
- Line 2 is the fourth quoted sentence's 'input consumption, then reset draining': an input place that is also a reset place hands the action as many tokens as the input arc's consumption count (IO-007) on the pre-firing counts, taken from its pre-firing queue in queue order; a drain first would hand it nothing (exec013_drainFirst violates it). Which tokens they are (the oldest, except for a correlated ν input) is EXEC-010's subject; the queue an input place that is not a reset place keeps, and the tail append of the emitted tokens, are EXEC-010's and EXEC-020's. What is delivered from a place that is not an input place (for instance the drained tokens of a reset place that is not an input place) is not constrained by this block, nor by any other requirement block: the spec has no shall sentence on it, so that the action receives nothing from such a place is not encoded.
- Line 3 is the fifth quoted sentence, which is the 'read peeks, then reset draining' half of the fourth: the read token of a place that is both a read place and a reset place is the head of its queue before the drain. The read bag is nonempty and every token in it is that head; how many copies a transition with several read arcs on one place receives is CORE-032's and EXEC-012's subject and is not fixed here.
- Line 4 is the sixth quoted sentence's 'the firing shall proceed': an unmarked reset place blocks no firing, so every firing the counts allow (enabled, an offered deposit, glossary Enabled, Consumes and OutputSpec) is a firing of Q. Its 'the drain shall remove nothing' is line 1 at an unmarked reset place: the place ends with exactly the emitted tokens, which is the empty queue followed by them (EXEC_013_unmarked_removes_nothing).
- The body sentence 'A token a synchronous action deposited earlier in the same firing pass survives the drain ([EXEC-003] AC5).' is about a firing pass of several firings; a single queue firing has no earlier firing, so it is EXEC-003's subject and the Phase 2 cycle relation's, not encoded.
- AC1 (reset place with 5 tokens, all removed) is line 1 at a reset place that is not an output place: it ends empty (EXEC_013_nonvacuous, the five-token place 3). On token counts it is CORE-034's first line (cited on CORE_034_reference).
- AC2 (reset place empty, no error) is line 4 and line 1 at an unmarked reset place (EXEC_013_nonvacuous). On token counts it is CORE-034's second line: a reset arc does not affect whether its transition can fire (cited on CORE_034_reference).
- AC3 (the reset place is also an input or read place of another enabled transition, whose clock restarts) is read in TIME-012's form and is not encoded here: the queue firing has no clocks. TIME-012 says the other transition loses its clock in the intermediate marking and is newly armed with a fresh clock when it is enabled again; AC3's bare 'restarts' is read as that, not as a restart of a transition that the drain leaves disabled. The evidence is TIME-012's AC3 example TIME_012_ac3 (cited there).
- AC4 (reset draining after read peeking; a read arc and a reset arc on one place observe the pre-reset front token; the overall order is input consumption, read peeks, reset draining) is lines 2 and 3; the mutants exec013_resetFirst (draining before the peek) and exec013_drainFirst (draining before input consumption) are killed by lines 3 and 2.
- The test derivation's clock-restart check is AC3's and is TIME-012's subject.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): The reference queue firing satisfies EXEC-013: `qFire` drains the reset places after the input step and before it appends the emitted tokens (`refQFire_apply`), the delivered inputs are a prefix of the pre-firing queue of the consumed length (`exec013_qInputs`), the read bag is taken before the drain (`qReads` reads `qPostInput`, not `qConsume`), and `RefQFire` asks only for enablement and an offered deposit on the counts, which a reset arc does not affect.
  <pre>∀ {N T : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], EXEC_013 RefQFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_forbids_noDrain` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): ✗ A firing that does not drain violates EXEC-013 (line 1): firing `exec013_t` leaves the five tokens of the reset place `3` in place.
  <pre>¬EXEC_013 exec013_noDrain</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_mutant_drainFirst_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): The firing that drains before it consumes kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-013. Firing `exec013_t` hands the action no token from the input and reset place `0`, whose `one()` input arc consumes one (AC4, 'input consumption first').
  <pre>EXEC_013_mutant_drainFirst exec013_drainFirst ∧ ¬EXEC_013 exec013_drainFirst</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_mutant_outputsFirst_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): The firing that deposits first kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-013. Firing `exec013_t` emits `99` to the reset place `0`, and the drain that follows removes it, so place `0` ends empty instead of holding `99`.
  <pre>EXEC_013_mutant_outputsFirst exec013_outputsFirst ∧ ¬EXEC_013 exec013_outputsFirst</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_mutant_resetFirst_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): The firing that drains before it peeks kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not EXEC-013. Firing `exec013_t` hands the action no read token from place `1`, which held `20` before the drain (AC4).
  <pre>EXEC_013_mutant_resetFirst exec013_resetFirst ∧ ¬EXEC_013 exec013_resetFirst</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_013_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_013): Every line of EXEC-013 is exercised by the reference, firing `exec013_t` (a `one()` input on `0`; a read on `1`; resets on `0`, `1`, `3`, `4`) from `exec013_M` with emission `99` to place `0`: the action receives the token `10` from the queue `10, 11, 12` of the input and reset place `0` (line 2, input before drain) and the pre-drain head `20` of the read and reset place `1` (line 3, AC4); place `0` ends with the emitted `99` only (line 1, outputs after the drain), place `1` and the five-token place `3` end empty (line 1, AC1), the unmarked reset place `4` ends empty, the drain removing nothing (line 1, AC2), and the firing exists although `4` is unmarked (line 4, AC2).
  <pre>RefQFire exec013_t exec013_M (qDelivered exec013_M exec013_t) exec013_e<br>    (qFire exec013_M exec013_t exec013_e) ∧<br>  (qDelivered exec013_M exec013_t).inputs 0 = [10] ∧<br>    (qDelivered exec013_M exec013_t).reads 1 = [20] ∧<br>      qFire exec013_M exec013_t exec013_e 0 = [99] ∧<br>        qFire exec013_M exec013_t exec013_e 1 = [] ∧<br>          exec013_M 3 = [30, 31, 32, 33, 34] ∧<br>            qFire exec013_M exec013_t exec013_e 3 = [] ∧<br>              exec013_M 4 = [] ∧<br>                qFire exec013_M exec013_t exec013_e 4 = [] ∧ NoInputRead exec013_t</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_queue` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyQueue): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002, the TIME blocks and the queue blocks EXEC-010, EXEC-012, EXEC-013 and EXEC-020 hold together on one model: the model of `spec_consistent_coreIO_time`, with one builder for the IO build lines and EXEC-012's, and a queue firing relation whose firings, counted, are the firings of the same firing relation `F`, and which lifts every firing of `F`. The witnesses are the reference builder, the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order, the timed reference step and the reference queue firing.
  <pre>∀ {N Tk : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R QF,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>
