<a id="Libpetri-Spec-EXEC_020"></a>
## EXEC-020: Output Token Deposition

`Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/04-execution-model.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `0a4f620cf1f188a0`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| Each token is added to the end of the target place's FIFO queue, in the order the action produced it.<br><br>*When* the executor admits the completion of an action and output validation does not reject the produced tokens ([EXEC-021], [CONC-026]), the executor *shall* add each produced token at the tail of its place's queue, in the order the action produced the tokens. | 1. For every queue firing of Q, of a transition t from the queue marking M, delivering δ, emitting e and leaving M', there is a queue marking base (the queues the firing leaves before its deposit) such that:<br>2. every place p holds after the firing the queue base holds at p, followed by the tokens e emits to p, in emission order (AppendedAtTail base M' e p);<br>3. on every place p that is neither an input place nor a reset place of t, base holds the queue M held at p;<br>4. on every input place p of t that is not a reset place, base holds at p tokens M held at p, in the order M held them (a sublist of M's queue at p). | <pre>fun {N T} [DecidableEq N] Q ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (M : QueueMarking N T) (δ : Delivered N T) (e : List (N × T))<br>    (M' : QueueMarking N T),<br>    Q t M δ e M' →<br>      ∃ base,<br>        (∀ (p : N), AppendedAtTail base M' e p) ∧<br>          (∀ p ∉ inPlaces t, ¬ResetArc t p → base p = M p) ∧<br>            ∀ p ∈ inPlaces t, ¬ResetArc t p → (base p).Sublist (M p)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A queue firing relates a transition, the queue marking it fires from, what it delivers to its action, the tokens the action emits (places and tokens, in the order the action produced them) and the queue marking it leaves (glossary: QFireRel, Queue order). The firing is atomic: the step that starts it deposits its emission.
- Phase 1 has no completion and no admission. Every firing of the reference queue relation (RefQFire) deposits an emission whose places are an offered deposit of the output spec, so it stands for an admitted completion whose produced tokens pass output validation, which is the trigger of the quoted 'shall' sentence. Which completions the executor admits (not one whose firing ended with its timeout outcome, [EXEC-022] and [IO-013]; not one a stop on a terminal place abandoned, [EXEC-042]; not one an immediate termination abandoned, [ENV-015]; every in-flight one a close waits for, [ENV-013]) is the executor model of Phase 2 (RefExec) and is deferred to it, as is the moment of admission ([EXEC-001] step 1, or the firing pass for a synchronous action).
- The sentence 'If the completion of an action is not admitted, or output validation rejects the produced tokens, then the executor shall not deposit any token the action produced at completion.' is deferred to Phase 2: it speaks of completions that are not admitted and of a validation that fails, neither of which is a firing of the atomic Phase 1 relation. The joint model's QProjectsTo Q RefFire excludes every step whose emitted places are not an offered deposit, so at Phase 1 grain no step deposits an emission validation would reject; it is not a line of this block. It does not model the rejected completion itself: a firing that consumes, fails validation and deposits nothing (TransitionFailed, [EXEC-021], [EVT-008]) is not a step of the atomic relation, and neither is a firing of an executor with the validation skip of [CONC-026], which deposits a write that is not an offered deposit. Both are Phase 2 (RefExec).
- Tokens an action published before it completed ([IO-015]) are not modelled: the Phase 1 action emits only at the end of the firing. That they are not deposited again and survive a failed or abandoned completion is deferred to Phase 2.
- Line 1 is the quoted sentences: the emitted tokens are the tail of each queue, in emission order. Lines 2 and 3 say what 'its place's queue' is, the queue the deposit goes after: on a place the firing does not consume from or reset, the tokens already there (line 2); on an input place that is not reset, tokens the place held, kept in queue order (line 3, glossary Queue order: 'The tokens of a place are kept in the order they were added'). Line 3 does not say which tokens the firing consumes: oldest first is [EXEC-010], and a correlated input of a ν-net match ([NU-020], ruling D16) may take other tokens, which also leaves a sublist; the exact queue left is [EXEC-010] and [EXEC-013]. On a reset place base is unconstrained here; [EXEC-013], [CORE-034] and the count guard QProjectsTo Q RefFire make it empty. Without line 3 an executor that reorders the tokens left on an input place it also deposits to would satisfy the block (EXEC_020_mutant_restOrder_killed).
- AC1 (an action produces V to P; V appears at the end of P's queue) is line 1 with line 2: the nonvacuous example EXEC_020_nonvacuous deposits 30 to a place already holding 40 and leaves 40 then 30.
- AC2 (multiple tokens to the same place are added in order) is line 1 for an emission with several tokens to one place. The reference queue firing deposits one token per claimed place on an action outcome (Reference.deposit, as every branch-enumerating analysis, [IO-016]), and the count guard QProjectsTo Q RefFire imposes the same on every Q, so the joint model has no step with two tokens to one place until the projection compares places as a set, as [IO-015] does. AC2 is therefore witnessed outside the joint model, by executors that accept several tokens into one claimed place under that set comparison: the in-order one satisfies EXEC-020 and leaves 40, 30, 31 when 30 then 31 go to a place holding 40 (EXEC_020_allows_several), and the reverse-order one violates it (EXEC_020_forbids_reversed).
- AC3 (a completion that is not admitted deposits none of the action's tokens) and AC4 (a completion whose produced tokens fail validation deposits none of them) are the deferred sentence above, Phase 2.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): The reference satisfies EXEC-020: its result is the reference consume (`qConsume`) with the emission appended one token at a time at the tail (`qAppend_apply`), and the consume leaves a place without input or reset arc as it was and an input place without reset arc a sublist.
  <pre>∀ {N T : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], EXEC_020 RefQFire</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_allows_several` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): ✓ Acceptance criterion 2 outside the joint model: the executor that writes several tokens into one claimed place, in emission order, satisfies EXEC-020; firing `qexT .one` from `exec020M` with `30` then `31` emitted to place 2, which holds `40`, it leaves `40, 30, 31` there.
  <pre>EXEC_020 exec020_severalFire ∧<br>  exec020_severalFire (qexT Card.one) exec020M (qDelivered exec020M (qexT Card.one))<br>      [(2, 30), (2, 31)]<br>      (qAppend (qConsume exec020M (qexT Card.one)) [(2, 30), (2, 31)]) ∧<br>    qAppend (qConsume exec020M (qexT Card.one)) [(2, 30), (2, 31)] 2 = [40, 30, 31]</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_forbids_prepend` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): ✗ Depositing at the head violates EXEC-020: firing `qexT .one` from `exec020M` with `30` emitted to place 2 leaves `[30, 40]` there, which no queue followed by `[30]` is.
  <pre>¬EXEC_020 exec020_prependFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_forbids_reversed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): ✗ Depositing in reverse emission order violates EXEC-020: firing `qexT .one` from `exec020M` with `30` then `31` emitted to place 2 (one claimed place, two tokens, [IO-015]) leaves `[40, 31, 30]` there, which no queue followed by `[30, 31]` is (AC2).
  <pre>¬EXEC_020 exec020_reversedFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_mutant_prepend_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): The head-depositing executor kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading (with the reference consume as `base`) but not EXEC-020.
  <pre>EXEC_020_mutant_prepend exec020_prependFire ∧ ¬EXEC_020 exec020_prependFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_mutant_restOrder_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): The self-loop reordering executor kills the third mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading (lines 1 and 2) but not EXEC-020. Firing `exec020_selfT` from `exec020_selfM` with `4` emitted to place 0, it leaves `3, 2, 4` there, where the reference leaves `2, 3, 4`; no sublist of `1, 2, 3` followed by `4` is `3, 2, 4`.
  <pre>EXEC_020_mutant_restOrder exec020_restReversedFire ∧ ¬EXEC_020 exec020_restReversedFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_mutant_reversed_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): The reverse-order executor kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading (with the reference consume as `base`) but not EXEC-020.
  <pre>EXEC_020_mutant_reversed exec020_reversedFire ∧ ¬EXEC_020 exec020_reversedFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.EXEC_020_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.EXEC_020): EXEC-020 is exercised by the reference (AC1): `qexT .one` fires from `exec020M`, where its output place 2 already holds `40`, its action emitting `30` there; the firing leaves `40` then `30` in place 2, where depositing at the head would leave `30` then `40`.
  <pre>RefQFire (qexT Card.one) exec020M (qDelivered exec020M (qexT Card.one)) [(2, 30)]<br>    exec020_after ∧<br>  exec020M 2 = [40] ∧<br>    exec020_after 2 = [40, 30] ∧ exec020_after 2 ≠ EmittedTo [(2, 30)] 2 ++ exec020M 2</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_queue` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyQueue): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002, the TIME blocks and the queue blocks EXEC-010, EXEC-012, EXEC-013 and EXEC-020 hold together on one model: the model of `spec_consistent_coreIO_time`, with one builder for the IO build lines and EXEC-012's, and a queue firing relation whose firings, counted, are the firings of the same firing relation `F`, and which lifts every firing of `F`. The witnesses are the reference builder, the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order, the timed reference step and the reference queue firing.
  <pre>∀ {N Tk : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R QF,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br> ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br> <a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_001"></a>
## IO-001: Input One

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_001` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `4d11a44d62674654`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `One(place)` is an input arc whose required count and consumption count are both 1.<br><br>*While* the input place of a `One` input arc is unmarked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* the input place of a `One` input arc is marked, the arc *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire.<br><br>*When* a transition with a `One` input arc fires, it *shall* consume exactly 1 token from the input place.<br><br>*When* a transition with a `One` input arc fires, and the input place is neither a reset place nor an output place of the transition, the input place *shall* end the firing holding exactly 1 fewer token than it held before the firing. | 1. For every transition t and place p such that the input arc of t on p is One:<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p is unmarked;<br>3. from a marking in which p is marked, t can fire exactly when t without its input arc on p can fire;<br>4. unless p is also a reset place of t, every firing of t from m that deposits d and leaves m' has m'(p) + 1 = m(p) + d(p): p loses exactly 1 token, besides what the firing deposits there; and if p is not an output place of t either, m'(p) + 1 = m(p): p loses exactly 1 token, whatever deposit the firing reports. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    InputArc t p Card.one →<br>      (while fun m ↦ ¬Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>            Marked m p →<br>              ((∃ m', Fire F t m m') ↔ ∃ m', Fire F (io001_withoutInput t p) m m')) ∧<br>          (¬ResetArc t p →<br>            ∀ (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>              F t m d m' →<br>                m' p + 1 = m p + Deposits d p ∧ (¬OutputArc t p → m' p + 1 = m p))</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking with its deposit (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' and 'enables' are both read as 'some firing exists'.
- The first quoted sentence and the bullets (`requiredCount()` returns 1, `consumptionCount(available)` returns 1) are IO-007's contract for `One`. Here it is observed through firing: the required count is the second and third lines, the consumption count the fourth. The bullet values themselves are `io001_counts`.
- AC1 (enables when the place has >= 1 token) is the second line (not with 0 tokens) and the third line (with >= 1 token the arc does not stand in the way; whether the other arcs allow the firing is theirs to say, IO-005 and CORE-022).
- AC2 (exactly 1 token consumed on firing) is the fourth line for the count. Which token is consumed (the oldest) is below the count granularity of the firing semantics and is the glossary's reading of Consume (CORE-030); it is not encoded here.
- AC3 (remaining tokens stay in the place) is the fourth line: p holds m(p) - 1 tokens plus the firing's deposit there afterwards.
- Consumption is observed through the marking: p loses 1 and gains what the firing deposits there (p may also be an output place). A place that is both the input place and a reset place of t ends holding what the firing deposited there (CORE-034), so the consumed token is not visible in the marking; the fourth line excludes that case.
- The deposit a firing reports is not trusted for the count: on a place that t's output spec does not name (glossary: Output arc) no firing of t can deposit ([CORE-035]), so there the consumed token shows in the marking alone, as exactly 1 token fewer. Without this a firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit of 1 in the input place would pass the count.
- The input arc of t on p is the first input arc on p (glossary: Input arc), and t without its input arc on p drops that arc only; CORE-030 rejects a second input arc on p at build.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): The reference semantics satisfies IO-001: `Seam.enabledNA` requires the `One` place to hold `Card.required .one = 1` token, so `t` does not fire with it unmarked, and with it marked the arc's conjunct of `enabledNA` holds, so dropping the arc changes no enablement (and every output spec offers a deposit); `Seam.nconsumed` takes 1 from it, and `Seam.fireNC` of a non-reset place is "minus consumed, plus deposited", where the deposit is empty outside the output spec's places.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_001 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_forbids_ghost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): ✗ A firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit in its input place violates IO-001 (third clause, its second part): `io001_t` has no output place, fires from the marking with one token in place `0` and leaves that token there.
  <pre>¬IO_001 io001_ghostFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_forbids_idle` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): ✗ A firing that consumes nothing violates IO-001 (third clause): `io001_t` fires from the marking with one token in place `0` and leaves that token there.
  <pre>¬IO_001 io001_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_forbids_needsTwo` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): ✗ A `One` arc that needs 2 tokens violates IO-001 (second clause): with place `0` holding one token, `io001_t` cannot fire, but without its input arc it can.
  <pre>¬IO_001 io001_needsTwoFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_forbids_outputBypass` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): ✗ A `One` arc that does not guard its transition violates IO-001 (first clause): `io001_t2` fires from the empty marking, its place `0` being an output place.
  <pre>¬IO_001 io001_outputBypassFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_mutant_dropConsume_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): The idle firing kills the third mutant: it fires exactly where the reference does, so the first two clauses hold; but it leaves the token of `io001_t`'s input place where it is.
  <pre>IO_001_mutant_dropConsume io001_idleFire ∧ ¬IO_001 io001_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_mutant_dropMarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): The needs-two firing kills the second mutant: it is a reference firing restricted further, so it never fires with the place unmarked and consumes 1 token; but with one token in place `0` `io001_t` cannot fire, although without its input arc it can.
  <pre>IO_001_mutant_dropMarked io001_needsTwoFire ∧ ¬IO_001 io001_needsTwoFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_mutant_dropUnmarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): The output-bypass firing kills the first mutant: a transition fires exactly when its input places outside the output spec are marked, which a marked `One` place does not change, so the second clause holds; an input place outside the output spec loses one token and one in it loses one and gets it back, so the third does; but `io001_t2` fires from the empty marking.
  <pre>IO_001_mutant_dropUnmarked io001_outputBypassFire ∧ ¬IO_001 io001_outputBypassFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_mutant_trustDeposit_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): The ghost-deposit firing kills the fourth mutant: it fires exactly where the reference does, so the first two clauses hold, and the deposit of one token it reports in the input place makes up for the token it does not take; but `io001_t` has no output place, and its input place keeps its token.
  <pre>IO_001_mutant_trustDeposit io001_ghostFire ∧ ¬IO_001 io001_ghostFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_001_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_001): All three clauses of IO-001 are exercised by the reference semantics, on the test derivation's marking: `io001_t` has a `One` arc on place `0`; it does not fire from the empty marking (first clause); with one token in place `0` it fires, as it does without its input arc (second clause); and from 3 tokens it fires to 2 (third clause: 1 consumed, 2 remain).
  <pre>InputArc io001_t 0 Card.one ∧<br>  (¬∃ m', Fire RefFire io001_t io001_empty m') ∧<br>    Fire RefFire io001_t io001_one io001_empty ∧<br>      Fire RefFire (io001_withoutInput io001_t 0) io001_one io001_one ∧<br>        RefFire io001_t io001_three [] io001_two</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

The witnesses are the reference builder, the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order, the timed reference step and the reference queue firing.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_002"></a>
## IO-002: Input Exactly

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_002` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `0ad9dd7705e7e2fa`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `Exactly(place, n)` is an input arc whose required count and consumption count are both N, where N >= 1.<br><br>*While* the input place of an `Exactly(n)` input arc holds fewer than N tokens, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*When* a transition with an `Exactly(n)` input arc fires, it *shall* consume exactly N tokens from the input place.<br><br>*When* a transition with an `Exactly(n)` input arc fires, and the input place is neither a reset place nor an output place of the transition, the input place *shall* end the firing holding exactly N fewer tokens than it held before the firing. | 1. For every transition t, place p and count N such that the input arc of t on p is Exactly(N):<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p holds fewer than N tokens;<br>3. unless p is also a reset place of t, every firing of t from m that deposits d and leaves m' has m'(p) + N = m(p) + d(p): p loses exactly N tokens, besides what the firing deposits there; and if p is not an output place of t either, m'(p) + N = m(p): p loses exactly N tokens, whatever deposit the firing reports. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N) (n : ℕ),<br>    InputArc t p (Card.exactly n) →<br>      (while fun m ↦ TokenCount m p &lt; n, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (¬ResetArc t p →<br>          ∀ (m : N → ℕ) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            F t m d m' →<br>              m' p + n = m p + Deposits d p ∧ (¬OutputArc t p → m' p + n = m p))</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking with its deposit (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Enables' is read as 'some firing exists'.
- The first quoted sentence (required count N, consumption count N; the bullets `requiredCount()` returns N and `consumptionCount(available)` returns N) is IO-007's contract for `Exactly(n)`. Here it is observed through firing: the required count is the second line, the consumption count the third. The bullets' qualifier 'when available >= N' is implied: by the second line no firing starts with fewer.
- AC1 (construction with N < 1 rejected, also when the transition is built from a spec written out without the constructor): a property of construction, not of firing, so the formula does not encode it. The formula quantifies over every N, N = 0 included; for N = 0 both lines hold of the reference semantics trivially, so the restriction N >= 1 neither weakens nor strengthens what is checked about firing.
- AC2 (enables only when the place has >= N tokens) and AC4 (N-1 tokens, not enabled) are the second line. The converse ('enables when the place has >= N tokens', given the other arcs allow it) is the AND-join of IO-005 and CORE-022, not this block's subject.
- AC3 (exactly N consumed on firing, the N oldest, FIFO) is the third line for the count; its second part (the fourth quoted sentence) states it without the deposit for an input place that is neither a reset place nor an output place. Which tokens are consumed (the oldest, FIFO) is below the count granularity of the firing semantics and is the glossary's reading of Consume (CORE-030); it is not encoded here.
- Consumption is observed through the marking: p loses N and gains what the firing deposits there (p may also be an output place). A place that is both the input place and a reset place of t ends holding what the firing deposited there (CORE-034), so the N tokens consumed are not visible in the marking; the third line excludes that case, as the fourth quoted sentence does.
- The deposit a firing reports is not trusted for the count: on a place that t's output spec does not name (glossary: Output arc) no firing of t can deposit ([CORE-035]), so there the consumed tokens show in the marking alone, as exactly N tokens fewer (the fourth quoted sentence, IO-001's guard). Without this a firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit of N in the input place would pass the count, and the block would lean on CORE-035 to stand.
- Deposits are counts per place (glossary: Deposit, [CORE-036]): `Deposits d p` counts the tokens d puts in p and ignores their order. The build-time output-spec checks of IO-011 to IO-014 (ambiguity, one Timeout, ForwardInput only under a Timeout) and the validation skip of CONC-026 do not touch input consumption, so the formula quantifies over every transition, built or not, and every executor.
- The input arc of t on p is the first input arc on p (glossary: Input arc); CORE-030 rejects a second one at build.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): The reference semantics satisfies IO-002: `Seam.enabledNA` requires the input place of an `Exactly(n)` arc to hold `Card.required (.exactly n) = n` tokens, and `Seam.nconsumed` takes `n` from it, so a place that is not a reset place loses `n` and gains the deposit, which is empty outside the output spec's places.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_002 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_forbids_asAtLeast` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): ✗ Reading `Exactly(n)` as `AtLeast(n)` violates IO-002 (second clause): `io002_t` fires from 7 tokens and leaves place `0` empty, consuming 7, not 5.
  <pre>¬IO_002 io002_asAtLeastFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_forbids_asOne` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): ✗ Reading `Exactly(n)` as `One` violates IO-002 (first clause): `io002_t` fires from the marking in which place `0` holds 4 tokens, fewer than 5.
  <pre>¬IO_002 io002_asOneFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_forbids_ghost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): ✗ A firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit in its input place violates IO-002 (second clause, its second part): `io002_t` has no output place, fires from 7 tokens and leaves all 7 in place `0`.
  <pre>¬IO_002 io002_ghostFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_mutant_dropConsume_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): The idle firing kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant (only enabled transitions fire, so an `Exactly(n)` place holds at least `n`) but not IO-002 (`io002_t` fires from 7 tokens and place `0` keeps all 7).
  <pre>IO_002_mutant_dropConsume io002_idleFire ∧ ¬IO_002 io002_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_mutant_dropEnable_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): The returning firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (place `0` loses 5 and gains the 5 it deposits, and is an output place of `io002_t2`) but not IO-002 (`io002_t2` fires from 4 tokens). Without the first clause a place that is also an output place may be short.
  <pre>IO_002_mutant_dropEnable io002_returnsFire ∧ ¬IO_002 io002_returnsFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_mutant_trustDeposit_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): The ghost-deposit firing kills the third mutant: it fires only when enabled, so the first clause holds, and the 5 tokens it reports in place `0` make up for the 5 it does not take; but `io002_t` has no output place, and place `0` keeps its tokens.
  <pre>IO_002_mutant_trustDeposit io002_ghostFire ∧ ¬IO_002 io002_ghostFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_002_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_002): Both clauses of IO-002 are exercised by the reference semantics, as in the test derivation: `io002_t` has the input arc `Exactly(5)` on place `0`; it does not fire from 4 tokens (first clause); and it fires from 7 tokens, leaving 2, with place `0` not an output place (second clause, both parts).
  <pre>InputArc io002_t 0 (Card.exactly 5) ∧<br>  ¬ResetArc io002_t 0 ∧<br>    ¬OutputArc io002_t 0 ∧<br>      (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire RefFire io002_t io002_m4 m') ∧<br>        Fire RefFire io002_t io002_m7 fun q ↦ if q = 0 then 2 else 7</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_003"></a>
## IO-003: Input All

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_003` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `5379451b947b7819`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* the place of an `All` input arc of a transition is unmarked, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* the place of an `All` input arc of a transition is marked, the `All` input arc *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, it *shall* consume every token from the place of each of its `All` input arcs.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, the place of each of its `All` input arcs that is not an output place of the transition *shall* end the firing empty. | 1. For every transition t and every place p on which t has an `All` input arc:<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p is unmarked;<br>3. from a marking in which p is marked, t can fire exactly when t without its `All` input arcs on p can fire;<br>4. every firing of t that deposits d leaves p holding exactly the tokens d puts there;<br>5. if p is not an output place of t, every firing of t leaves p empty, whatever deposit the firing reports. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    AllArc t p →<br>      (while fun m ↦ ¬Marked m p, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>            Marked m p → ((∃ m', Fire F t m m') ↔ ∃ m', Fire F (withoutAll t p) m m')) ∧<br>          (∀ (d : List N),<br>              on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ m' p = Deposits d p) ∧<br>            (¬OutputArc t p →<br>              ∀ (d : List N), on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ m' p = 0)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' is read as 'some firing to some marking exists'.
- The first two lines together say that the `All` arc requires exactly one token: `requiredCount()` returns 1 (the first bullet; `Card.required .all = 1`).
- 'Consume every token' is read through the place's count after the firing: p ends holding only what this firing deposited in p, so it kept none of its own tokens. This is the second bullet, `consumptionCount(available)` returns `available`. A firing that also deposits into p (p is an output place of t) leaves those tokens in p.
- AC1 (enables when the place has >= 1 token) is the second line: with p marked the `All` arc removes no firing. AC3 (0 tokens, not enabled) is the first line. AC2 (all tokens consumed, place empty after) is the third line, with 'empty' read as 'empty apart from this firing's own deposit into p', and the fourth line, where p is not an output place and 'empty' is literal.
- The fourth line is the guard IO-001 also carries: it does not trust the deposit a firing reports. On a place that t's output spec does not name (glossary: Output arc) no firing can deposit (CORE-035, CORE-063), so there the drain shows in the marking alone, as an empty place. Without it, a firing that keeps p's tokens and reports them as its own deposit into p would pass the third line. The fourth line stands alone: it needs neither the third line nor CORE-035.
- A reset arc on p does not weaken the fourth line: the spec sentence has no reset exclusion, and a reset place that is not an output place ends holding what the firing deposited there (CORE-034), which is nothing.
- Deposits are compared per place (CORE-036): the lines read the count `Deposits d p` and the marking's count at p, never the order of d.
- Which token is consumed first, and that the consumed tokens reach the action, are below the count granularity of a firing relation; they are not encoded.
- A transition with two input arcs on p is rejected at build by CORE-030. On such a transition the formula reads the first arc on p, as the reference does (`InputArc`); `withoutAll` removes only the `All` arcs on p.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): The reference semantics satisfies IO-003: `Seam.enabledNA` asks `Card.required .all = 1` token of an `All` place, so the transition does not fire while it is unmarked and the arc asks nothing more while it is marked; `Seam.nconsumed` takes every token of an `All` place, so the place ends with only what the firing deposited there (`refFire_apply`); and a reference firing deposits only in output places, so an `All` place that is not one ends empty.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_003 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_forbids_allAsOne` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): ✗ Reading `All` as `One` violates IO-003 (third clause): from five tokens `io003_t` consumes one and leaves four, although it deposits nothing in place `0`.
  <pre>¬IO_003 io003_allAsOneFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_forbids_phantomDeposit` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): ✗ A reported deposit in a place the output spec does not name violates IO-003 (fourth clause): `io003_t` has no output place, yet its firing from five tokens leaves one token in its `All` place `0`. The third clause alone accepts it, because the firing reports that token as its own deposit.
  <pre>¬IO_003 io003_phantomFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_forbids_requiresNothing` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): ✗ An `All` arc that asks for no token violates IO-003 (first clause): `io003_t` fires from the empty marking.
  <pre>¬IO_003 io003_requiresNothingFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_mutant_dropDrain_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): The net-emptying firing of enabled transitions kills the third mutant: it fires exactly the enabled transitions (first and second clauses) and leaves every place empty (fourth clause), but when it reports a deposit of one token in place `0`, place `0` does not hold it (the third clause fails).
  <pre>IO_003_mutant_dropDrain io003_drainAllFire ∧ ¬IO_003 io003_drainAllFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_mutant_dropEmpty_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): The phantom-deposit firing kills the fourth mutant: it fires exactly the enabled transitions (first and second clauses) and every place holds what the reported deposit puts there (third clause), but `io003_t`, which has no output place, leaves a token in its `All` place (the fourth clause fails).
  <pre>IO_003_mutant_dropEmpty io003_phantomFire ∧ ¬IO_003 io003_phantomFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_mutant_dropMarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): The always-blocking firing kills the second mutant: a transition with an `All` arc never fires, so the first, third and fourth clauses hold vacuously; but from five tokens `io003_t` does not fire although without its `All` arc it does (the second clause fails).
  <pre>IO_003_mutant_dropMarked io003_allBlocksFire ∧ ¬IO_003 io003_allBlocksFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_mutant_dropUnmarked_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): The wiping firing kills the first mutant: whether a transition fires does not depend on its arcs, and every place ends empty (second, third and fourth clauses hold), but `io003_t` fires from the empty marking (the first clause fails).
  <pre>IO_003_mutant_dropUnmarked io003_wipeFire ∧ ¬IO_003 io003_wipeFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_003_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_003): All four clauses of IO-003 are exercised by the reference semantics: `io003_t` has an `All` arc on place `0`, which is not an output place; it does not fire from the empty marking (first clause); from five tokens it fires, as it does without its `All` arc (second clause); and that firing deposits nothing and leaves place `0` empty while the other places keep their tokens (third and fourth clauses, the test derivation's five tokens drained).
  <pre>AllArc io003_t 0 ∧<br>  ¬OutputArc io003_t 0 ∧<br>    ¬Fire RefFire io003_t io003_empty io003_empty ∧<br>      RefFire io003_t io003_five [] io003_drained ∧<br>        Fire RefFire (withoutAll io003_t 0) io003_five io003_five</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_004"></a>
## IO-004: Input AtLeast

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_004` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `8a1fed506443b2d2`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* the input place of an `AtLeast(place, minimum)` input arc holds fewer than `minimum` tokens, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* the input place of an `AtLeast(place, minimum)` input arc holds at least `minimum` tokens, the input arc *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire.<br><br>*When* a transition with an `AtLeast(place, minimum)` input arc fires, it *shall* consume every token the input place holds.<br><br>*When* a transition with an `AtLeast(place, minimum)` input arc fires, and the input place is not an output place of the transition, the input place *shall* end the firing empty. | 1. For every transition t, place p and number n such that the input arc of t on p is AtLeast(p, n):<br>2. t does not fire from a marking in which p holds fewer than n tokens;<br>3. from a marking m in which p holds at least n tokens, t can fire from m exactly when t without its input arc on p can fire from m;<br>4. every firing of t, with whatever deposit d, leaves p holding exactly the tokens d deposited there (every token p held was consumed);<br>5. if p is not an output place of t, every firing of t leaves p empty, whatever deposit the firing reports. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N) (n : ℕ),<br>    InputArc t p (Card.atLeast n) →<br>      (while fun m ↦ TokenCount m p &lt; n, never Fire F t) ∧<br>        (∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>            n ≤ TokenCount m p →<br>              ((∃ m', Fire F t m m') ↔ ∃ m', Fire F (withoutInputArc t p) m m')) ∧<br>          (∀ (d : List N),<br>              on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ TokenCount m' p = Deposits d p) ∧<br>            (¬OutputArc t p →<br>              ∀ (d : List N),<br>                on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x m' ↦ TokenCount m' p = 0)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' is read as 'some firing from m exists', whatever marking it leads to: removing the arc changes what the firing consumes from p, so only whether t fires is compared, not where it leads.
- 'The input arc on p' is the first input arc on p, as the glossary term Input arc reads it; `withoutInputArc` removes that one arc. [CORE-030] allows at most one input arc per place, so on a well-formed transition it is the only one.
- AC1 (construction with minimum < 1 is rejected): not encoded. It is a build-time check on the net definition, outside the firing relation. The statement quantifies over every n, n = 0 included; the reference semantics satisfies it there too (AtLeast(p, 0) never blocks and drains p).
- AC2 (transition enables when the place has >= minimum tokens) is the second line: with p holding at least n tokens the arc does not stop t from firing. Read with [IO-005], the other arcs of t still decide.
- AC3 (all tokens consumed on firing, not just minimum) is the third line, stated on the marking the firing leaves: p ends holding what was deposited there and nothing it held before. A reset arc on p ([CORE-034]) gives the same end count, so neither the third nor the fourth line needs a reset guard.
- The fourth line is the guard IO-001 carries, so the block stands alone: the deposit a firing reports is not trusted for the count. On a place the output spec of t does not name, no firing of t can deposit ([CORE-035]), so the drain shows in the marking alone, as an empty place. Without it a firing that consumes nothing but reports a deposit in p equal to what p held would pass the third line.
- AC4 (place with minimum - 1 tokens, transition not enabled) is the first line, at the count minimum - 1.
- The two bullets (`requiredCount()` returns `minimum`, `consumptionCount(available)` returns `available`) are the pure-function contract of [IO-007] and the glossary terms Required count and Consumption count; this block encodes their effect on firing (lines 1 to 4), not the functions themselves.
- The output-spec build checks ([IO-011] to [IO-014]) and the output validation skip ([CONC-026]) do not touch input consumption; the block holds of every firing relation over every transition, built or not.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): The reference semantics satisfies IO-004: `Seam.enabledNA` requires `requiredCount` = `n` tokens in `p` (first line); with `p` holding that many, the arc's test passes, so removing it leaves enablement unchanged, and whether the output spec offers an outcome does not depend on consumption (second line); `Seam.nconsumed` takes every token of an `AtLeast` place, so `p` ends with its deposit (third line); and a reference firing deposits only in places the output spec names, so outside them that deposit is empty and `p` ends empty (fourth line).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_004 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_forbids_ghost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): ✗ A firing that reports a deposit in an input place its output spec does not name violates IO-004 (fourth line): `io004_t` has no output place, fires from 7 tokens in place `0` and leaves 1 token there, the one it claims to have deposited.
  <pre>¬IO_004 io004_ghostFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_forbids_ignoresMinimum` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): ✗ Ignoring the minimum violates IO-004 (first line): `io004_t` fires while place `0` holds 2 of its 3 required tokens.
  <pre>¬IO_004 io004_ignoresMinimumFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_forbids_takesMinimum` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): ✗ Taking only the minimum violates IO-004 (third line), the test derivation's case: `io004_t` fires from 7 tokens in place `0`, takes 3 and leaves 4.
  <pre>¬IO_004 io004_takesMinimumFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_mutant_dropDrain_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): The keep-output firing kills the third mutant: it satisfies the mutant (it fires exactly when the reference enablement says so, and empties an `AtLeast` place that is not an output place) but not IO-004 (`io004_t2` fires from 7 tokens, deposits nothing and leaves all 7 in place `0`, its output place).
  <pre>IO_004_mutant_dropDrain io004_keepOutputFire ∧ ¬IO_004 io004_keepOutputFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_mutant_dropEmpty_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): The ghost-deposit firing kills the fourth mutant: it satisfies the mutant (it fires exactly when the reference enablement says so, and every place ends with what the firing reports as deposited there) but not IO-004 (`IO_004_forbids_ghost`).
  <pre>IO_004_mutant_dropEmpty io004_ghostFire ∧ ¬IO_004 io004_ghostFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_mutant_dropEnables_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): The input-free firing kills the second mutant: it satisfies the mutant (a transition with an `AtLeast` arc never fires) but not IO-004 (`io004_t` does not fire from 7 tokens, although it fires there without its input arc).
  <pre>IO_004_mutant_dropEnables io004_inputFreeFire ∧ ¬IO_004 io004_inputFreeFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_mutant_dropMinimum_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): The drain-everything firing kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (every transition fires, and every place ends empty with nothing deposited) but not IO-004 (`io004_t` fires while place `0` holds 2 tokens).
  <pre>IO_004_mutant_dropMinimum io004_drainEverythingFire ∧ ¬IO_004 io004_drainEverythingFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_004_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_004): All four lines of IO-004 are exercised by the reference semantics: `io004_t` has the input arc `AtLeast(0, 3)`; it does not fire from 2 tokens in place `0` (first line); it fires from 7 tokens, as it does there without its input arc (second line); that firing drains all 7, not just the minimum 3, and place `0`, which is not an output place, ends empty (third and fourth lines); and `io004_t2`, whose `AtLeast` place `0` is also its output place, fires from 7 tokens and leaves exactly the 1 token it deposits there (third line, with the fourth not applying).
  <pre>InputArc io004_t 0 (Card.atLeast 3) ∧<br>  (¬∃ m', Fire RefFire io004_t io004_two m') ∧<br>    Fire RefFire io004_t io004_seven io004_drained ∧<br>      Fire RefFire (withoutInputArc io004_t 0) io004_seven io004_seven ∧<br>        ¬OutputArc io004_t 0 ∧<br>          io004_drained 0 = 0 ∧<br>            InputArc io004_t2 0 (Card.atLeast 3) ∧<br>              OutputArc io004_t2 0 ∧<br>                RefFire io004_t2 io004_seven [0] io004_refilled ∧<br>                  io004_refilled 0 = Deposits [0] 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_005"></a>
## IO-005: Input AND-Join Semantics

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_005` · Kind **B** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `0475383de59e3f53`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* any input place of a transition holds fewer tokens than the required count of its input arc, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* every input place of a transition holds at least the required count of its input arc, the input arcs *shall not* affect whether the transition can fire. | 1. For every transition t:<br>2. for every input arc s of t, t does not fire from a marking in which the input place of s holds fewer tokens than the required count of s;<br>3. from a marking m in which every input place of t holds at least the required count of its input arc, t can fire from m exactly when t without its input arcs can fire from m. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N),<br>    (∀ s ∈ t.core.inputs,<br>        while fun m ↦ TokenCount m s.place &lt; requiredCount s.card, never Fire F t) ∧<br>      ∀ (m : CountMarking N),<br>        (∀ s ∈ t.core.inputs, requiredCount s.card ≤ TokenCount m s.place) →<br>          ((∃ m', Fire F t m m') ↔ ∃ m', Fire F (withoutInputs t) m m')</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking (glossary: Fire); timing and priority play no part. 'Can fire' and 'enabled' are both read as 'some firing exists' (to some marking): the input arcs change what a firing consumes, so the second line compares whether t fires, not where it leads.
- 'Simultaneously satisfied' is read as 'satisfied in the one marking the firing starts from': both lines fix a single marking m.
- Input arcs are read from the transition's list of input arcs, every one of them. A transition with two input arcs on one place, which [CORE-030] rejects at build, is gated by both.
- AC1 (a transition with 3 inputs enables only when all 3 places have sufficient tokens) is the first line, for any number of input arcs, 3 included; the test derivation's 'satisfy all 3; verify enabled' is the second line together with a transition that, without its input arcs, can fire (no read or inhibitor arc blocks it).
- AC2 (any one input unsatisfied disables the transition) is the first line.
- How many tokens an input arc requires (requiredCount) is IO-001 to IO-004 and IO-007, not this block: the statement takes requiredCount as given.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): The reference semantics satisfies IO-005: `Seam.enabledNA` requires every input arc's required count, so an unsatisfied input arc disables the transition; with every input arc satisfied the remaining conditions (read and inhibitor arcs) are those of the transition without its input arcs, and an enabled transition can always fire.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_005 RefFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_forbids_inputsAlwaysBlock` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): ✗ Input arcs that block their transition although all of them are satisfied violate IO-005 (second clause): `io005_t` cannot fire from `io005_all`, but without its input arcs it can.
  <pre>¬IO_005 io005_inputsAlwaysBlockFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_forbids_orJoin` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): ✗ An OR-join violates IO-005 (first clause): `io005_t` fires with two of its three input arcs satisfied.
  <pre>¬IO_005 io005_orJoinFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_mutant_dropSatisfied_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): The always-blocking input arcs kill the second mutant: they satisfy the mutant (a transition with an input arc never fires) but not IO-005.
  <pre>IO_005_mutant_dropSatisfied io005_inputsAlwaysBlockFire ∧<br>  ¬IO_005 io005_inputsAlwaysBlockFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_mutant_dropUnsatisfied_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): The OR-join kills the first mutant: it satisfies the mutant (with every input arc satisfied, the OR-join's condition holds and the firing is that of the transition without its input arcs) but not IO-005.
  <pre>IO_005_mutant_dropUnsatisfied io005_orJoinFire ∧ ¬IO_005 io005_orJoinFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_005_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_005): Both clauses of IO-005 are exercised by the reference semantics on AC1's transition: with two of its three input arcs satisfied `io005_t` does not fire (first clause, place `2` short of its required count), although without its input arcs it does; with all three satisfied it fires, as it does without its input arcs (second clause).
  <pre>io005_twoOfThree 2 &lt; requiredCount Card.all ∧<br>  (∀ (m' : ℕ → ℕ), ¬Fire RefFire io005_t io005_twoOfThree m') ∧<br>    Fire RefFire (withoutInputs io005_t) io005_twoOfThree<br>        (io005_after (withoutInputs io005_t) io005_twoOfThree) ∧<br>      (∀ s ∈ io005_t.core.inputs, requiredCount s.card ≤ io005_all s.place) ∧<br>        Fire RefFire io005_t io005_all (io005_after io005_t io005_all) ∧<br>          Fire RefFire (withoutInputs io005_t) io005_all<br>            (io005_after (withoutInputs io005_t) io005_all)</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_007"></a>
## IO-007: requiredCount and consumptionCount Contract

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_007` · Kind **F** · Scope all input cardinalities · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `1860eb65b8a890ff`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| The required count *shall* be 1 for `One` and `All`, `n` for `Exactly(n)` and `m` for `AtLeast(m)`.<br><br>*While* `available` is at least the required count, `consumptionCount(available)` *shall* return 1 for `One`, `n` for `Exactly(n)` and `available` for `All` and `AtLeast(m)`.<br><br>*If* `consumptionCount(available)` is called with `available` less than the required count, *then* the call *shall* fail with an error. | 1. requiredCount() is 1 for One, n for Exactly(n), 1 for All and m for AtLeast(m);<br>2. for every cardinality c and every available ≥ requiredCount(c), consumptionCount(available) returns 1 for One, n for Exactly(n), available for All and available for AtLeast(m);<br>3. for every cardinality c and every available &lt; requiredCount(c), consumptionCount(available) fails (none). | <pre>fun K ↦<br>  (∀ (c : Card),<br>      K.required c =<br>        match c with<br>        \| Card.one => 1<br>        \| Card.exactly n => n<br>        \| Card.all => 1<br>        \| Card.atLeast m => m) ∧<br>    (∀ (c : Card) (a : ℕ),<br>        K.required c ≤ a →<br>          K.consume c a =<br>            some<br>              (match c with<br>              \| Card.one => 1<br>              \| Card.exactly n => n<br>              \| Card.all => a<br>              \| Card.atLeast n => a)) ∧<br>      ∀ (c : Card), ∀ a &lt; K.required c, K.consume c a = none</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A contract is the pair of functions of one implementation; a call that raises (an exception, a panic, an assertion failure) is read as `none`, a call that returns `k` as `some k`. Which error is raised, and its message, are not part of the statement.
- AC1 (each variant returns the values of the table) is the first two lines: the first line is the requiredCount column, the second the consumptionCount column on every call the third line allows.
- AC2 (calling consumptionCount with insufficient tokens is an error) is the third line.
- The counts n and m range over all naturals. That Exactly(n) and AtLeast(m) are built only with n, m ≥ 1 is IO-002 AC1 and IO-004 AC1, not this block's subject; the table holds for 0 too.
- '(matching) tokens' in the definition of requiredCount() is read at the granularity of a token count: which tokens of a place count as matching (the ν correlation of NU-020) is decided before the count reaches these functions, so `available` is already the matching count.
- That an executor calls consumptionCount only on an enabled transition, where every input place holds its required count, is enablement (CORE-022, IO-005), not this block's subject; that a firing removes exactly consumptionCount(available) tokens is CORE-030's subject (glossary: Consume, bridge lemma `consumes_eq`).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): The reference contract satisfies IO-007: `Card.required` is the requiredCount column, `consumptionCount` (`available` where `Card.consumesAll`, else `Card.required`) is the consumptionCount column, and the guard makes every call below the required count fail.
  <pre>IO_007 ioRefContract</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_forbids_allNeedsNothing` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): ✗ An `All` that needs no token violates IO-007 (first line): its requiredCount is 0, not 1; with it, firing an `All` arc from an empty place would be enabled.
  <pre>¬IO_007 io007_allNeedsNothing</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_mutant_dropConsumption_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): The minimum-taking `AtLeast` kills the second mutant: it meets the mutant (required counts and failures as the table says) but not IO-007 (`AtLeast(1)` with 2 available returns 1, not 2).
  <pre>IO_007_mutant_dropConsumption io007_atLeastTakesMinimum ∧<br>  ¬IO_007 io007_atLeastTakesMinimum</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_mutant_dropError_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): The contract that never fails kills the third mutant: it meets the mutant (both columns are right) but not IO-007 (`One` with 0 available returns 1 instead of failing).
  <pre>IO_007_mutant_dropError io007_neverFails ∧ ¬IO_007 io007_neverFails</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_mutant_dropRequired_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): The contract that requires nothing kills the first mutant: it meets the mutant (the consumption column is right and no call is below a required count of 0) but not IO-007 (`One` requires 0 tokens, not 1).
  <pre>IO_007_mutant_dropRequired io007_requiresNothing ∧ ¬IO_007 io007_requiresNothing</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_007_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_007): Every line of IO-007 is exercised by the reference contract, and the contract is the one the reference firing uses: `AtLeast(2)` requires 2, returns `available` (3) with 3 available and fails with 1; `Exactly(2)` returns 2 with 3 available; and a reference firing of an `AtLeast(2)` arc from a place holding 3 consumes `consumptionCount(3) = 3`, draining it.
  <pre>ioRefContract.required (Card.atLeast 2) = 2 ∧<br>  ioRefContract.consume (Card.atLeast 2) 3 = some 3 ∧<br>    ioRefContract.consume (Card.exactly 2) 3 = some 2 ∧<br>      ioRefContract.consume (Card.atLeast 2) 1 = none ∧<br>        Consumes io007_t io007_m 0 = consumptionCount (Card.atLeast 2) 3 ∧<br>          Fire RefFire io007_t io007_m io007_m'</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO` (Libpetri.Spec.Consistency): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks hold together: the reference builder, the reference firing relation and its timed-out firings satisfy every behaviour block, and the reference ambiguity check, consumption contract, output validator and branch enumeration satisfy IO-012, IO-007, IO-015 and IO-016.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  (∃ Build F T,<br>      CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>    (∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E</pre>

The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_010"></a>
## IO-010: Output place (leaf)

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_010` · Kind **B** · Scope executors with output validation enabled (the default) · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `43da83ca0e74b3ef`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| `Place(place)` is a leaf node of an output spec that names a single output place.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `Place(place)` fires, it *shall* deposit one or more tokens in `place`.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `Place(place)` fires, it *shall not* deposit a token in any other place.<br><br>*When* a transition whose output spec is `Place(place)` fires, the marking the firing leaves *shall* hold in `place` the tokens the firing deposited there, in addition to the tokens `place` kept through the firing.<br><br>This requirement applies to an executor with output validation enabled, which is the default ([CONC-026]). | 1. For every transition t whose output spec is the leaf Place(p), and every deposit d:<br>2. every firing of t that deposits d puts at least one token in p;<br>3. every firing of t that deposits d puts no token in any place other than p;<br>4. every firing of t that deposits d, from m to m', leaves p holding at least what p kept (nothing if t has a reset arc on p, else m p minus what t consumed from p) plus what d put in p. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] F ↦<br>  ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (p : N),<br>    OutputSpec t = some (Out.place p) →<br>      ∀ (d : List N),<br>        (on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦ Produced d p) ∧<br>          (on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun x x_1 ↦ ∀ (q : N), Produced d q → q = p) ∧<br>            on fun m m' ↦ F t m d m', shall fun m m' ↦<br>              (if ResetArc t p then 0 else TokenCount m p - Consumes t m p) +<br>                  Deposits d p ≤<br>                TokenCount m' p</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- The firing relation F is that of an executor with output validation enabled, the default (CONC-026). An executor with the CONC-026 skip enabled deposits whatever the action wrote, its Place leaf included, so it may write nothing; CONC-026 lifts IO-010 for it, and the evidence below shows that such an executor's firing relation (io010_skipFire) does not satisfy IO-010 (IO_010_forbids_skip), which is why the scope is stated.
- A transition whose output spec is the leaf Place(p) passes every build-time check of its output spec (IO-011 to IO-014: one place, no Timeout, no ForwardInput, one output branch), so the statement ranges over transitions that build; no well-formedness hypothesis is needed.
- A firing is read as one step from a marking to a marking that deposits the tokens of one outcome (glossary: Fire, Deposit); timing and priority play no part. A completion that fails, by an action exception or by IO-015 validation, deposits no outcome and is not a firing here.
- The statement covers a transition whose whole output spec is the leaf. A Place leaf inside And, Xor or Timeout contributes its claim {place} to an output branch (IO-015); what that branch must receive is the subject of IO-011, IO-012, IO-013 and IO-015, not of this block.
- 'One or more tokens' is read on the deposit (glossary: Produced set): p is in the produced set. How many tokens beyond one is not constrained: an output branch is a set of places, and several tokens into p conform (IO-015, IO-016 AC4, CORE-036); the reference semantics deposits exactly one (IO-016). Deposits are compared per place (Deposits d p, Produced d p), never by list order (CORE-036).
- The second clause (nothing outside p) reads 'a single output place' together with IO-015's exact-explanation rule: the produced set of a firing of a Place(p) spec is exactly {p}.
- AC1 (output spec declares a single place) is structural and holds by construction: the leaf names one place, outPlaces (Place p) = [p] (checked by example below); the statement's hypothesis selects exactly these transitions.
- AC2 (produced tokens appear in the place) is the first clause on the deposit and the third clause on the marking. The third clause is a lower bound: it rules out a lost write (the firing reports [p] but leaves p as it was) and a misrouted write (the token lands in another place), also when p already holds tokens. The exact count of p (no extra tokens) is [CORE-030]'s on an input place, [CORE-034]'s on a reset place and [CORE-035]'s third line on every other place; tokens appearing in other places are [CORE-035]'s.
- 'The tokens place kept' is the glossary's effect of a firing on one place: a reset place keeps none [CORE-034], an input place keeps its count minus the consumption count [CORE-030], [IO-007], any other place keeps its count. Inhibitor and read arcs consume nothing [CORE-031], [CORE-032].

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): The reference semantics satisfies IO-010: a firing of a transition whose output spec is `Place(p)` deposits `[p]` (`deposit_place_leaf`), one token in `p` and nothing elsewhere, and `Seam.fireNC` adds that token to what `p` kept (`refFire_apply`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_010 RefFire</pre>
- ✓ **allows** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_allows_multi` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✓ Several tokens into the leaf's place conform (an output branch is a set of places, IO-015, IO-016 AC4, CORE-036): a firing that deposits `[p, p]` satisfies every clause of IO-010.
  <pre>IO_010 io010_multiFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_forbids_idle` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✗ A firing that deposits nothing violates IO-010 (first clause): `io010_t` fires from the empty marking with an empty deposit, so place `0` receives no token.
  <pre>¬IO_010 io010_idleFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_forbids_lost` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✗ A lost write violates IO-010 (third clause), also when the leaf's place already holds tokens: `io010_t` reports `[0]` from the marking with one token in place `0` and leaves it there, so place `0` holds 1 token where it must hold at least 1 + 1.
  <pre>¬IO_010 io010_lostFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_forbids_misroute` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✗ A misrouted write violates IO-010 (third clause), also when the leaf's place already holds tokens: `io010_t` reports `[0]` from the marking with two tokens in place `0`, but the token lands in place `1`, so place `0` holds 2 tokens where it must hold at least 2 + 1.
  <pre>¬IO_010 io010_misrouteFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_forbids_skip` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✗ The skip executor is outside IO-010's scope (CONC-026): its firing relation lets `io010_t` fire from the empty marking with the empty write, so the first clause fails. This is why IO-010 is scoped to executors with output validation enabled.
  <pre>¬IO_010 io010_skipFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_forbids_spill` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): ✗ A firing that writes a second place violates IO-010 (second clause): `io010_t` deposits `[0, 1]`, and place `1` is not its leaf's place.
  <pre>¬IO_010 io010_spillFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_mutant_dropMarking_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): The lost write kills the third mutant: it reports the deposit `[p]`, so the mutant holds, but it leaves the marking unchanged, so IO-010 does not.
  <pre>IO_010_mutant_dropMarking io010_lostFire ∧ ¬IO_010 io010_lostFire</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_010_mutant_dropOnly_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_010): The spilling firing kills the second mutant: it always writes the leaf's place, so the mutant holds, but it also writes the next place, so IO-010 does not.
  <pre>IO_010_mutant_dropOnly io010_spillFire ∧ ¬IO_010 io010_spillFire</pre>
