<a id="Libpetri-Spec-IO_016"></a>
## IO-016: Branch Enumeration

`Libpetri.Spec.IO_016` · Kind **F** · Scope all output specs · Source **verbatim** (spec/02-input-output-specs.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `88c2b9b595b1495c`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| The branch enumeration of a `Place` node *shall* be one output branch, holding that place.<br><br>The branch enumeration of a `ForwardInput(from, to)` node *shall* be one output branch, holding `to`.<br><br>The branch enumeration of an `And` node *shall* be the Cartesian product of its children's branch enumerations: one output branch for each choice of one output branch per child, holding the union of the chosen output branches, with the first child's choice varying slowest.<br><br>The branch enumeration of an `Xor` node *shall* be the union of its children's branch enumerations: one output branch for each output branch of each child, the first child's output branches first.<br><br>The branch enumeration of a `Timeout` node *shall* be its child's branch enumeration. | 1. Place(p) enumerates exactly the one branch {p};<br>2. ForwardInput(from, to) enumerates exactly the one branch {to};<br>3. And(l, r) enumerates, for each branch x of l in order and, within it, each branch y of r in order, the branch x ∪ y, and nothing else;<br>4. Xor(l, r) enumerates the branches of l, in order, then the branches of r, in order, each as often as it occurs there, and nothing else;<br>5. Timeout(c) enumerates exactly the branches of c, in order. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] E ↦<br>  (∀ (p : N), E (Out.place p) = [{p}]) ∧<br>    (∀ (s d : N), E (Out.forward s d) = [{d}]) ∧<br>      (∀ (l r : Out N), E (l.and r) = branchCross (E l) (E r)) ∧<br>        (∀ (l r : Out N), E (l.xor r) = E l ++ E r) ∧ ∀ (c : Out N), E c.timeout = E c</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A branch enumeration is a function from an output spec alone to a list of output branches, so it is static by its type. A branch is a set of places (`Finset`), as the text says ("a set of places: it says which places receive a token when the branch is taken, not how many"). The list keeps multiplicity (`Xor(P, P)` has two branches) and its order: the lines compare lists with equality, so the enumeration is fixed exactly (`IO_016_unique`). The order is normative because the analyses expand a transition into its branches "in the order above" and name the virtual transitions `<t>_b<i>` in enumeration order ([VER-001]), which the byte-identical SMT scripts carry ([VER-013]).
- An n-ary `And` or `Xor` is read as right-nested binary nodes, as `Reference.Out` writes it. List append and the lexicographic Cartesian product are associative and union is associative, so the nesting changes neither the branches nor their order: the n-ary product with the first child slowest is the right-nested product of binary ones.
- A branch read as a set loses a repeated claim: `And(P, P)` enumerates {P}, while the reference `Out.branches` lists [P, P]. IO-011 rejects such a spec when it is built, so on every buildable spec the two agree (`IO_016_card_of_wf`: each set has as many places as the list it reads).
- The list has one entry per assignment (IO-015), counted by position, so on a spec that builds no two entries are the same set: IO-012 rejects at build time any spec with two distinct assignments claiming one set, and its check enumerates per IO-016 and compares the entries as sets in index order (the numbering of VER-001's `<t>_b<i>`). The paragraph saying so is a consequence of IO-012's build check, not a further constraint on the enumeration; it is derived, not stated (`IO_016_nodup_of_wf`). The enumeration itself is defined on every spec, ambiguous ones included, because the build check is computed from it.
- A spec that builds holds at most one `Timeout` node, and a `ForwardInput` only inside that node's child (IO-013, IO-014). The `Timeout` and `ForwardInput` lines still fix the enumeration on every spec: the enumeration is a total function on output specs, and the build checks read it before a spec is known to build.
- AC1 (`And(P1, P2)` → {P1, P2}) follows from lines 1 and 3; AC2 (`Xor(P1, P2)` → {P1}, {P2}) from lines 1 and 4; AC3 (`Xor(And(P1, P2), P3)` → {P1, P2}, {P3}) from lines 1, 3 and 4. The non-vacuity theorem checks all three on the reference enumeration.
- AC4 (the executor's WARN log-message event when a validated firing deposits more than one token in a place its matched branch names, at most once per transition per execution) is an [EVT-013] event of a running executor, and is skipped with the rest of validation by the CONC-026 option. Neither the branch enumeration nor the firing relation has an event store or an execution; it is not encoded here and needs a behaviour block over executions.
- A branch fixes which places receive tokens, not how many: with output validation enabled, a firing that ends with its action deposits one or more tokens in each place of one output branch and none in any other (glossary: Output branch, IO-015, CORE-036). The paragraph on what the analyses deposit (one token per place of a branch: VER-010, VER-001, NU-051) describes those analyses, not the enumeration, and is their subject. The reference firing semantics deposits one token per claim of a branch, the case the analyses model; the non-vacuity theorem shows one such firing.
- The paragraph on the timeout outcome says that it is not an output branch (glossary: Timeout outcome), which the `Timeout` line respects by enumerating the child's branches only; the `ForwardInput` line is the branch an action writes, `to` claimed once (glossary: Forward, IO-015), not the timeout outcome's per-consumed-token deposit (IO-014). How the analyses append the timeout outcome after the branches, when it differs place by place from every branch (deposits compared per place, CORE-036), is VER-001's subject.
- The block fixes the enumeration function only. That executors and analyses use it (the executor's validation, the SCG virtual transitions, the flattener) is the subject of IO-015, VER-001 and VER-010.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The reference enumeration satisfies IO-016: `Out.branches` is `[[p]]` on a place, `[[to]]` on a forward, `cross` (concatenation of one branch per child, left child slowest) on `And`, append on `Xor` and the child's on `Timeout`; read as sets, concatenation is union (`map_toFinset_cross`). Each line holds with equality, in the reference order.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], IO_016 refBranchSets</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_forbids_andAsXor` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): ✗ Reading `And` like `Xor` violates IO-016 (third line): `And(0, 1)` enumerates the two branches {0}, {1} instead of the one branch {0, 1}.
  <pre>¬IO_016 io016_andAsXor</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_forbids_forwardNone` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): ✗ Dropping a forward from the enumeration violates IO-016 (second line): `ForwardInput(0, 1)` enumerates no branch instead of {1}.
  <pre>¬IO_016 io016_forwardNone</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_dropAnd_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The `And`-as-`Xor` enumeration kills the third mutant: every other node follows the text, but `And(0, 1)` enumerates two branches, not one.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_dropAnd io016_andAsXor ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_andAsXor</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_dropForward_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The source-place enumeration kills the second mutant: every other node follows the text, but `ForwardInput(0, 1)` enumerates {0}, not {1}.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_dropForward io016_forwardSrc ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_forwardSrc</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_dropPlace_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The empty-leaf enumeration kills the first mutant: every other node follows the text, but `Place(0)` enumerates {∅}, not {{0}}.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_dropPlace io016_placeEmpty ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_placeEmpty</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_dropTimeout_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The no-branch-`Timeout` enumeration kills the fifth mutant: every other node follows the text, but `Timeout(Place(0))` enumerates no branch, not its child's {0}.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_dropTimeout io016_timeoutNone ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_timeoutNone</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_dropXor_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The `Xor`-as-`And` enumeration kills the fourth mutant: every other node follows the text, but `Xor(0, 1)` enumerates the one branch {0, 1}, not the two branches {0}, {1}.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_dropXor io016_xorAsAnd ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_xorAsAnd</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_mutant_unordered_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): The reversed-`Xor` enumeration kills the order mutant: its branches are the right ones up to order, but `Xor(0, 1)` lists {1} before {0}, so the virtual transitions `<t>_b<i>` of [VER-001] would be numbered the other way round.
  <pre>IO_016_mutant_unordered io016_xorReversed ∧ ¬IO_016 io016_xorReversed</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.IO_016_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.IO_016): Every line of IO-016 is exercised by the reference enumeration on the acceptance criteria: AC1 `And(P1, P2)` → {P1, P2}; AC2 `Xor(P1, P2)` → {P1}, {P2}; AC3 `Xor(And(P1, P2), P3)` → {P1, P2}, {P3}; `Timeout(Xor(P1, P2))` → {P1}, {P2}; `Xor(P1, Timeout(ForwardInput(P1, P2)))` → {P1}, {P2}, the forward inside a `Timeout` child as [IO-014] requires. And a reference firing of a transition with output spec `And(0, 1)` deposits its one branch, one token in each of 0 and 1.
  <pre>refBranchSets ((Out.place 1).and (Out.place 2)) = [{1, 2}] ∧<br>  refBranchSets ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 2)) = [{1}, {2}] ∧<br>    refBranchSets (((Out.place 1).and (Out.place 2)).xor (Out.place 3)) = [{1, 2}, {3}] ∧<br>      refBranchSets ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.place 2)).timeout = [{1}, {2}] ∧<br>        refBranchSets ((Out.place 1).xor (Out.forward 1 2).timeout) = [{1}, {2}] ∧<br>          RefFire io016_t io016_m [0, 1] io016_m'</pre>

## TIME-001: Timing Specification

           ("Exact time must be at most MAX_DURATION_MS (3153600000000): " ++<br>                                  toString a)) ∧<br>                        (∀ (tm : Novel.TimedScg.Timing), K.build tm = K.factory tm) ∧<br>                          K.defaultTiming = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.immediate</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A contract is the accessors, the factories and the transition builder of one implementation over the five variants of the Rust enum (Novel.TimedScg.Timing), with bounds in milliseconds; a call that fails is read as `.error msg` with its message, a call that returns as `.ok`. `factory tm` is the factory of tm's variant called with tm's bounds; `build tm` builds a transition with tm written out directly (a Rust enum variant, a TypeScript object literal, a Java record constructor).
- Bounds are naturals, as Rust's `u64` arguments are, so the rows of the message table that check for a negative bound (TypeScript and Java only) and the `0 <=` halves of the validity table are not encoded. The messages are the Rust, TypeScript and Python form, with the argument printed in decimal milliseconds; Java's form (`MAX_DURATION (PT876000H)`, `Duration.toString()`) is the same table in another notation and is not encoded.
- 'The first rule the bounds break' together with 'The checks run in the order of the table' is encoded by the premises of lines 5 to 11: each line assumes the bounds pass every earlier rule of the variant (only `window` has rules the same bounds can break together; `deadline(0)` and `deadline(by > maximum)` exclude each other). Lines 4 to 11 fix the factory on every timing: a valid one is returned as written, an invalid one fails with exactly one message.
- AC1 (each transition has a timing, Immediate by default) is line 13: `defaultTiming` is the timing the builder gives a transition built without one. The default Delayed(0), which has the same interval [0, maximum] as Immediate, is killed as TIME_001_mutant_sameInterval.
- AC2 (earliest() and latest() are queryable) is lines 1 and 2; the value table of the accessors is the first two shall sentences.
- AC3 (hasDeadline() by variant whatever the bounds, so a Window whose latest bound equals the maximum still has a deadline) is line 3 (ruling D2). The older literal reading 'hasDeadline() is latest below the maximum' is killed as TIME_001_mutant_latestBelowMax, on window(0, maximum). That a transition SHOULD be disabled after its latest bound is keyed on Reapable (Deadline, Window), not on hasDeadline: Exact has a deadline but is enforced softly (TIME-006, TIME-013).
- The validity table of line 4 is Timing.Valid, the predicate the timed step, the state-class graph and the TIME-002 to TIME-006 blocks read (time001_valid_iff, cited).
- AC4 (the validity table is canonical; the boundary at the maximum duration for every bound, including deadline's by and window's latest, with the table's messages) is lines 4 to 11 (ruling D11). The nonvacuous example TIME_001_nonvacuous checks each boundary named in AC4 and in the test derivation, window(maximum + 1, maximum + 2) included. The old checks (Timing.ValidPre: deadline only 0 < by, window only earliest <= latest, delayed and exact nothing) are killed as TIME_001_mutant_validPre.
- AC5 (a timing written out without a factory is held to AC4 and to the factories' other checks, and a valid one builds unchanged) is line 12; TIME_001_forbids_uncheckedBuild is the builder that skips the check and passes delayed(maximum + 1) on to the verifier. Java's records check in their constructors, which the contract reads as a factory and a builder that agree.
- A factory returns the timing as written (line 4): a valid window(earliest, maximum) stays a Window and keeps its deadline (AC3); it is not normalised to delayed(earliest).
- The two body bullets are the rules of other blocks and are not encoded again: no firing before the earliest bound since the transition was armed is TIME-010 (cited by time001_fire_after_earliest), and reaping a reapable transition once its elapsed time exceeds latest plus the deadline tolerance is TIME-013 (cited by time001_reap_past_latest). That Immediate and Delayed have no finite latest bound and their latest() reports the maximum duration is line 2; reading the maximum as no bound at all is not proven (Novel.TimedScg.Timing, module docstring).
- The maximum-duration constant is maxDurationMs = 3153600000000 (time001_maxDuration); Java's MAX_DURATION is Duration.ofDays(36500), the same duration. Java's sixth variant `Unconstrained` is not in the spec and not modelled.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): The reference contract satisfies TIME-001: the accessors are `Timing.earliest`, `Timing.latest` and `Timing.hasDeadline`, whose tables are the spec's; the factory accepts exactly `Timing.Valid`, which is the validity table (`time001_valid_iff`), and otherwise fails with the message of the first check `timing.rs` runs; and the builder runs the same check.
  <pre>TIME_001 refTimingQ</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_forbids_normalise` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): ✗ Normalising `window(0, maximum)` to `delayed(0)` violates TIME-001 (line 4): a valid timing is returned as written, so the window keeps its deadline (AC3).
  <pre>¬TIME_001 time001_normaliseQ</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_forbids_uncheckedBuild` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): ✗ A builder that skips the check violates TIME-001 (line 12, AC5): it builds a transition with `delayed(maximum + 1)` written out directly, which the factory rejects, and hands the verifier an empty interval.
  <pre>¬TIME_001 time001_uncheckedBuildQ</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_mutant_anyRule_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): The latest-first factory kills the mutant: it fails every invalid `window` with the message of a rule the bounds break, but not TIME-001, since `window(maximum + 1, maximum + 2)` breaks the earliest bound's rule first and gets the latest bound's message (the test derivation of AC4).

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_mutant_latestBelowMax_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): The literal contract kills the mutant: it meets the mutant, but not TIME-001, since `window(0, maximum)` has a deadline by its variant and none by the literal reading (AC3).

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_001_mutant_sameInterval_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_001): The `delayed(0)` default kills the mutant: `delayed(0)` has the interval `[0, maximum]` and no deadline, as `Immediate` has, so it meets the mutant, but TIME-001 fixes the default to `Immediate` itself (AC1, line 13). The two differ wherever the variant is read, for example by the TIME-003 factory checks and by a state-class graph keyed on the variant.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_002"></a>
## TIME-002: Immediate Timing

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `dd46f1ae494fe220`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a transition with `Immediate` timing is armed, its timing *shall not* stop it from being ready ([TIME-010]).<br><br>The executor *shall not* reap a transition with `Immediate` timing ([TIME-013]). | 1. For every timed net X and every transition u the net holds under its name, with timing Immediate:<br>2. while u is armed, the step relation has every firing of u that the reference semantics has, with the same successor marking;<br>3. no step disarms u while it stays enabled (StaysArmed): when the net's names are distinct, every step other than a reap of u, a firing of u itself included, leaves u armed if u was armed before it and is enabled after it;<br>4. the step relation has no reap of u. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>    InNet X u →<br>      TimingOf X u = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.immediate →<br>        (∀ (s : TSt N) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            Armed s u → RefFire u s.m d m' → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s' ∧ s'.m = m') ∧<br>          StaysArmed R X u ∧ ∀ (s s' : TSt N), ¬R X s (TLbl.reap u) s'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt); a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic, and the relation may be late: a delay may pass any bound. Armed, Elapsed time, Ready and Reapable are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean).
- Line 1 is the first 'shall' sentence. Readiness is read through the step relation: a transition whose timing does not stop it is one the relation may fire. Immediate has earliest bound 0 and is not reapable, so it is ready whenever it is armed, at every elapsed time, also past its finite latest() (the maximum duration). The firing order among ready transitions (priority, FIFO) is EXEC-002 and is not part of an atomic step.
- Line 2 is the same sentence for steps that would make u not ready by disarming it while it stays enabled, whatever the label of the step; TIME_002_forbids_drop shows a firing of another transition that drops u's stamp, and TIME_002_forbids_selfDrop a firing of u itself that leaves u enabled and unarmed. It is the frame StaysArmed shared with TIME-003 to TIME-006 (GlossaryTime.lean); its exception for the reap of u is empty here, by line 3. Distinct names (NamesDistinct, ruling D10) are needed because stamps are kept per name, so the reap of another transition of the same name would drop u's stamp. A firing that leaves u enabled re-arms it, possibly with a fresh stamp (TIME-012); this line says only that u stays armed.
- Line 3 is the second 'shall' sentence: no reap step of u exists at all. Together with line 2 it excludes a reap under another label.
- The interval [0, ∞), earliest() = 0, latest() = maximum duration and hasDeadline() = false are the Timing accessors (time002_interval, cited); the reading of the maximum duration as no bound (ruling D11) is what line 1 and line 3 state for the executor.
- AC1 (fires immediately when enabled) is read as readiness, line 1, exercised in TIME_002_nonvacuous at elapsed time 0. Line 1 speaks of an armed transition; 'when enabled' becomes 'when armed' only through TIME-010 (a transition enabled in the initial marking or after a firing that writes one of its arc places is newly armed) and TIME-012, which ConsistencyTime conjoins: an executor that never arms a newly enabled Immediate transition meets this block on its own. That it fires on the next cycle, before any other ready transition, is the eager Phase 2 executor's and EXEC-002's, not an atomic step's (ruling D3). AC2 (no deadline enforcement) is lines 2 and 3, exercised in TIME_002_nonvacuous past the maximum duration plus the tolerance, where u is still armed, still fires and has no reap.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-002: an `Immediate` transition has earliest bound 0 and is never expired under `refPolicy` (it is not reapable), so an armed one fires with every reference firing (line 1), no reap of it exists (line 3), and every other step leaves it armed if it is enabled after (line 2, `staysArmed_of`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_002 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_forbids_drop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): ✗ An executor whose firing disarms a still-enabled armed `Immediate` transition violates TIME-002 (line 2): at `T = 0` `v` fires, writing none of `u`'s arc places, `u` stays enabled, and the step drops `u`'s stamp, so `u` is no longer ready.
  <pre>¬TIME_002 (RefTStepW time002_dropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_forbids_idle` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): ✗ An executor that does not fire an armed `Immediate` transition violates TIME-002 (line 1): at `T = 0` the transition `u` is armed and has a reference firing, which the idle relation does not take.
  <pre>¬TIME_002 time002_idle</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_forbids_selfDrop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): ✗ An executor whose firing of `u` itself leaves `u` enabled but unarmed violates TIME-002 (line 2): with two tokens in `u`'s input place at `T = 0`, `u` fires and stays enabled, and the self-drop policy gives it no clock, a reap hidden in its own firing.
  <pre>¬TIME_002 (RefTStepW selfDropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_mutant_reapMax_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): The step that reaps `Immediate` at the maximum duration kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-002, since at `T = MAX_DURATION_MS + 6` it reaps `u`, armed since `0` (line 3).
  <pre>TIME_002_mutant_reapMax (RefTStepW time002_reapMaxPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_002 (RefTStepW time002_reapMaxPolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): Every line of TIME-002 is exercised by the reference step, with tolerance 5 ms. AC1: armed at `T = 0` (elapsed 0), `u` fires (line 1). AC2: a delay to `T = MAX_DURATION_MS + 6` keeps `u` armed (line 2); there its elapsed time is past its finite `latest()` plus the tolerance, yet it still fires (line 1) and has no reap (line 3), where the mutant reading reaps it.
  <pre>NamesDistinct time002_X ∧<br>  (∃ s',<br>      RefTStep time002_X (time002_at 0) (TLbl.fire time002_u []) s' ∧ s'.m = time002_m1) ∧<br>    RefTStep time002_X (time002_at 0) (TLbl.delay time002_late)<br>        (time002_at time002_late) ∧<br>      Armed (time002_at 0) time002_u ∧<br>        Armed (time002_at time002_late) time002_u ∧<br>          (TimingOf time002_X time002_u).latest + Tolerance time002_X &lt;<br>              Elapsed (time002_at time002_late) time002_u ∧<br>            (∃ s',<br>                RefTStep time002_X (time002_at time002_late) (TLbl.fire time002_u []) s' ∧<br>                  s'.m = time002_m1) ∧<br>              (∀ (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                  ¬RefTStep time002_X (time002_at time002_late) (TLbl.reap time002_u)<br>                      s') ∧<br>                ∃ s',<br>                  RefTStepW time002_reapMaxPolicy time002_X (time002_at time002_late)<br>                    (TLbl.reap time002_u) s'</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_002_nonvacuous_two` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_002): Line 2 exercised through another firing by the reference step: at `T = 0` `v` fires, and `u` stays enabled and armed with its stamp `0`.
  <pre>NamesDistinct time002_Y ∧<br>  Armed time002_two time002_u ∧<br>    RefTStep time002_Y time002_two (TLbl.fire time002_v []) (time002_afterV refPolicy) ∧<br>      Enabled time002_u (time002_afterV refPolicy).m ∧<br>        Armed (time002_afterV refPolicy) time002_u ∧<br>          (time002_afterV refPolicy).stamp "u" = some 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_003"></a>
## TIME-003: Deadline Timing

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `d86c761d85d1c4c5`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a transition with `Deadline(by)` timing is armed, its timing *shall not* stop it from being ready until it is expired.<br><br>*If* a transition with `Deadline(by)` timing is armed and its elapsed time exceeds `by` plus the deadline tolerance, *then* the executor *shall* reap it ([TIME-013]). | 1. For every timed net X and every transition u the net holds under its name, with timing Deadline(by):<br>2. while u is armed and its elapsed time is not greater than by plus the tolerance, the step relation has every firing of u that the reference semantics has, with the same successor marking;<br>3. no firing of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is greater than by plus the tolerance;<br>4. from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is greater than by plus the tolerance, the step relation has a reap of u;<br>5. a reap of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is greater than by plus the tolerance, and leaves u not armed;<br>6. u's clock is framed (ClockFrame): when the net's names are distinct, no step but the reap of u disarms u while it stays enabled, and a delay, the reap of a transition of another name, and a firing of a transition of another name that leaves u enabled in its intermediate marking and after it keep u's stamp. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N) (u : Reference.Trans N) (b : ℕ),<br>    InNet X u →<br>      TimingOf X u = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.deadline b →<br>        (∀ (s : TSt N) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            Armed s u →<br>              ¬b + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u →<br>                RefFire u s.m d m' → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s' ∧ s'.m = m') ∧<br>          (while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ b + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u, never fun s s' ↦<br>              ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>            (∀ (s : TSt N),<br>                Armed s u →<br>                  b + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.reap u) s') ∧<br>              (on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.reap u) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                  Armed s u ∧ b + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u ∧ ¬Armed s' u) ∧<br>                ClockFrame R X u</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt); a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic, and the relation may be late: a delay may pass any bound. Armed, Elapsed time, Expired, Reaped and Deadline tolerance are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean). For a Deadline(by) transition, expired is exactly armed with an elapsed time strictly greater than by plus the tolerance, since Deadline is reapable and its latest bound is by.
- Line 1 is the first 'shall' sentence. Readiness is read through the step relation: a transition whose timing does not stop it is one the relation may fire. Its earliest bound is 0, so the only timing obstacle is expiry, and line 1 asks for every reference firing up to and including the bound, so an executor that refuses to fire at the bound is excluded; one that reaps early is excluded by line 4. The firing order among ready transitions (priority, FIFO) is EXEC-002 and is not part of an atomic step.
- Lines 2 to 4 are the second 'shall' sentence (ruling D4: strictly past by plus the tolerance, and the tolerance is MUST). An atomic relation cannot say that a reap happens, only that it is available and is the only way out: line 3 is the reap step being available (TIME-013 states the same for every expired transition, its last line), line 2 that the expired transition does not fire instead, and line 4 that a reap happens only when it is expired and disarms it. That the reap happens in the deadline step of the very cycle in which the bound is passed ([EXEC-001] step 4) and emits TransitionTimedOut ([EVT-009]) is the Phase 2 executor's, not this block's.
- Line 5 is the frame ClockFrame shared with TIME-004 to TIME-006 (GlossaryTime.lean). Its first part is the first 'shall' sentence for steps that would make u not ready by disarming it: the only step that may take the clock from an enabled Deadline transition is its reap, and a firing of u itself that leaves u enabled re-arms it (TIME_003_forbids_selfDrop). Its other parts read 'elapsed time' in the second 'shall' sentence through the glossary (Elapsed: the clock reading minus the stamp taken when the transition was most recently newly armed): a delay, the reap of another transition and a firing of another transition that leaves u enabled throughout newly arm nothing, so they keep u's stamp. Without them an executor that restamps every armed transition on each delay (TIME_003_mutant_anyStamp_killed), or restarts every armed clock on every firing (TIME_003_mutant_restart_killed), keeps the elapsed time of u from growing in a busy net and never reaps it. That a delay advances the clock by its length is TIME-015 line 2, conjoined in ConsistencyTime. Distinct names (NamesDistinct, ruling D10) are needed because stamps are kept per name. Under ruling D6 an injected token also writes arc places, but injection is not a step of the Phase 1 relation.
- The body sentence that the transition must fire within by (plus the tolerance) is not a property of the executor, which may be late (ruling D3): the executor reaps instead. The strongly urgent reading, under which time cannot pass beyond the latest bound of an enabled transition, is the verification semantics (Novel.TimedScg.TStep, timed_run_sound) and is not encoded here.
- Construction (0 < by <= maximum duration) is TIME-001 AC4 (ruling D11), a property of Timing.Valid, not of the step relation (time003_construction, cited); the interval, earliest() = 0, latest() = by and hasDeadline() = true are the Timing accessors (time003_interval, cited).
- AC1 (fires immediately after enablement) is line 1 at elapsed time 0, exercised in TIME_003_nonvacuous. AC2 (a transition still armed past by plus the deadline tolerance does not fire after that bound; if it is still armed on the first cycle whose elapsed time is strictly greater than the bound, it is reaped in that cycle) is lines 2 to 4, exercised in TIME_003_nonvacuous with Deadline(5000 ms) and tolerance 5 ms: u fires at elapsed 5005 and cannot be reaped there, and at 5006 it cannot fire, is reaped and is no longer armed. Its first sentence is the safety line 2 (no firing past the bound). Its second sentence is conditional on u still being armed, so a competitor that disables u before its bound does not falsify it ([TIME-011]); on the step relation it is lines 3 and 4, the reap offered in every state strictly past the bound and in no other. That the reap happens in the first such cycle is a property of the cycle loop ([EXEC-001] step 4), which the atomic Phase 1 relation does not have, and is deferred to Phase 2 (RefExec). The test derivation's 'verify transition disabled' after the reap is the reaped transition staying unarmed: across delays and other reaps by line 5, across a firing that writes none of its arc places by TIME-010 line 1 and TIME-013, so it is covered only jointly, in ConsistencyTime. The TransitionTimedOut event of AC2 is EVT-009, deferred to Phase 2.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-003: every `Deadline(by)` transition has earliest bound 0 and is expired under `refPolicy` exactly when it is armed and strictly past `by` plus the tolerance, so `time003_lines` applies with the strict test.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_003 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_forbids_drop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): ✗ An executor whose firing disarms a still-enabled armed transition violates TIME-003 (line 5, `StaysArmed`): at `T = 3000` `v` fires, writing none of `u`'s arc places, `u` stays enabled, and the step drops `u`'s stamp.
  <pre>¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW time003_dropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_forbids_earlyReap` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): ✗ An executor that reaps before the bound violates TIME-003 (line 4): at `T = 0` it reaps the `Deadline(5000)` transition `u`, armed since `0`, whose elapsed time is `0`.
  <pre>¬TIME_003 time003_earlyReap</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_forbids_noReap` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): ✗ An executor without deadline enforcement violates TIME-003 (line 2): at `T = 5006` the `Deadline(5000)` transition `u`, armed since `0`, is past `5000 + 5` and still fires.
  <pre>¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW time003_noReapPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_forbids_noReapStep` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): ✗ An executor that blocks an expired transition without reaping it violates TIME-003 (line 3): at `T = 5006` the `Deadline(5000)` transition `u`, armed since `0`, is past `5000 + 5` and has no reap step.
  <pre>¬TIME_003 time003_noReapStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_forbids_selfDrop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): ✗ An executor whose firing of `u` itself leaves `u` enabled but unarmed violates TIME-003 (line 5, `StaysArmed`): with two tokens in `u`'s input place at `T = 0`, `u` fires and stays enabled, and the self-drop policy gives it no clock.
  <pre>¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW selfDropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_mutant_anyStamp_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): The snooze executor kills the mutant: its firings and reaps are the reference's and its delays keep every armed transition armed, so it meets the mutant reading, but it never lets an elapsed time grow and so never reaps; TIME-003 rejects it by line 5 (the delay part of the clock frame), since its delay of 5006 ms from `T = 0` moves `u`'s stamp from `0` to `5006`.
  <pre>TIME_003_mutant_anyStamp time003_snooze ∧ ¬TIME_003 time003_snooze</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_mutant_noTol_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): The step that ignores the tolerance kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-003, since at `T = 5003` (elapsed `5003`, inside the 5 ms tolerance band past `5000`) it refuses to fire `u`, which the spec still allows.
  <pre>TIME_003_mutant_noTol (RefTStepW time003_noTolPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW time003_noTolPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_mutant_reapGe_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): The step that reaps at the bound kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-003, since at `T = 5005` (elapsed `5005 = 5000 + 5`, not past the bound) it refuses to fire `u`, which the spec still allows (AC2: reaped only once elapsed is strictly greater).
  <pre>TIME_003_mutant_reapGe (RefTStepW time003_reapGePolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW time003_reapGePolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_mutant_restart_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): The restart on every firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-003 (line 5, the firing part of the clock frame). At `T = 3000` the unrelated `v` fires and `u`, enabled throughout, is restamped `3000` instead of keeping `0`, so in a busy net every firing pushes `u`'s bound back and `u` is never reaped.
  <pre>TIME_003_mutant_restart (RefTStepW time003_restartPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_003 (RefTStepW time003_restartPolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): Every line of TIME-003 is exercised by the reference step, with `Deadline(5000)` and tolerance 5 ms. AC1: armed at `T = 0` (elapsed 0), `u` fires (line 1). A delay of 5005 ms keeps `u` armed (line 5). At `T = 5005`, elapsed equal to the bound plus the tolerance, `u` still fires (line 1) and has no reap (line 4). AC2: at `T = 5006`, elapsed strictly greater, `u` is armed and cannot fire (line 2), it is reaped (line 3), and after the reap it is not armed but still enabled, that is reaped (line 4). The delays exercise the delay part of line 5.
  <pre>NamesDistinct time003_X ∧<br>  (∃ s',<br>      RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 0) (TLbl.fire time003_u []) s' ∧ s'.m = time003_m1) ∧<br>    RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 0) (TLbl.delay 5005) (time003_at 5005) ∧<br>      Armed (time003_at 0) time003_u ∧<br>        Armed (time003_at 5005) time003_u ∧<br>          (∃ s', RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 5005) (TLbl.fire time003_u []) s') ∧<br>            (∀ (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                ¬RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 5005) (TLbl.reap time003_u) s') ∧<br>              Armed (time003_at 5006) time003_u ∧<br>                (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                    ¬RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 5006) (TLbl.fire time003_u d) s') ∧<br>                  RefTStep time003_X (time003_at 5006) (TLbl.reap time003_u)<br>                      time003_reaped ∧<br>                    ¬Armed time003_reaped time003_u ∧ Reaped time003_reaped time003_u</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_003_nonvacuous_two` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_003): Line 5 (the clock frame) exercised in the two-transition net by the reference step: at `T = 3000` `v` fires, `u` stays enabled and armed with its stamp `0`; at `T = 5006` `v` is reaped and `u`'s stamp is still `0`.
  <pre>NamesDistinct time003_Y ∧<br>  Armed (time003_two 3000 0 0) time003_u ∧<br>    RefTStep time003_Y (time003_two 3000 0 0) (TLbl.fire time003_v [])<br>        (time003_afterV refPolicy) ∧<br>      Enabled time003_u (time003_afterV refPolicy).m ∧<br>        Armed (time003_afterV refPolicy) time003_u ∧<br>          (time003_afterV refPolicy).stamp "u" = some 0 ∧<br>            RefTStep time003_Y (time003_two 5006 0 0) (TLbl.reap time003_v)<br>                time003_reapedV ∧<br>              time003_reapedV.stamp "u" = some 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_004"></a>
## TIME-004: Delayed Timing

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `ca6f7474897dec0b`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a transition with `Delayed(after)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is less than `after`, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a transition with `Delayed(after)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is at least `after`, its timing *shall not* stop it from being ready.<br><br>The executor *shall not* reap a transition with `Delayed` timing ([TIME-013]). | 1. For every timed net X, every transition u and every bound a such that u's timing is Delayed(a):<br>2. no firing of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is below a (this restates [TIME-010] line 4 for this timing);<br>3. from every state in which u, the net's transition of its name, is armed and its elapsed time is at least a, every reference firing of u is a step, to the marking that firing gives;<br>4. no step reaps u;<br>5. when u is the net's transition of its name, its clock is framed (ClockFrame): when the net's names are distinct, no step but a reap of u disarms u while it stays enabled, a firing of u itself included, and a delay, the reap of a transition of another name, and a firing of a transition of another name that leaves u enabled in its intermediate marking and after it keep u's stamp. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N) (u : Reference.Trans N) (a : ℕ),<br>    TimingOf X u = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.delayed a →<br>      (while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ Elapsed s u &lt; a, never fun s s' ↦<br>          ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>        (∀ (s : TSt N) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            InNet X u →<br>              Armed s u →<br>                a ≤ Elapsed s u →<br>                  RefFire u s.m d m' → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s' ∧ s'.m = m') ∧<br>          (∀ (s s' : TSt N), ¬R X s (TLbl.reap u) s') ∧ (InNet X u → ClockFrame R X u)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic. Armed, Elapsed time, Reapable and Reaped are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean); bounds are in milliseconds.
- Line 2 reads 'its timing shall not stop it from being ready' on the step relation: a Delayed transition that is armed and past its bound is ready (glossary: Ready; Delayed is not reapable, so it is never expired), and a ready transition can take every reference firing it has, as Covers says for all transitions. Line 2 is that coverage for this timing, so the step relation may not refuse such a firing for a timing reason. Choosing among several ready transitions (priority, FIFO) is [EXEC-002] and [TIME-014]; the atomic step relation of Phase 1 has no such choice.
- Lines 3 and 4 read 'shall not reap' with the glossary meaning of reaping, to disarm without firing: no step labelled as a reap of u (line 3), and the frame ClockFrame shared with TIME-003, TIME-005 and TIME-006 (line 4, GlossaryTime.lean). Its first part, StaysArmed, says no step leaves u enabled and unarmed (reaped, in the glossary's state reading), a firing of u itself included: a firing of u that leaves u enabled re-arms it (TIME_004_forbids_selfDrop). Its other parts keep u's stamp across a delay, a reap of another name and a firing of another name that leaves u enabled throughout; the last is [TIME-012] line 2 specialised to u: without it a firing of an unrelated transition could drop u's clock, a reap hidden in a firing (TIME_004_mutant_dropClean_killed), or restart it, which pushes u's bound back so that its timing stops it from being ready (TIME_004_mutant_restart_killed). A firing that disables u in its intermediate marking or after it drops or restarts u's clock as [TIME-011] and [TIME-012] say, and which stamp a firing of u itself leaves (a fresh one) is [TIME-012] line 4; this block fixes neither. A reap labelled with a transition other than u that carries u's name is not constrained by line 3: it is excluded by [TIME-013] line 1 (only a transition of the net is reaped) together with NamesDistinct (ruling D10). That a delay advances the clock by its length is [TIME-015] line 2.
- The interval bullets (earliest bound after, latest bound the maximum duration, hasDeadline false) are the Timing accessors (time004_interval). The construction bullet is [TIME-001] AC4 (Timing.Valid, ruling D11): Delayed(after) is valid exactly when after is at most the maximum duration.
- AC1 (cannot fire before after has elapsed since enablement) is line 1, with 'since enablement' read as the elapsed time since the stamp ([TIME-010]); measuring from the creation of the consumed tokens is the mutant TIME_004_mutant_tokenAge_killed. AC2 (after after, the transition can fire at any time) is line 2, which holds in every state past the bound, however late, and lines 3 and 4, by which neither a later clock reading nor an unrelated firing takes the transition's clock away or moves it.
- The test derivation (Delayed(3s), enabled at T=0, not ready at T=2s, ready at T=3s) is the nonvacuous example TIME_004_nonvacuous, in milliseconds; it also fires an unrelated transition at T=500 that keeps u's stamp (line 4).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-004: a firing of a `Delayed(a)` transition passes the spec's gate, so its elapsed time is at least `a` (line 1); an armed transition past `a` is not expired, since `Delayed` is not reapable, so it takes every reference firing it has (line 2); for the same reason it is never reaped (line 3); and the reference step meets the clock frame of every transition of the net (line 4, `clockFrame_refTStep`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_004 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_forbids_selfDrop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): ✗ An executor whose firing of `u` itself leaves `u` enabled but unarmed violates TIME-004 (line 4, `StaysArmed`): with two tokens in `u`'s input place at `T = 3000`, `u` fires and stays enabled, and the self-drop policy gives it no clock, so it is left reaped, though `Delayed` is never reaped.
  <pre>¬TIME_004 (RefTStepW selfDropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_forbids_untimed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): A relation that fires every reference firing and ignores the clock (`time010_untimed`) violates TIME-004 (line 1): at `T = 0` it fires `u`, armed with elapsed time `0`, below its bound of 3000 ms.
  <pre>¬TIME_004 time010_untimed</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_mutant_dropClean_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): The clean-set drop kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-004 (line 4). At `T = 500` the unrelated `v` fires; `u` stays enabled in the intermediate marking and after it, yet loses its clock: it is left reaped, though `Delayed` is never reaped (sentence 3), and no step fires it again (AC2).
  <pre>TIME_004_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time004_dropCleanPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_004 (RefTStepW time004_dropCleanPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_mutant_restart_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): The restart on every firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-004 (line 4). At `T = 500` the unrelated `v` fires and `u`, enabled throughout, is restamped `500` instead of keeping `0`, so in a busy net its bound of 3000 ms is pushed back by every firing and its timing stops it from being ready (sentence 2).
  <pre>TIME_004_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time004_restartPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_004 (RefTStepW time004_restartPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_mutant_strictGate_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): The step with the strict gate kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-004, since at `T = 3000`, with elapsed time exactly 3000, `u` cannot fire (AC test derivation: "fire at T=3s → ready").
  <pre>TIME_004_mutant_strictGate (RefTStepW time004_strictGatePolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_004 (RefTStepW time004_strictGatePolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_mutant_tokenAge_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): The step that measures from the creation of the tokens kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-004. At `T = 6000` the token `u` consumes is 6000 ms old, so the mutant fires `u`, armed only 1000 ms before (AC1: "since enablement").
  <pre>TIME_004_mutant_tokenAge 0 (RefTStepW (time004_tokenAgePolicy 0)) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_004 (RefTStepW (time004_tokenAgePolicy 0))</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_004_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_004): Every line of TIME-004 is exercised by the reference step, on the test derivation in milliseconds. `u` is `Delayed(3000)` and armed at `T = 0`. Two seconds later (elapsed 2000, below 3000) it cannot fire (line 1); at `T = 3000` it can (line 2), and it still can at the maximum duration plus the tolerance plus one, where a `Deadline` with that bound would be expired, and no reap of `u` is a step there (line 3). The delays keep its stamp `0` (line 4). At `T = 2000` the deadline transition `v` is expired and reaped, and `u` keeps its stamp (line 4). <a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_005"></a>
## TIME-005: Window Timing

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `e993bed4aabdc912`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a transition with `Window(earliest, latest)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is less than `earliest`, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a transition with `Window(earliest, latest)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is at least `earliest`, its timing *shall not* stop it from being ready until it is expired.<br><br>*If* a transition with `Window(earliest, latest)` timing is armed and its elapsed time exceeds `latest` plus the deadline tolerance, *then* the executor *shall* reap it ([TIME-013]). | 1. For every timed net X, every transition u and all bounds e and l such that u's timing is Window(e, l):<br>2. no firing of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is below e (this restates [TIME-010] line 4 for this timing);<br>3. from every state in which u, the net's transition of its name, is armed and its elapsed time is at least e and at most l plus the tolerance, every reference firing of u is a step, to the marking that firing gives;<br>4. no firing of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is greater than l plus the tolerance;<br>5. from every state in which u, the net's transition of its name, is armed and its elapsed time is greater than l plus the tolerance, a reap of u is a step (the 'shall reap' is encoded as the reap being available; that it is taken is deferred, see below);<br>6. a reap of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is greater than l plus the tolerance, and leaves u reaped: enabled and not armed;<br>7. when u is the net's transition of its name, its clock is framed (ClockFrame): when the net's names are distinct, no step but a reap of u disarms u while it stays enabled, a firing of u itself included, and a delay, the reap of a transition of another name, and a firing of a transition of another name that leaves u enabled in its intermediate marking and after it keep u's stamp. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N) (u : Reference.Trans N) (e l : ℕ),<br>    TimingOf X u = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.window e l →<br>      (while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ Elapsed s u &lt; e, never fun s s' ↦<br>          ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>        (∀ (s : TSt N) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            InNet X u →<br>              Armed s u →<br>                e ≤ Elapsed s u →<br>                  Elapsed s u ≤ l + Tolerance X →<br>                    RefFire u s.m d m' → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s' ∧ s'.m = m') ∧<br>          (while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ l + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u, never fun s s' ↦<br>              ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>            (∀ (s : TSt N),<br>                InNet X u →<br>                  Armed s u →<br>                    l + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.reap u) s') ∧<br>              (on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.reap u) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                  Armed s u ∧ l + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u ∧ Reaped s' u) ∧<br>                (InNet X u → ClockFrame R X u)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic. Armed, Elapsed time, Deadline tolerance, Expired, Ready and Reaped are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean); bounds are in milliseconds.
- Expired is read with ruling D4: the bound is latest plus the deadline tolerance, and the test is strict, as MUST. A Window transition that is armed is expired exactly when its elapsed time is greater than l plus the tolerance (Window is reapable), which is how lines 2 to 5 spell 'until it is expired' and 'exceeds'.
- Line 2 reads 'its timing shall not stop it from being ready until it is expired' on the step relation: a Window transition that is armed, past e and not expired is ready (glossary: Ready), and a ready transition can take every reference firing it has, as Covers says for all transitions. Choosing among several ready transitions (priority, FIFO) is [EXEC-002] and [TIME-014]; the atomic step relation of Phase 1 has no such choice.
- Lines 3 to 5 are the 'If ... then shall reap' sentence: past the bound u does not fire (line 3), a reap of it is a step (line 4), and a reap of u happens only strictly past the bound and leaves it reaped (line 5). The 'shall' of the sentence is encoded only as the reap being offered (line 4 is existential): a relation that lets the clock run on past the bound without reaping satisfies the block, because the atomic relation of Phase 1 lets a delay pass any bound (the executor may be late). That the reap is taken is the cycle loop's obligation ([EXEC-001], Phase 2), as below. Ignoring the tolerance (bound l instead of l plus the tolerance) is the mutant TIME_005_mutant_noTol_killed, a non-strict bound TIME_005_mutant_reapAt_killed.
- Line 6 is the frame ClockFrame shared with TIME-003, TIME-004 and TIME-006 (GlossaryTime.lean); it keeps u's clock to the reap step and to the firings that disable u. Its first part, StaysArmed, says no step but a reap of u leaves u enabled and unarmed, a firing of u itself included: a firing of u that leaves u enabled re-arms it (TIME_005_forbids_selfDrop). Its other parts keep u's stamp across a delay, a reap of another name and a firing of another name that leaves u enabled throughout; the last is [TIME-012] line 2 specialised to u: without it a firing of an unrelated transition could drop u's clock, a reap hidden in a firing that disarms u before it expires (TIME_005_mutant_dropClean_killed), or restart it, so that a continuously enabled window escapes its bound (TIME_005_mutant_restart_killed). A firing that disables u in its intermediate marking or after it drops or restarts u's clock as [TIME-011] and [TIME-012] say, and which stamp a firing of u itself leaves (a fresh one) is [TIME-012] line 4; this block fixes neither. That a delay advances the clock by its length is [TIME-015] line 2. A reap labelled with a transition other than u that carries u's name is not constrained by line 5: it is excluded by [TIME-013] line 1 (only a transition of the net is reaped) together with NamesDistinct (ruling D10).
- AC2 (a transition still armed past latest plus the deadline tolerance does not fire after that bound; if it is still armed on the first cycle whose elapsed time is strictly greater than the bound, it is reaped in that cycle) is lines 2 to 5 and is not quoted: fireable up to the bound, not fireable past it (the first sentence), the reap offered in every state strictly past it and allowed in no other (the second sentence on the step relation). Both sentences speak of a transition still armed, so a competitor that disables u before its bound falsifies neither ([TIME-011] drops its clock). That the reap happens in the first such cycle is a property of the cycle loop ([EXEC-001] step 4), which the atomic step relation of Phase 1 does not have, and is deferred to Phase 2 (RefExec). Line 4 is the reap of u being available; TIME-013's last line states the same for every expired transition. Which event a reap emits is [TIME-013] and [EVT-009].
- AC1 (cannot fire before earliest) is line 1. AC3 (latest below earliest is rejected) and AC4 (earliest or latest above the maximum duration is rejected) are construction rules over Timing.Valid, the F reading of [TIME-001] AC4 with ruling D11: time005_construction states that Window(e, l) is valid exactly when e and l are at most the maximum duration and e is at most l, and time005_construction_examples checks the boundary cases.
- The interval bullets (earliest bound e, latest bound l, hasDeadline true) are the Timing accessors (time005_interval); Window is reapable, unlike Exact ([TIME-006]).
- The test derivation (Window(1s, 5s): not ready at 0.5s, ready at 2s, expired at 6s) is the nonvacuous example TIME_005_nonvacuous, in milliseconds with the default tolerance of 5 ms, which also checks both sides of the bound 5005, and fires an unrelated transition at T=500 that keeps u's stamp (line 6).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-005: a firing of a `Window(e, l)` transition passes the spec's gate, so its elapsed time is at least `e` (line 1), and is not expired, so its elapsed time is at most `l` plus the tolerance (line 3); an armed transition between the two bounds takes every reference firing it has (line 2); past the upper bound it is expired, so a reap is a step (line 4), and a reap needs it expired and drops its stamp only (line 5); the reference step meets the clock frame of every transition of the net (line 6, `clockFrame_refTStep`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_005 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_forbids_selfDrop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): ✗ An executor whose firing of `u` itself leaves `u` enabled but unarmed violates TIME-005 (line 6, `StaysArmed`): with two tokens in `u`'s input place at `T = 2000`, inside the window, `u` fires and stays enabled, and the self-drop policy gives it no clock, a reap before the window's bound hidden in its own firing.
  <pre>¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW selfDropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_forbids_untimed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): A relation that fires every reference firing and ignores the clock (`time010_untimed`) violates TIME-005 (line 1): at `T = 0` it fires `u`, armed with elapsed time `0`, below its earliest bound of 1000 ms.
  <pre>¬TIME_005 time010_untimed</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_mutant_dropClean_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The clean-set drop kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-005 (line 6). At `T = 500` the unrelated `v` fires; the window `u` stays enabled in the intermediate marking and after it, yet loses its clock long before it expires: a reap hidden in a firing.
  <pre>TIME_005_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time005_dropCleanPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW time005_dropCleanPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_mutant_noTol_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The step that ignores the tolerance kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-005, since at `T = 5003`, with elapsed time `5003`, past `5000` but within the tolerance of 5 ms, `u` cannot fire (sentence 2: ready until it is expired, expired only past latest plus the deadline tolerance).
  <pre>TIME_005_mutant_noTol (RefTStepW time005_noTolPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW time005_noTolPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_mutant_reapAt_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The step that reaps at the bound kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-005, since at `T = 5005`, with elapsed time exactly `5000 + 5`, it reaps `u` (AC2: "strictly greater than that bound").
  <pre>TIME_005_mutant_reapAt (RefTStepW time005_reapAtPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW time005_reapAtPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_mutant_restart_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The restart on every firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-005 (line 6). At `T = 500` the unrelated `v` fires and the window `u`, enabled throughout, is restamped `500` instead of keeping `0`, so a continuously enabled window is pushed past its bound by every firing of a busy net and escapes the reap.
  <pre>TIME_005_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time005_restartPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW time005_restartPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_mutant_soft_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): The soft step kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-005, since at `T = 6000` the window `u` is past `5000 + 5` and no reap of it is a step (test derivation: "at 6s → expired").
  <pre>TIME_005_mutant_soft (RefTStepW time005_softPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_005 (RefTStepW time005_softPolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_005_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_005): Every line of TIME-005 is exercised by the reference step, on the test derivation in milliseconds with a tolerance of 5 ms. `u` is `Window(1000, 5000)` and armed at `T = 0`. At `T = 500` it cannot fire (line 1); at `T = 2000` it can (line 2), and still at `T = 5005`, its bound plus the tolerance, where no reap of it is a step (line 5). At `T = 5006` it cannot fire (line 3), a reap of it is a step (line 4) and leaves it reaped (line 5); at `T = 6000` it is expired. The delays keep its stamp `0` (line 6). At `T = 2000` the deadline transition `v` is expired and reaped, and `u` keeps its stamp (line 6). At `T = 500` `v` fires instead; `u`, armed, is enabled in the intermediate marking and after the firing, and keeps its stamp `0` (line 6).
  <pre>RefTStep time005_X time005_s0 (TLbl.delay 500) (time005_at 500) ∧<br>  Armed (time005_at 500) time005_u ∧<br>    Elapsed (time005_at 500) time005_u = 500 ∧<br>      (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>          ¬RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 500) (TLbl.fire time005_u d) s') ∧<br>        RefTStep time005_X time005_s0 (TLbl.delay 2000) (time005_at 2000) ∧<br>          (time005_at 2000).stamp "u" = time005_s0.stamp "u" ∧<br>            (∃ s', RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 2000) (TLbl.fire time005_u []) s') ∧<br>              (∃ s', RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 5005) (TLbl.fire time005_u []) s') ∧<br>                (∀ (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                    ¬RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 5005) (TLbl.reap time005_u) s') ∧<br>                  (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                      ¬RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 5006) (TLbl.fire time005_u d) s') ∧<br>                    RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 5006) (TLbl.reap time005_u)<br>                        time005_reaped ∧<br>                      Reaped time005_reaped time005_u ∧<br>                        Expired time005_X (time005_at 6000) time005_u ∧<br>                          Expired time005_X (time005_at 2000) time005_v ∧<br>                            (∃ s',<br>                                RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 2000) (TLbl.reap time005_v)<br>                                    s' ∧<br>                                  s'.stamp "u" = some 0) ∧<br>                              ∃ s',<br>                                RefTStep time005_X (time005_at 500)<br>                                    (TLbl.fire time005_v []) s' ∧<br>                                  Armed (time005_at 500) time005_u ∧<br>                                    Enabled time005_u (Mid time005_v time005_m0) ∧<br>                                      Enabled time005_u s'.m ∧ s'.stamp "u" = some 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
