<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_006"></a>
## TIME-006: Exact Timing

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `9a5e081db9a5fa53`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* a transition with `Exact(at)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is less than `at`, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a transition with `Exact(at)` timing is armed and its elapsed time is at least `at`, its timing *shall not* stop it from being ready.<br><br>The executor *shall not* reap a transition with `Exact` timing, however late it observes it ([TIME-013]). | 1. For every timed net X, every transition u and every bound a such that u's timing is Exact(a):<br>2. no firing of u starts from a state in which u is armed and its elapsed time is below a (this restates [TIME-010] line 4 for this timing);<br>3. from every state in which u, the net's transition of its name, is armed and its elapsed time is at least a, every reference firing of u is a step, to the marking that firing gives;<br>4. no step reaps u;<br>5. when u is the net's transition of its name, its clock is framed (ClockFrame): when the net's names are distinct, no step but a reap of u disarms u while it stays enabled, a firing of u itself included, and a delay, the reap of a transition of another name, and a firing of a transition of another name that leaves u enabled in its intermediate marking and after it keep u's stamp. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N) (u : Reference.Trans N) (a : ℕ),<br>    TimingOf X u = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.exact a →<br>      (while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ Elapsed s u &lt; a, never fun s s' ↦<br>          ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>        (∀ (s : TSt N) (d : List N) (m' : N → ℕ),<br>            InNet X u →<br>              Armed s u →<br>                a ≤ Elapsed s u →<br>                  RefFire u s.m d m' → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s' ∧ s'.m = m') ∧<br>          (∀ (s s' : TSt N), ¬R X s (TLbl.reap u) s') ∧ (InNet X u → ClockFrame R X u)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic. Armed, Elapsed time, Reapable and Reaped are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean); bounds are in milliseconds.
- The block describes the executor (ruling D3), as the section says: Exact is enforced softly, gated at a and never force-disabled. The verification reading of [at, at] belongs to [VER-002] and [VER-012] and is only cited here: the state class graph reads Exact(a) as the interval [a, a] (time006_graph_interval), and the late-executor analysis lifts its latest bound (Late.relaxLate). No single step relation satisfies both readings, so this block takes no position on the logical-time one.
- Line 2 reads 'its timing shall not stop it from being ready' on the step relation: an Exact transition that is armed and past its bound is ready (glossary: Ready; Exact is not reapable, so it is never expired, though hasDeadline is true), and a ready transition can take every reference firing it has, as Covers says for all transitions. Line 2 holds however late the state is, which is 'never force-disabled for overrunning at'.
- Lines 3 and 4 read 'shall not reap' with the glossary meaning of reaping, to disarm without firing: no step labelled as a reap of u (line 3), and the frame ClockFrame shared with TIME-003 to TIME-005 (line 4, GlossaryTime.lean). Its first part, StaysArmed, says no step leaves u enabled and unarmed (reaped, in the glossary's state reading), a firing of u itself included: a firing of u that leaves u enabled re-arms it (TIME_006_forbids_selfDrop). Its other parts keep u's stamp across a delay, a reap of another name and a firing of another name that leaves u enabled throughout; the last is [TIME-012] line 2 specialised to u: without it a firing of an unrelated transition could drop u's clock, a reap hidden in a firing that force-disables u (TIME_006_mutant_dropClean_killed), or restart it, which pushes at back so that its timing stops it from being ready (TIME_006_mutant_restart_killed). A firing that disables u in its intermediate marking or after it drops or restarts u's clock as [TIME-011] and [TIME-012] say, and which stamp a firing of u itself leaves (a fresh one) is [TIME-012] line 4; this block fixes neither. That a delay advances the clock by its length is [TIME-015] line 2. A reap labelled with a transition other than u that carries u's name is not constrained by line 3: it is excluded by [TIME-013] line 1 (only a transition of the net is reaped) together with NamesDistinct (ruling D10).
- AC1 (cannot fire before at) is line 1. AC2's 'not force-disabled for being observed late' is lines 2, 3 and 4. AC2's 'fires at the first opportunity at or after at' is liveness of the cycle loop ([EXEC-001], [EXEC-002]), which the atomic step relation of Phase 1 does not state; line 2 makes every such firing available from the bound on. 'Exactness is observable from the firing event' is the event log ([EVT-006], [EVT-007]), not this block; that a late Exact transition emits no TransitionTimedOut ([EVT-009]) follows from line 3, as only a reap emits it.
- The interval bullets (earliest and latest bound at, hasDeadline true) are the Timing accessors (time006_interval). The construction bullet is [TIME-001] AC4 (Timing.Valid, ruling D11): Exact(at) is valid exactly when at is at most the maximum duration. The advice to use Delayed(at) or Window(at, at + slack) instead is not a requirement on the executor.
- The test derivation (Exact(50ms) under a busy executor that cannot run a cycle until well past 50ms: it still fires and is not timed out) is the nonvacuous example TIME_006_nonvacuous: at 1000 ms, far past 50 plus the tolerance, the transition can fire and no reap of it is a step; at 10 ms an unrelated transition fires and the transition keeps its stamp (line 4).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-006: a firing of an `Exact(a)` transition passes the spec's gate, so its elapsed time is at least `a` (line 1); an armed transition past `a` is never expired, since `Exact` is not reapable, so it takes every reference firing it has however late (line 2), and it is never reaped (line 3); the reference step meets the clock frame of every transition of the net (line 4, `clockFrame_refTStep`).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_006 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_forbids_selfDrop` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): ✗ An executor whose firing of `u` itself leaves `u` enabled but unarmed violates TIME-006 (line 4, `StaysArmed`): with two tokens in `u`'s input place at `T = 50`, `u` fires and stays enabled, and the self-drop policy gives it no clock, so it is left reaped, though `Exact` is never reaped.
  <pre>¬TIME_006 (RefTStepW selfDropPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_forbids_untimed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): A relation that fires every reference firing and ignores the clock (`time010_untimed`) violates TIME-006 (line 1): at `T = 0` it fires `u`, armed with elapsed time `0`, below `at` of 50 ms.
  <pre>¬TIME_006 time010_untimed</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_mutant_dropClean_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): The clean-set drop kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-006 (line 4). At `T = 10` the unrelated `v` fires; `u` stays enabled in the intermediate marking and after it, yet loses its clock: it is left reaped, force-disabled with no reap step and no `TransitionTimedOut` (sentence 3).
  <pre>TIME_006_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time006_dropCleanPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_006 (RefTStepW time006_dropCleanPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_mutant_hard_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): The hard step kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-006. Under a busy executor that first looks at `T = 1000`, far past `50 + 5`, it reaps `u` (test derivation: "verify it still fires and emits no `TransitionTimedOut`").
  <pre>TIME_006_mutant_hard (RefTStepW time006_hardPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_006 (RefTStepW time006_hardPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_mutant_restart_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): The restart on every firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-006 (line 4). At `T = 10` the unrelated `v` fires and `u`, enabled throughout, is restamped `10` instead of keeping `0`, so `at` is pushed back by every firing of a busy net (sentence 2).
  <pre>TIME_006_mutant_noKeep (RefTStepW time006_restartPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_006 (RefTStepW time006_restartPolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_006_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_006): Every line of TIME-006 is exercised by the reference step, on the test derivation in milliseconds with a tolerance of 5 ms. `u` is `Exact(50)` and armed at `T = 0`. At `T = 49` it cannot fire (line 1); at `T = 50` it can (line 2); under a busy executor that first looks at `T = 1000`, far past `50 + 5`, it still can, and no reap of it is a step (lines 2 and 3). The delays keep its stamp `0` (line 4). At `T = 1000` the deadline transition `v` is expired and reaped, and `u` keeps its stamp (line 4). At `T = 10` `v` fires instead; `u`, armed, is enabled in the intermediate marking and after the firing, and keeps its stamp `0` (line 4).
  <pre>RefTStep time006_X time006_s0 (TLbl.delay 49) (time006_at 49) ∧<br>  Armed (time006_at 49) time006_u ∧<br>    Elapsed (time006_at 49) time006_u = 49 ∧<br>      (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>          ¬RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 49) (TLbl.fire time006_u d) s') ∧<br>        RefTStep time006_X time006_s0 (TLbl.delay 50) (time006_at 50) ∧<br>          (∃ s', RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 50) (TLbl.fire time006_u []) s') ∧<br>            RefTStep time006_X time006_s0 (TLbl.delay 1000) (time006_at 1000) ∧<br>              (time006_at 1000).stamp "u" = time006_s0.stamp "u" ∧<br>                (∃ s', RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 1000) (TLbl.fire time006_u []) s') ∧<br>                  (∀ (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                      ¬RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 1000) (TLbl.reap time006_u) s') ∧<br>                    Expired time006_X (time006_at 1000) time006_v ∧<br>                      (∃ s',<br>                          RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 1000) (TLbl.reap time006_v) s' ∧<br>                            s'.stamp "u" = some 0) ∧<br>                        ∃ s',<br>                          RefTStep time006_X (time006_at 10) (TLbl.fire time006_v []) s' ∧<br>                            Armed (time006_at 10) time006_u ∧<br>                              Enabled time006_u (Mid time006_v time006_m0) ∧<br>                                Enabled time006_u s'.m ∧ s'.stamp "u" = some 0</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_010"></a>
## TIME-010: Clock Starts on Enablement

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `6d72fb8703c93a22`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| A transition is newly armed when it is enabled in the initial marking, afterwards when it is enabled after a firing or an injected token writes one of its arc places while it is not armed, and when its clock restarts in a firing ([TIME-012]).<br><br>*While* a transition is armed, its elapsed time *shall* be the current reading of the firing clock minus its stamp.<br><br>*While* a transition is armed and its elapsed time is less than its earliest bound, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*While* a transition is not armed, the transition *shall not* fire. | 1. For every timed net X:<br>2. a transition of the net that is not armed before a step and is armed after it was armed by a firing that writes one of its arc places, and holds the reading of the firing clock after the step;<br>3. a transition that is armed before and after a step holds the stamp it held before, or the step is a firing and the transition holds the reading of the firing clock after it;<br>4. a firing that writes an arc place of the net's transition u, from a state in which u is not armed, leaves u armed if u is enabled after it;<br>5. no transition fires from a state in which it is armed and its elapsed time is below its earliest bound;<br>6. no transition fires from a state in which it is not armed. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N),<br>    (∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>        InNet X u →<br>          on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>            ¬Armed s u →<br>              Armed s' u →<br>                ∃ t d,<br>                  l = TLbl.fire t d ∧<br>                    (∃ p, ArcPlace u p ∧ Writes t d p) ∧<br>                      s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')) ∧<br>      (∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>          on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>            Armed s u →<br>              Armed s' u →<br>                s'.stamp u.core.name = s.stamp u.core.name ∨<br>                  (∃ t d, l = TLbl.fire t d) ∧<br>                    s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')) ∧<br>        (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>            InNet X u →<br>              on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                ¬Armed s u →<br>                  (∃ p, ArcPlace u p ∧ Writes t d p) → Enabled u s'.m → Armed s' u) ∧<br>          (∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>              while fun s ↦ Armed s u ∧ Elapsed s u &lt; (TimingOf X u).earliest, never<br>                fun s s' ↦ ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>            ∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>              while fun s ↦ ¬Armed s u, never fun s s' ↦ ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic: the step that starts a firing completes it. Armed, Elapsed time, Arc place and Write are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean).
- Line 1 of the formula is the first 'shall' sentence at the grain of the Phase 1 step, together with the quoted sentence on being newly armed. A transition that is not armed before a step and is armed after it is newly armed, so the step is a firing that writes one of its arc places, and the new stamp is the reading of the firing clock after the step (which a firing does not advance). Nothing else newly arms a transition in the step relation: a delay or a reap arms nothing.
- The first 'shall' sentence, 'When a transition is newly armed, the executor shall stamp it with the firing-clock reading taken by the enablement update that newly arms it ([EXEC-001] step 3).', is encoded only at the grain of the atomic Phase 1 step, so it is not quoted. The step relation has no cycles: the enablement update is part of the firing step that makes the transition enabled, and its reading is the reading of that step, which lines 1 and 2 fix. The section's paragraph on the enablement update says more: a synchronous action's deposit is first seen by the enablement update of the next cycle, an asynchronous completion or an injected token by the update of the cycle that takes it in, so the stamp is neither the reading of the cycle in which the arming firing started nor the moment an action completed, and a clock restarted in a firing is stamped by the next enablement update that finds the transition enabled. In the atomic step these readings coincide; that they can differ, and that the stamp is the later one, needs the cycle relation of Phase 2 (RefExec, [EXEC-001] steps 1 to 3) and is deferred to it. No mutant is given for the wrong reading (the reading of the cycle that started the arming firing): the Phase 1 relation cannot tell it apart.
- Line 2 reads 'newly armed' through 'most recently newly armed' of the section's first paragraph: a transition armed before and after a step keeps its stamp, unless the step is a firing that restarts its clock ([TIME-012], glossary: Armed), which stamps it with the current reading as well. When a firing keeps, drops or restarts a clock is TIME-011, TIME-012 and TIME-013, not this block; this block fixes which steps may set a stamp and the value a new stamp takes.
- Line 3 is the converse of line 1 for firings: a firing that writes an arc place of a transition that is not armed, and leaves it enabled, arms it. Lines 1 and 3 together are the quoted sentence's second case, as an equivalence, for a transition that is not armed before the step. Its third case, a clock that restarts in a firing ([TIME-012]), is line 2's second case for a transition armed before the firing and enabled after it: the restart stamps it with the current reading. Which firings restart a clock, including a transition with no arc place the firing writes that stays enabled after its own firing, is [TIME-012] lines 1 and 3, not this block. Its first case is outside the step relation: the initial marking is the initial state (TSt.init stamps every transition enabled in it with the start reading). Injected tokens are not steps of the Phase 1 relation, which has no environment places.
- The second 'shall' sentence (elapsed time is the clock reading minus the stamp) is the glossary definition Elapsed, which line 4 of the formula uses; no step relation can break it, so it is not a separate line.
- Transitions are identified by name (ruling D10): a stamp is kept per name, and lines 1 and 3 speak of the transition the net holds under the name (InNet). The section assumes pairwise distinct names (NamesDistinct), under which InNet is membership in the net (inNet_iff_mem).
- AC1 (enabled at T=10, earliest 3 s, ready at T=13) is the nonvacuous example TIME_010_nonvacuous, in milliseconds as the timings are: armed at 10, not fireable at 12, fireable at 13. The same example re-arms a reaped transition when a firing writes its input place and leaves it unarmed after a firing that writes none of its arc places (ruling D6).
- AC2 (disabled at T=11, re-enabled at T=15, ready at T=18) is the restart of TIME-011, which drops the stamp when the transition is disabled; this block fixes that the stamp taken at 15 is 15 (line 1), not one held from before (TIME_010_mutant_resume_killed), and that the transition does not fire before 18 (line 4).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-010: a delay and a reap arm nothing and keep every stamp they do not drop; a firing newly arms a transition only through `refPolicy.rearm`, which for a transition that was not armed asks for a written arc place, and stamps it with the current reading (`refPolicy.fresh`, line 1); a firing keeps a stamp or restarts it at the current reading (line 2); a firing that writes an arc place of an unarmed transition and leaves it enabled re-arms it (line 3); and a firing needs its transition armed and past `refPolicy`'s earliest-bound gate (lines 4 and 5).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_010 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_forbids_untimed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): ✗ Firing without a clock violates TIME-010 (line 5): from a state in which no transition holds a stamp, the untimed relation fires the source, which is not armed.
  <pre>¬TIME_010 time010_untimed</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_mutant_fromStart_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): The step that stamps from the start kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-010, since `u`, newly armed at `T = 10` when the source fills its input place, gets the stamp `0` instead of `10`.
  <pre>TIME_010_mutant_fromStart (RefTStepW time010_fromStartPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_010 (RefTStepW time010_fromStartPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_mutant_rearmAny_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): The step that re-arms on any firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-010. From the reaped state, `w` fires and writes no place, and `u` is armed again.
  <pre>TIME_010_mutant_rearmAny (RefTStepW time010_rearmAnyPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_010 (RefTStepW time010_rearmAnyPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_mutant_resume_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): The resuming step kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-010. In AC2's state at `T = 15` the source fills `u`'s input place and `u` is armed again with its old stamp `10`, so it would be ready at `13` instead of `18`.
  <pre>TIME_010_mutant_resume (RefTStepW time010_resumePolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_010 (RefTStepW time010_resumePolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_010_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_010): Every line of TIME-010 is exercised by the reference step. AC1: at `T = 10` the transition `u` is not armed and cannot fire (line 5); the source fires and writes `u`'s input place, and `u` is newly armed (lines 1 and 3) with the stamp `10`; two milliseconds later it cannot fire (line 4, elapsed 2 below its earliest bound 3); three milliseconds later it can. A delay keeps the stamp (line 2). A reaped `u` at `T = 20`: `w` fires and writes none of its arc places, and `u` stays unarmed (line 1); the source fires and writes its input place, and `u` is armed again with the stamp `20` (lines 1 and 3).
  <pre>(∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ), ¬RefTStep time010_X time010_s0 (TLbl.fire time010_u d) s') ∧<br>  RefTStep time010_X time010_s0 (TLbl.fire time010_src [0]) (time010_after refPolicy) ∧<br>    ¬Armed time010_s0 time010_u ∧<br>      Armed (time010_after refPolicy) time010_u ∧<br>        (time010_after refPolicy).stamp "u" = some 10 ∧<br>          RefTStep time010_X (time010_after refPolicy) (TLbl.delay 2) (time010_later 2) ∧<br>            (time010_later 2).stamp "u" = some 10 ∧<br>              (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                  ¬RefTStep time010_X (time010_later 2) (TLbl.fire time010_u d) s') ∧<br>                RefTStep time010_X (time010_after refPolicy) (TLbl.delay 3)<br>                    (time010_later 3) ∧<br>                  (∃ s',<br>                      RefTStep time010_X (time010_later 3) (TLbl.fire time010_u []) s') ∧<br>                    Reaped time010_r0 time010_u ∧<br>                      RefTStep time010_Y time010_r0 (TLbl.fire time010_w [])<br>                          (time010_rAfter refPolicy time010_w []) ∧<br>                        ¬Armed (time010_rAfter refPolicy time010_w []) time010_u ∧<br>                          RefTStep time010_Y time010_r0 (TLbl.fire time010_src [0])<br>                              (time010_rAfter refPolicy time010_src [0]) ∧<br>                            Armed (time010_rAfter refPolicy time010_src [0]) time010_u ∧<br>                              (time010_rAfter refPolicy time010_src [0]).stamp "u" =<br>                                some 20</pre>

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_011"></a>
## TIME-011: Clock Restarts on Re-enablement

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `79f782cb6058365b`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| If a transition stops being armed and is later armed again, the clock restarts from zero.<br><br>The previous elapsed time is discarded.<br><br>*When* an armed transition stops being enabled, the executor *shall* discard its stamp.<br><br>*When* a transition that lost its clock is armed again, its elapsed time *shall* be measured from the moment it was armed again, not from any earlier stamp. | 1. For every timed net X:<br>2. a transition of the net that is armed before a step and is not enabled at the marking after it holds no stamp after the step;<br>3. a transition of the net that is not armed before a step and is armed after it holds the reading of the firing clock after the step. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N),<br>    (∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>        InNet X u →<br>          on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>            Armed s u → ¬Enabled u s'.m → s'.stamp u.core.name = none) ∧<br>      ∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>        InNet X u →<br>          on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>            ¬Armed s u → Armed s' u → s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic: the step that starts a firing completes it. Armed, Enabled and Elapsed time are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean): a transition is armed when it is enabled at the marking and holds a stamp.
- Line 1 is the first 'shall' sentence. 'Discard its stamp' is read on the state: the transition holds no stamp after the step (none), not merely that it is not armed, which a transition that is not enabled never is. The line is the hygiene of the state the sentence literally asks for: no glossary predicate reads the stamp of a transition that is not enabled (Armed, Ready and Expired all ask for it enabled), and a stale stamp cannot be resumed because line 2 overwrites it when the transition is armed again. TIME_011_mutant_disarm_killed shows the line is not implied by its weaker 'disarm' reading, and time011_lazy_TIME_010 that the stale-stamp step it kills satisfies TIME-010. The line reads only the marking after the step: a transition disabled in the intermediate marking of a firing and enabled again after it is TIME-012 (ruling D7), whose pre-6.0.0 mutant (keep a stamp when enabled before and after, the intermediate marking playing no part) TIME_012_mutant_survivesOld_killed kills.
- Line 2 is the second 'shall' sentence and the restart of the opening paragraph. A transition that is not armed before a step may still hold a stamp (a stale one, or none after a reap, [TIME-013]); no resume is possible because the line asks every such transition armed after the step to hold the current reading, overwriting whatever it held, so its elapsed time is 0 after the step and is measured from that reading. It is the stamp value of TIME-010 line 1 without TIME-010's question of which steps may arm (time011_line2_of_time010): next to TIME-010, line 1 is the only line this block adds (time011_iff_line1), and the resuming mutant it kills is the one TIME-010 kills too. Which steps re-arm (a firing that writes an arc place, ruling D6) is TIME-010, not this block.
- 'Measured from the moment it was armed again' over the later steps is TIME-010 line 2: a transition armed before and after a step keeps its stamp unless a firing restarts its clock ([TIME-012]). This block does not restate it: TIME-011 alone admits a delay that backdates the stamp of a transition that stays armed, which TIME-010 line 2 forbids (time011_backdate_gap). time011_elapsed_from_rearm derives the elapsed time after a delay from TIME-010 and TIME-011 together, which is how ConsistencyTime conjoins them.
- An injected token can also disable or re-arm a transition ([ENV-003], ruling D6). Injection is not a step of the Phase 1 relation, which has no environment places; the two lines are stated for the steps it has.
- Transitions are identified by name (ruling D10): a stamp is kept per name, and both lines speak of the transition the net holds under the name (InNet). Under pairwise distinct names (NamesDistinct) InNet is membership in the net (inNet_iff_mem).
- AC1 (Delayed(5 s), disabled at 3 s, re-enabled later, waits another 5 s) is TIME_011_nonvacuous in milliseconds: u is armed at 0, a consumer takes its token at 3000 and u holds no stamp (line 1), the source refills the place at 7000 and u is armed with the stamp 7000 (line 2), u cannot fire at 11999 and can at 12000. The resuming mutant would let u fire at 7000. For every relation satisfying TIME-010 and TIME-011 whose delay advances the clock by the delay, a transition armed again cannot fire after a delay shorter than its earliest bound (time011_no_early_fire).

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_011): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-011: a delay or a reap keeps the marking, so it disables nothing, and a firing that leaves a transition disabled neither keeps its stamp nor re-arms it, since `refPolicy`'s `keep` and `rearm` both ask for the transition enabled after the firing (line 1); a step newly arms a transition only as a firing, through `refPolicy.rearm`, with the current reading (`time010_newly`, line 2).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_011 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011_forbids_frozen` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_011): ✗ A firing that leaves every stamp in place violates TIME-011 (line 1): at `T = 3000` the consumer takes `u`'s token, and `u`, disabled, still holds the stamp `0`.
  <pre>¬TIME_011 time011_frozen</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011_mutant_disarm_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_011): The step that keeps stale stamps kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, since a transition armed again still gets the current reading, but not TIME-011, since at `T = 3000` the consumer's firing leaves `u` disabled with the stamp `0`.
  <pre>TIME_011_mutant_disarm (RefTStepW time011_lazyPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_011 (RefTStepW time011_lazyPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011_mutant_resume_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_011): The resuming step (`time010_resumePolicy`: every stamp a transition other than the fired one holds is kept, armed or not) kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-011. In AC1, `u` keeps its stamp `0` while the consumer's firing leaves it disabled, and when the source refills its place at `T = 7000` it is armed again with the stamp `0`, so it could fire at once instead of at `T = 12000`.
  <pre>TIME_011_mutant_resume (RefTStepW time010_resumePolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_011 (RefTStepW time010_resumePolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_011_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_011): Both lines of TIME-011 are exercised by the reference step, on AC1 in milliseconds. `u` (`Delayed(5000)`) is armed at `T = 0` with the stamp `0`. At `T = 3000` the consumer takes its token: `u` was armed and is no longer enabled, and holds no stamp (line 1). At `T = 7000` the source refills the place: `u` was not armed and is armed again with the stamp `7000` (line 2). It cannot fire at `T = 11999` (elapsed 4999) and can at `T = 12000`: the full delay applies again.
  <pre>Armed time011_a0 time011_u ∧<br>  time011_a0.stamp "u" = some 0 ∧<br>    RefTStep time011_X time011_a0 (TLbl.delay 3000) time011_a1 ∧<br>      RefTStep time011_X time011_a1 (TLbl.fire time011_c []) (time011_a2 refPolicy) ∧<br>        Armed time011_a1 time011_u ∧<br>          ¬Enabled time011_u (time011_a2 refPolicy).m ∧<br>            (time011_a2 refPolicy).stamp "u" = none ∧<br>              RefTStep time011_X (time011_a2 refPolicy) (TLbl.delay 4000)<br>                  (time011_a3 refPolicy) ∧<br>                RefTStep time011_X (time011_a3 refPolicy) (TLbl.fire time011_src [0])<br>                    (time011_a4 refPolicy) ∧<br>                  ¬Armed (time011_a3 refPolicy) time011_u ∧<br>                    Armed (time011_a4 refPolicy) time011_u ∧<br>                      (time011_a4 refPolicy).stamp "u" = some 7000 ∧<br>                        RefTStep time011_X (time011_a4 refPolicy) (TLbl.delay 4999)<br>                            (time011_later 4999) ∧<br>                          (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                              ¬RefTStep time011_X (time011_later 4999)<br>                                  (TLbl.fire time011_u d) s') ∧<br>                            RefTStep time011_X (time011_a4 refPolicy) (TLbl.delay 5000)<br>                                (time011_later 5000) ∧<br>                              ∃ s',<br>                                RefTStep time011_X (time011_later 5000)<br>                                  (TLbl.fire time011_u []) s'</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_012"></a>
## TIME-012: Clock Restart on Intermediate Disablement

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `7567794250d69169`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| The marking between the halves is the intermediate marking `M - Pre(t)` of Berthomieu and Diaz.<br><br>This holds even when `t`'s own outputs refill the place, and it holds whether `t`'s action completes synchronously or asynchronously: a synchronous action MUST NOT hide the gap.<br><br>The intermediate marking comes from the live marking `t` fires from: the tokens in the places when `t` fires, minus what `t` consumes.<br><br>*When* a transition `t` fires, each armed transition other than `t` that is not enabled in the intermediate marking *shall* lose its clock.<br><br>*When* a transition `t` fires, each armed transition other than `t` that is enabled in the intermediate marking and after the firing *shall* keep its stamp.<br><br>*When* a transition fires, it *shall* lose its clock.<br><br>*When* a transition `t` fires and a transition that lost its clock in that firing is enabled after it, that transition *shall* be newly armed ([TIME-010]). | 1. For every timed net X:<br>2. a firing of t from a state in which the net's transition u, named differently from t, is armed and not enabled in the intermediate marking of t (taken from the state's marking) leaves u armed with the reading of the firing clock after the firing if u is enabled after it, and leaves u without a stamp otherwise;<br>3. a firing of t from a state in which the net's transition u, named differently from t, is armed and enabled in the intermediate marking of t, and after which u is enabled, leaves u's stamp as it was;<br>4. a firing of t from a state in which the net's transition u named like t (t itself when t is the net's transition of its name) is armed leaves u armed with the reading of the firing clock after the firing if u is enabled after it, and leaves u without a stamp otherwise. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N),<br>    (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>        InNet X u →<br>          u.core.name ≠ t.core.name →<br>            on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>              Armed s u →<br>                ¬Enabled u (Mid t s.m) →<br>                  (Enabled u s'.m →<br>                      Armed s' u ∧ s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')) ∧<br>                    (¬Enabled u s'.m → s'.stamp u.core.name = none)) ∧<br>      (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>          InNet X u →<br>            u.core.name ≠ t.core.name →<br>              on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                Armed s u →<br>                  Enabled u (Mid t s.m) →<br>                    Enabled u s'.m → s'.stamp u.core.name = s.stamp u.core.name) ∧<br>        ∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>          InNet X u →<br>            u.core.name = t.core.name →<br>              on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                Armed s u →<br>                  (Enabled u s'.m →<br>                      Armed s' u ∧ s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')) ∧<br>                    (¬Enabled u s'.m → s'.stamp u.core.name = none)</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic: the step that starts a firing completes it. Armed, Mid (the intermediate marking: inputs consumed, reset places drained, nothing deposited) and Enabled are the glossary terms.
- The intermediate marking is computed from the marking of the state the firing step starts from, which is the live marking (ruling D7, revised). In a firing pass of several firings each firing is its own atomic step, so a token a synchronous action deposited earlier in the pass is in that marking and counts, as the section says. This agrees with a pass only for arcs that take a fixed number of tokens: a draining arc (reset, all(), atLeast(n)) later in a pass takes only the tokens present when the pass began ([EXEC-003] AC5), while Mid drains the live place. The section's bullet on reset places now carries the exception for this case (ruling 2): a same-pass deposit that the reset does not take ([EXEC-003] AC5) and that keeps the transition enabled in the intermediate marking keeps its clock. The Phase 1 relation has no pass, so a same-pass deposit never arises in it, and Mid draining the whole live place is the bullet without its exception; the exception, and an intermediate marking that keeps a deposit the reset does not take, are deferred to Phase 2 (RefExec). Outputs of an asynchronous action still in flight do not exist in the Phase 1 relation, which has no asynchronous actions. The enablement recheck of the pass ([EXEC-003] AC3-AC5) is not part of this block; see the next line. A synchronous action does not hide the gap: in the atomic Phase 1 step its deposit is the step's deposit d, which Mid t s.m never sees, so line 1 applies to a refill by the firing's own outputs (AC1). The asynchronous half is AC5, deferred below.
- The fifth 'shall' sentence, 'If the enablement recheck of a firing pass finds a transition disabled ([EXEC-003] AC3), then the transition shall lose its clock, even when a same-pass deposit keeps it enabled in the live marking.', and the body's sentences on it (a transition the recheck finds disabled is disarmed even when a same-pass deposit refilled its place, and is newly armed, with a fresh stamp, by a later enablement update that finds it enabled) are not encoded and not quoted (ruling 1). The recheck judges a transition on the marking of the pass, which the atomic Phase 1 relation does not have: each firing is its own step and sees the live marking. Deferred to Phase 2 (RefExec), with the order of the two rules inside one pass: the intermediate-marking rule is applied at each firing on the live marking, and the recheck afterwards on the pass's marking, so a transition can keep its stamp through a firing by sentence 2 and then lose its clock to the recheck later in the same pass. The two rules are applied in sequence, they do not contradict each other.
- To lose a clock is to hold no stamp after the firing. Sentences 1 and 4 together are line 1 of the formula: an armed transition other than t that is not enabled in the intermediate marking holds no stamp after the firing, unless it is enabled after it, in which case it is newly armed, that is armed with the current reading of the firing clock ([TIME-010]). Sentences 3 and 4 together are line 3, for the fired transition itself; it presupposes, as sentence 4 does, a clock to lose, so it speaks of the transition armed before the firing. An unarmed transition does not fire ([TIME-010] line 5). Line 2 is sentence 2. An armed transition enabled in the intermediate marking but not after the firing is disabled by the firing's deposit; that case is [TIME-011], not this block.
- Transitions are identified by name (ruling D10): 'other than t' is 'named differently from t', and lines 1-3 speak of the transition the net holds under the name (InNet). Line 3 picks it by t's name, so a fired t that is not the net's transition of its name (a foreign or duplicate-named t) still restarts the clock under that name. The section assumes pairwise distinct names (NamesDistinct), under which InNet is membership in the net.
- Bullet 1 (removing tokens never disables through an inhibitor arc) is time012_inhibitor_never: a transition enabled before whose input and read arcs still hold in the intermediate marking is enabled there. Its ν-join clause is not encoded: the Phase 1 relation has no ν correlation, it belongs to the NU blocks.
- Bullet 2 (a reset arc drains its place) is time012_reset_disables, read as an input arc with a positive required count or a read arc on a reset place of t; with line 1 such a transition restarts its clock. Its exception for a same-pass deposit the reset does not take is Phase 2, as said above. An input arc whose required count is 0 stays enabled in a drained place and keeps its clock (the time012_Tzero conjuncts of TIME_012_ac3). Bullet 3 (surplus tokens keep the clock) is AC4. Bullet 4 (the fired transition always starts a fresh clock) is line 3.
- AC1, AC2, AC3 and AC4 are the examples TIME_012_ac1 (input refill), TIME_012_ac2 (read only), TIME_012_ac3 (reset drain with a positive requirement) and TIME_012_ac4 (surplus keeps), in milliseconds, with T Delayed(5) and the refresh transition Immediate. TIME-012 fixes only the stamp a firing leaves; AC1's lower bound (T fires no earlier than d after the refill) follows from TIME-012 together with [TIME-010] lines 2 and 4, which frame the stamps across the other steps and gate the firing. TIME_012_ac1 shows the bound for the reference step: T does not fire 4 ms after the refill. AC5 (an asynchronous action) and AC6 (TransitionClockRestarted or TransitionEnabled) need in-flight actions and the event log, and are deferred to the Phase 2 executor; AC4's event clause (no TransitionClockRestarted or TransitionEnabled for T) needs the event log and is deferred with AC6. The state class graph's persistence rule ([VER-010]) is in Novel/TimedScg/Run.lean: persistence_rule, fired_never_persists, reset_restarts and surplus_keeps cite this ID.
- A firing is instantaneous on the firing clock: that frame is not a sentence of this section and is not in the statement. Without it a firing fused with a hidden delay satisfies TIME-012 (TIME_012_gap_fireAdvancesClock); the frame is time012_FramesClock, which the reference satisfies (TIME_012_reference_framesClock) and which ConsistencyTime conjoins.
- Injected tokens are not steps of the Phase 1 relation, which has no environment places, so injection plays no part here.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-012: `refPolicy` keeps the stamp of an armed transition other than the fired one exactly when it is enabled in the intermediate marking (`mid`, which is the glossary's `Mid`) and after the firing (line 2), and otherwise re-arms an armed transition with the current reading when it is enabled after the firing and drops its stamp when it is not (lines 1 and 3; the fired transition is armed, since it fires).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_012 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_forbids_firedKeeps` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): ✗ A firing transition that keeps its clock violates TIME-012 (line 3): in AC1 the refresh `r` fires at `T = 10`, is enabled after, and keeps its stamp `0` instead of restarting at `10`.
  <pre>¬TIME_012 (RefTStepW time012_firedKeepsPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_forbids_restartAll` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): ✗ Restarting every clock on every firing violates TIME-012 (line 2): in AC4 the refresh leaves `T` enabled in the intermediate marking and after the firing, and `T` gets the stamp `10` instead of keeping `0`.
  <pre>¬TIME_012 (RefTStepW time012_restartAllPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_mutant_noReset_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): The step that ignores the resets kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-012. In AC3 `z` drains `T`'s input place and puts a token back; without the drain `T` looks enabled in the intermediate marking and keeps its stamp `0` instead of restarting at `10`.
  <pre>TIME_012_mutant_noReset (RefTStepW time012_noResetPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_012 (RefTStepW time012_noResetPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_mutant_survivesOld_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): The pre-6.0.0 step kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-012. In AC1 the refresh refills `T`'s input place in one firing; `T` is enabled before and after, so it keeps its stamp `0` and would be ready at `5` instead of `15`.
  <pre>TIME_012_mutant_survivesOld (RefTStepW time012_survivesOldPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_012 (RefTStepW time012_survivesOldPolicy)</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_012_ac1` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_012): AC1 (input refill): `T` consumes from place `0`, holding its one token, armed since `0`. At `T = 10` the refresh `r` takes the token and puts one back. `T` is not enabled in the intermediate marking and restarts its clock at `10`; the refresh itself restarts too (line 3). With `Delayed(5)`, `T` cannot fire 4 ms after the refill, and can 5 ms after it. Without the refill (`c` takes the token) `T` and `c` hold no stamp.

<a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_013"></a>
## TIME-013: Deadline Enforcement

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013` · Kind **T** · Scope all executors · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `cf60477e972fe310`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| When a reapable transition is expired, that is, armed with an elapsed time strictly greater than its latest bound plus the deadline tolerance, the executor reaps it: it disarms the transition without firing it and emits a `TransitionTimedOut` event.<br><br>Implementations MUST apply a deadline tolerance — a grace band beyond `latest` (default 5ms) that absorbs timer-resolution and scheduling jitter — and SHOULD expose it as a configurable per-executor option (e.g. `deadlineTolerance` / `deadline_tolerance_ms`); a value of `0` gives strict enforcement.<br><br>Only `Deadline` and `Window` transitions are reaped here.<br><br>*If* a reapable transition's elapsed time is greater than its latest bound plus the deadline tolerance, *then* the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>The executor *shall not* reap a transition that is not reapable.<br><br>*While* a transition is reaped, the transition *shall not* fire.<br><br>*If* a reapable transition is armed and its elapsed time is greater than its latest bound plus the deadline tolerance, *then* the executor *shall* reap it in the deadline step of that cycle ([EXEC-001] step 4) and emit `TransitionTimedOut` ([EVT-009]).<br><br>*When* a firing or an injected token writes one of a reaped transition's arc places (an input, read, inhibitor or reset place) and the transition is enabled afterwards, the transition *shall* be newly armed with a fresh clock ([TIME-010]).<br><br>A firing writes a place when it consumes from it, deposits into it or drains it through a reset arc; draining a reset place that is already empty counts as a write.<br><br>A reaped transition keeps its input tokens and is not armed again until one of its arc places is written, so an executor that fell behind can come to rest at a marking that still enables it. | 1. For every timed net X:<br>2. a reap of u is of a transition of the net, is taken only from a state in which u is expired, and leaves u reaped, the marking, the reading of the firing clock and the stamp of every other name as they were;<br>3. no transition fires from a state in which it is reapable and its elapsed time is greater than its latest bound plus the tolerance;<br>4. no transition is reaped from a state in which it is not reapable;<br>5. no transition fires from a state in which it is reaped;<br>6. a step that leaves the net's transition u reaped, from a state in which u is armed, is a reap of a transition of u's name;<br>7. a step that leaves the net's transition u armed, from a state in which u is reaped, is a firing that writes one of u's arc places;<br>8. a firing that writes an arc place of the net's transition u, from a state in which u is reaped, leaves u armed with the reading of the firing clock if u is enabled after it;<br>9. a firing is of a transition of the net;<br>10. from every state in which a transition of the net is expired, the step relation has a reap of it. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N),<br>    (∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>        on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.reap u) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>          u ∈ X.net.transitions ∧<br>            Expired X s u ∧<br>              Reaped s' u ∧<br>                s'.m = s.m ∧<br>                  ClockReading s' = ClockReading s ∧<br>                    ∀ (n : String), n ≠ u.core.name → s'.stamp n = s.stamp n) ∧<br>      (∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>          while fun s ↦ Reapable X u ∧ (TimingOf X u).latest + Tolerance X &lt; Elapsed s u,<br>            never fun s s' ↦ ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>        (∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>            while fun x ↦ ¬Reapable X u, never fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.reap u) s') ∧<br>          (∀ (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>              while fun s ↦ Reaped s u, never fun s s' ↦ ∃ d, R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>            (∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>                InNet X u →<br>                  on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                    Armed s u →<br>                      Reaped s' u → ∃ v, l = TLbl.reap v ∧ v.core.name = u.core.name) ∧<br>              (∀ (l : TLbl N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>                  InNet X u →<br>                    on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                      Reaped s u →<br>                        Armed s' u →<br>                          ∃ t d, l = TLbl.fire t d ∧ ∃ p, ArcPlace u p ∧ Writes t d p) ∧<br>                (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>                    InNet X u →<br>                      on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>                        Reaped s u →<br>                          (∃ p, ArcPlace u p ∧ Writes t d p) →<br>                            Enabled u s'.m →<br>                              Armed s' u ∧<br>                                s'.stamp u.core.name = some (ClockReading s')) ∧<br>                  (∀ (t : Reference.Trans N) (d : List N),<br>                      on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.fire t d) s', shall fun x x_1 ↦<br>                        t ∈ X.net.transitions) ∧<br>                    ∀ (u : Reference.Trans N) (s : TSt N),<br>                      u ∈ X.net.transitions → Expired X s u → ∃ s', R X s (TLbl.reap u) s'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). Firings are atomic. Reapable, Expired, Reaped, Armed, Elapsed time, Arc place and Write are the glossary terms (GlossaryTime.lean); Reaped is the state reading, enabled at the marking and not armed.
- Line 1 reads the section's first paragraph as the definition of a reap step: it is taken only from an expired transition (reapable, armed, elapsed time strictly greater than its latest bound plus the tolerance, ruling D4), so the tolerance is applied, and it disarms that transition and nothing else, without firing it: the marking ("keeps its input tokens"), the clock reading and every other stamp stay. Several reaps in one deadline step are several reap steps.
- Line 9 is the 'shall' sentence on reaping, and the first quoted sentence's 'the executor reaps it', as far as one atomic step can state them: from every state in which a transition of the net is expired, a reap of it is a step (the same condition as CoversReap, for this block; TIME-003 and TIME-005 state it for their timings). The step relation has no cycles and no events, so that the reap is taken, in the deadline step of the very cycle ([EXEC-001] step 4), and emits TransitionTimedOut ([EVT-009]) are deferred to Phase 2 and EVT. The atomic relation also lets another transition fire while u is expired (RefTStep does), and that firing may restart u's clock under [TIME-012], which a real cycle cannot do because its deadline step reaps before its firing pass; ClashesTime records this as a Phase 2 obligation (clash013_refresh_rescue).
- The tolerance is the field TimedNet.tol, any natural number of milliseconds; the statement holds for every tolerance. Its default of 5 ms, the value 0 for strict enforcement and its exposure as a per-executor option (AC2) are configuration (kind N) and are not encoded.
- Line 5 ties the state reading Reaped to the act of reaping: an armed transition becomes enabled and unarmed only by a reap of it, so a delay or a firing that silently drops the clock of an enabled transition is not a reap. Transitions are identified by name (ruling D10, InNet): the section assumes pairwise distinct names (NamesDistinct), and the reap of line 5 is of a transition of u's name.
- Lines 1 and 8 make the transition of a reap or a firing label one of the net's. Without them a label outside the net that shares a name with a net transition but has another timing could reap or fire under that name, and lines 1 to 4 would check the label's timing, not the net's. Under distinct names a label of the net with u's name is u itself (time013_label_eq), so lines 1 to 5 speak of the net's transition (TIME_013_reap_is_of_u). Nets with two transitions of one name are outside ruling D10: the reference step itself then shares one stamp between them, so the statement does not speak to them.
- Line 3 follows from line 1, as Expired includes Reapable (time013_line3_of_line1); it is kept so the quoted sentence on not reaping a non-reapable transition maps to one line.
- Lines 6 and 7 are TIME-010's lines 1 and 3 read for a reaped transition (with line 1's fresh stamp in line 7), and are proven from the same policy-generic lemmas. A write is the glossary's Write (ruling D6): any arc place, not only an input place, and draining an empty reset place counts. The injected-token case of the last quoted 'shall' sentence is outside the step relation: injection is not a step of the Phase 1 relation, which has no environment places.
- AC1 (a Deadline(5s) transition still armed past 5 s plus the tolerance does not fire after that bound; if it is still armed on the first cycle whose elapsed time is strictly greater than the bound, it is reaped in that cycle and TransitionTimedOut is emitted) is the nonvacuous example TIME_013_nonvacuous in milliseconds with tolerance 5: not reaped at elapsed 5005, reaped at 5006, and not fireable then. That the reap falls in the first such cycle is the cycle loop of Phase 2 (RefExec, [EXEC-001] step 4), and the TransitionTimedOut event is [EVT-009], deferred with it. AC3 (an Exact transition past its target is not disabled) is the same example: the Exact(3) transition, at elapsed 5006, cannot be reaped and fires. AC4 (a reaped transition does not fire until an arc place is written, then is armed again with a fresh clock and may fire) is the same example after the reap, ending with a firing of the re-armed transition; the backends (Bitmap, Precompiled) are Phase 2.
- The paragraph on verification (quiescence reads a marking whose enabled transitions are all reapable as quiescent, Route B drops the latest bounds, assumeNoReaping) belongs to [VER-002], [VER-004] and [VER-012]; it is cited by ReapAware.rest_sound, not encoded here.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-013: `refPolicy` reaps only an expired transition (`tExpired`, which asks for a reapable timing, an armed transition and an elapsed time strictly past the latest bound plus the tolerance), and the reap drops that stamp alone (lines 1 and 3); a firing needs its transition armed and not expired (lines 2 and 4); a firing keeps or restarts the clock of every armed transition enabled after it (line 5); and a reaped transition is armed again only through `rearm`, which asks for a written arc place, with the current reading (lines 6 and 7); a firing is of a transition of the net (line 8); and an expired transition of the net has a reap (line 9).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_013 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_forbids_consumingReap` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): ✗ A reap that consumes tokens violates TIME-013 (line 1): reaping `u` at `T = 5006` takes the token of its input place, so `u` is no longer enabled, not reaped.
  <pre>¬TIME_013 time013_consumingReap</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_forbids_phantomFire` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): ✗ A firing of a label outside the net violates TIME-013 (line 8): at `T = 0` the net's `u` fires, and the same firing is taken under the label `time013_ghostU`.
  <pre>¬TIME_013 time013_phantomFire</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_forbids_phantomReap` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): ✗ A reap of a label outside the net violates TIME-013 (line 1). At `T = 5006` the expired ghost of `w` is reaped, which drops the stamp of the name `w` and so leaves the net's `Exact(3)` transition `w`, not reapable, reaped.
  <pre>¬TIME_013 time013_phantomReap</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_forbids_silentReap` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): ✗ A delay that reaps silently violates TIME-013 (line 5): the delay from `T = 5005` to `5006` leaves `u`, armed before it, reaped, and a delay is not a reap.

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_mutant_noTolerance_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): The step that reaps with no tolerance kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-013. At `T = 5001` the elapsed time of `u` is past `5000` but not past `5000 + 5`, and `u` is reaped.

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_mutant_reapAt_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): The step that reaps at the bound kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-013. At `T = 5005` the elapsed time of `u` is `5000 + 5`, not past it, and `u` is reaped.

At `T = 5006` the `Exact(3)` transition `w` is past its target plus the tolerance and is reaped (AC3 says it is not).

- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_mutant_rearmAny_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): The step that re-arms on any firing (TIME-010's `time010_rearmAnyPolicy`, the pre-ruling precompiled behaviour of `deadline_reap_dirty_diverges`) kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-013. - ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_013_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_013): Every line of TIME-013 is exercised by the reference step, in milliseconds with tolerance 5. AC1: at `T = 5005` the `Deadline(5000)` transition `u` is armed since `0` and cannot be reaped (line 1, the tolerance band); a delay of 1 makes it expired, and it cannot fire (line 2). <a id="Libpetri-Spec-TIME_015"></a>
## TIME-015: Injectable Clock

`Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015` · Kind **A** · Scope all executors, on the default or an injected firing clock · Source **verbatim** (spec/03-timing.md) · Sign-off **pending** · Statement hash `1fe98378e952e8b1`

| Natural language | Plain reading | Lean statement |
|---|---|---|
| *While* an executor runs on an injected clock, successive readings of its firing clock *shall* be non-decreasing.<br><br>*When* the executor's wait completes, the executor *shall* re-check its boundary conditions before it fires any transition, so an armed transition *shall not* fire before its earliest bound because a wait completed early ([TIME-010]). | 1. For every timed net X:<br>2. no step lowers the reading of the firing clock;<br>3. a delay of δ advances the reading by exactly δ;<br>4. no transition fires from the state a delay of any length reached if, read with the stamps held before the delay, it is armed there and its elapsed time is below its earliest bound;<br>5. no transition fires from the state a delay of any length reached if, read with the stamps held before the delay, it is expired there. | <pre>fun {N} [DecidableEq N] R ↦<br>  ∀ (X : TimedNet N),<br>    (∀ (l : TLbl N),<br>        on fun s s' ↦ R X s l s', shall fun s s' ↦ ClockReading s ≤ ClockReading s') ∧<br>      (∀ (δ : ℕ),<br>          on fun s s' ↦ R X s (TLbl.delay δ) s', shall fun s s' ↦<br>            ClockReading s' = ClockReading s + δ) ∧<br>        (∀ (δ : ℕ) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>            on fun s₀ s ↦ R X s₀ (TLbl.delay δ) s, shall fun s₀ s ↦<br>              Armed (AfterWait s₀ s) u →<br>                Elapsed (AfterWait s₀ s) u &lt; (TimingOf X u).earliest →<br>                  ∀ (d : List N) (s' : TSt N), ¬R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s') ∧<br>          ∀ (δ : ℕ) (u : Reference.Trans N),<br>            on fun s₀ s ↦ R X s₀ (TLbl.delay δ) s, shall fun s₀ s ↦<br>              Expired X (AfterWait s₀ s) u →<br>                ∀ (d : List N) (s' : TSt N), ¬R X s (TLbl.fire u d) s'</pre> |

**Assumptions**

- A step relates two states of the executor (glossary: TSt), each holding a marking, the reading of the firing clock (ClockReading) and a stamp per transition name; a step is a delay of the clock, a firing or a reap (TRel). The relation has one firing clock and does not say where its readings come from, so the block holds of the default clock as of an injected one. The epoch clock (token creation times, [CORE-011]) is not modelled: the Phase 1 marking is token counts. Injected tokens are not steps of the Phase 1 relation.
- Line 1 of the formula is the first 'shall' sentence, over every step. A host reading that goes backwards cannot be written as a delay, since a delay's length is a natural number: across a wait, the sentence is carried by that type together with line 2, and a clock that rewinds on a delay breaks lines 1 and 2 at once (TIME_015_forbids_rewind). Line 1 on its own constrains firings and reaps: one that lowers the reading breaks it alone (TIME_015_mutant_waitOnly_killed).
- Line 2 says a delay moves the clock by its length; it is the only statement of that fact, and TIME-003 to TIME-006 rely on it through ConsistencyTime for the elapsed time of their transitions to grow with the clock (their clock frame keeps the stamp across a delay, GlossaryTime ClockFrame). That a firing and a reap take no time (an atomic step happens at one reading) is a convention of the Phase 1 relation, not a sentence of this section, so it is not a line of this block; its firing half is time012_FramesClock, which ConsistencyTime conjoins, and its reap half is [TIME-013] line 1 (a reap leaves the clock reading as it is). Which reading stamps a transition armed by a firing is not fixed by the spec text and is open: the model takes the firing step's own reading (refPolicy.fresh), while a runtime that samples the clock once per cycle stamps it with the reading of the next cycle's enablement update, and an asynchronous action's outputs arm at its completion reading ([TIME-012] AC5, deferred).
- Contract 1's other half (the clock is not assumed to advance between two reads) is not a line of this block. A repeated reading is a delay of 0; that one is offered is CoversDelay, and that it changes nothing else is TIME-010's framing of stamps across a delay together with FramesMarking. The reference meets it (time015_stutter).
- Lines 3 and 4 are the second 'shall' sentence. A completed wait is a delay step of any length: the executor cannot tell an early or spurious completion from a due one, so both lines quantify over every delay. The boundary conditions are read in AfterWait: the marking and reading after the wait with the stamps held before it, so that a wait that rewrites or drops stamps cannot hide an early firing (TIME_015_mutant_inState_killed, TIME_015_forbids_dropStamps). For a relation that keeps stamps across a delay, as TIME-010 line 2 asks, AfterWait is the state the wait reached.
- Line 3 is the earliest side, which the sentence names. Line 4 is the latest side of 'its boundary conditions': a transition that is expired (glossary: reapable, armed, elapsed time strictly greater than its latest bound plus the deadline tolerance; rulings D2 and D4) does not fire after a wait that overshot. Exact transitions are not reapable and are never expired [TIME-006]. With TIME-010 and FramesMarking, line 3 holds of every relation (time015_ac4_of_time010, cited for AC4).
- AC6: the reap test, that with tolerance 0 a Deadline transition can be reaped exactly when it is armed and its elapsed time is strictly greater than its bound, is the cited theorem time015_ac6_reap_strict (the strict test of TIME-013, ruling D4). That the reap happens on the first such cycle is a property of the executor loop and is deferred to Phase 2.
- AC10 is not encoded: its ruling (D12) is open. The clash is recorded, and cited for TIME-015, with the other TIME clashes (ClashesTime.lean): if its 'same outcome' quantifies over strictly increasing clocks that may skip a window, the reference refutes it (time015_ac10_coarse_jump: a Window(3, 5) transition with tolerance 0 fires under a clock advancing 1 ms per reading, and is reaped, its token stranded, under the readings 0 then 10); repeated readings alone change nothing (refTStepW_delay_zero: a delay of 0 is an identity step; time015_stutter here is the converse, that such a step exists).
- AC1, AC2, AC3, AC9, AC12, AC14 and AC15 are properties of the seam, its default path, its documentation and its identifiers (configuration, N kind), not of a step relation. AC5, AC7, AC8 and AC11 are properties of the executor loop (abort, the boundary short-circuit, wake-up, reentrant admission) and AC13 of token stamping through the epoch clock; they are deferred to Phase 2.

**Evidence**

- ✓ **holds** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_reference` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The timed reference step satisfies TIME-015: a delay advances the reading by its length, a firing and a reap leave it as it is, so no step lowers it (lines 1 and 2); a delay keeps the stamps, so the state it reaches is its own `AfterWait`, and a firing needs its transition past `refPolicy`'s earliest-bound gate and not expired in the state it fires from (lines 3 and 4).
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N], TIME_015 RefTStep</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_forbids_dropStamps` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): ✗ A wait that forgets every clock violates TIME-015 (line 3). Read in the state the wait reached, nothing is armed, so the boundary check there passes vacuously; read with the stamps held before the wait, `w` (`Window(2, 3)`, armed at `0`) has elapsed time `1` after a wait of `1`, below its earliest bound `2`, and it fires.
  <pre>¬TIME_015 time015_dropStamps</pre>
- ✗ **forbids** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_forbids_rewind` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): ✗ A clock that runs backwards on a wait violates TIME-015, lines 1 and 2 at once: a delay of `1` at reading `5` reads `4`.
  <pre>¬TIME_015 time015_rewind</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_mutant_earliestOnly_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The executor that ignores expiry kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-015. A wait of `4` overshoots `w`'s window (`Window(2, 3)`, armed at `0`, tolerance 0): `w` is expired at reading `4` and still fires there.
  <pre>TIME_015_mutant_earliestOnly (RefTStepW time015_ignoreExpiryPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_015 (RefTStepW time015_ignoreExpiryPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_mutant_hostNow_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The rewinding clock kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, since its delays keep the stamps and its firings are the reference's, but not TIME-015.
  <pre>TIME_015_mutant_hostNow time015_rewind ∧ ¬TIME_015 time015_rewind</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_mutant_inState_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The stamp-shifting wait kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-015. `w` (`Window(2, 3)`) is armed at reading `1` with stamp `1`; a wait of `1` reaches reading `2` and moves the stamp to `0`, so `w` fires there, `1` ms after its stamp, below its earliest bound `2`.
  <pre>TIME_015_mutant_inState time015_shiftStamps ∧ ¬TIME_015 time015_shiftStamps</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_mutant_trustWait_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The executor that trusts its wait kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-015. A wait that completes early, at reading `1`, wakes it, and `w` fires with elapsed time `1`, below its earliest bound `2`.
  <pre>TIME_015_mutant_trustWait (RefTStepW time015_trustWaitPolicy) ∧<br>  ¬TIME_015 (RefTStepW time015_trustWaitPolicy)</pre>
- ✗ **mutant** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_mutant_waitOnly_killed` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): The stale firing kills the mutant: it satisfies the mutant reading, but not TIME-015 (line 1 alone), since `w`, ready at reading `2`, fires and leaves the clock at `1`.
  <pre>TIME_015_mutant_waitOnly time015_staleFire ∧ ¬TIME_015 time015_staleFire</pre>
- ✓ **nonvacuous** `Libpetri.Spec.TIME_015_nonvacuous` (Libpetri.Spec.Req.TIME_015): Every line of TIME-015 is exercised by the reference step, in the net of `w` (`Window(2, 3)`) and `k` (`Deadline(3)`), both armed at reading `0`, tolerance 0. A delay of `1` reads `1` (lines 1 and 2); a wait that completes there finds `w` armed with elapsed time `1`, below its earliest bound `2`, and `w` does not fire (line 3). A delay of `2` reads `2`, where `w` fires and the reading stays `2` (line 1 for a firing). A delay of `4` overshoots `w`'s window: `w` is expired at reading `4` and does not fire (line 4), and `k`, expired too, is reaped and the reading stays `4` (line 1 for a reap). A delay of `0` stays where it is, since readings may repeat.
  <pre>RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 0) (TLbl.delay 1) (time015_at 1) ∧<br>  ClockReading (time015_at 1) = ClockReading (time015_at 0) + 1 ∧<br>    Armed (AfterWait (time015_at 0) (time015_at 1)) time015_w ∧<br>      Elapsed (AfterWait (time015_at 0) (time015_at 1)) time015_w &lt;<br>          (TimingOf time015_X time015_w).earliest ∧<br>        (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>            ¬RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 1) (TLbl.fire time015_w d) s') ∧<br>          RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 0) (TLbl.delay 2) (time015_at 2) ∧<br>            RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 2) (TLbl.fire time015_w [])<br>                (time015_fired refPolicy 2) ∧<br>              ClockReading (time015_fired refPolicy 2) = ClockReading (time015_at 2) ∧<br>                RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 0) (TLbl.delay 4) (time015_at 4) ∧<br>                  Expired time015_X (AfterWait (time015_at 0) (time015_at 4)) time015_w ∧<br>                    (∀ (d : List ℕ) (s' : TSt ℕ),<br>                        ¬RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 4) (TLbl.fire time015_w d) s') ∧<br>                      RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 4) (TLbl.reap time015_k)<br>                          (have __src := time015_at 4;<br>                          { m := __src.m, now := __src.now,<br>                            stamp := fun n ↦<br>                              if n = "k" then none else (time015_at 4).stamp n }) ∧<br>                        RefTStep time015_X (time015_at 4) (TLbl.delay 0) (time015_at 4)</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_coreIO_time` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyTime): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002 and the TIME blocks over the timed step relation hold together on one model: one firing relation `F` for the CORE and IO blocks, whose firings are the firings of the timed step relation `R` of the TIME blocks; a timing contract whose bounds are the `Timing` accessors the TIME blocks read; and a ready order for every pass input. The witnesses are the reference firing relation, the reference timing contract, the reference ready order and the timed reference step, whose firings take no time on the firing clock, whose delays and reaps leave the marking as it is, and which covers every ready firing, every expired reap and every delay.
  <pre>∀ {N : Type} [inst : DecidableEq N] [inst_1 : ToString N],<br>  ∃ Build F T Q O R,<br>    (CORE_022 F ∧<br>        CORE_030 F ∧<br>          CORE_031 F ∧<br>            CORE_032 F ∧<br>              CORE_033 F ∧<br>                CORE_034 F ∧<br>                  CORE_035 F ∧<br>                    CORE_036 F ∧<br>                      IO_001 F ∧<br>                        IO_002 F ∧<br>                          IO_003 F ∧<br>                            IO_004 F ∧<br>                              IO_005 F ∧<br>                                IO_010 F ∧<br>                                  IO_011 Build F ∧<br>                                    IO_012 IO_012_check Build F ∧<br>                                      IO_013 Build F T ∧ IO_014 Build T) ∧<br>      ((∃ K, IO_007 K) ∧ (∃ V, IO_015 V) ∧ ∃ E, IO_016 E) ∧<br>        (TIME_001 Q ∧<br>            Q.earliest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.earliest ∧<br>              Q.latest = Novel.TimedScg.Timing.latest) ∧<br>          EXEC_002 O ∧<br>            ProjectsTo R F ∧<br>              FramesMarking R ∧<br>                time012_FramesClock R ∧<br>                  Covers R ∧<br>                    CoversReap R ∧<br>                      CoversDelay R ∧<br>                        TIME_002 R ∧<br>                          TIME_003 R ∧<br>                            TIME_004 R ∧<br>                              TIME_005 R ∧<br>                                TIME_006 R ∧<br>                                  TIME_010 R ∧<br>                                    TIME_011 R ∧ TIME_012 R ∧ TIME_013 R ∧ TIME_015 R</pre>
- ✓ **consistent** `Libpetri.Spec.spec_consistent_queue` (Libpetri.Spec.ConsistencyQueue): ✓ The CORE and IO requirement blocks, TIME-001, EXEC-002, the TIME blocks and the queue blocks EXEC-010, EXEC-012, EXEC-013 and EXEC-020 hold together on one model: the model of `spec_consistent_coreIO_time`, with one builder for the IO build lines and EXEC-012's, and a queue firing relation whose firings, counted, are the firings of the same firing relation `F`, and which lifts every firing of `F`.
